<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:H0}
		The space $(\mathcal H_{K}$, $\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal H_{K}})$ is a separable Hilbertian seminormed space in the sense of \cite[Section 1.1]{Zbl054760064}. 
		Furthermore, $[0]_K$ is a linear subspace of $\mathcal H_{K}$, and there exists a unique norm $\|\cdot\|_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}}$ on the quotient space $\mathcal H_{K}/[0]_K:= \{[f]_K: f\in \mathcal H_{K}\}$ such that $\|[f]_K\|_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}} = \|f\|_{\mathcal H_{K}}$ for each $f\in \mathcal H_{K}.$
		The completion of the normed space $(\mathcal H_{K}/[0]_K, \|\cdot\|_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}})$ is a separable Hilbert space, denoted by $(\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}, \|\cdot\|_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}})$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:HS}
		For each  $g\in \mathcal H_{K}$,
		$\Gamma_g$ is a Hilbert-Schmidt operator from 
		$Q^{1/2}\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}$
		to $\mathbb R$ satisfying that, for any orthonormal basis  $(\eta_i)_{i\in I}$ of 
		$Q^{1/2}\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}$,
		
		\[
			\|\Gamma_g\|^2_{\mathcal L^{\mathrm{HS}}_{Q^{1/2}\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}}}
			:=\sum_{i\in I} |\Gamma_g \eta_i|^2 
			= \Norm*{Q^{1/2}[g]_K}^2_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}} 
			\leq \|Q^{1/2}\|^2 \|g\|^2_{\mathcal H_{K}}
		\]
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main2}
		Let $({\mathbb X}, d,m)$ be a metric measure space such that \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} is satisfied. 
		Let the stochastic basis $(\Omega, \mathscr{F}, (\mathscr{F}_t)_{t\geq 0}, \mathbb P, W)$, drift coefficient $b$, noise coefficient
		$\sigma$, and the initial value $f$ be given as in \eqref{eq:SB}--\eqref{eq:IV}.
        Suppose
		$\mathcal L$ admits a heat kernel $\{G_t(x, y)\}_{t>0,x,y\in{\mathbb X}}$ (with respect to the Radon measure $m$) that satisfies \eqref{cond:ctem continuous},
		\eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} and
		\eqref{cond:time_holder}.
		Suppose
		the existence of
		a $\mathbb P$-null subset $\mathrm N_{\refstepcounter{c}{\thec}\label{c:Lip}}$ of $\Omega$ s.t.
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:LiG}]
			(linear growth condition)
			for any $T>0$, uniformly for
			$\omega \in \Omega \setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$,
			$(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $u \in \mathbb{R}$,
			\begin{equation}
				|b(\omega, t,x,u) |+|\sigma(\omega, t,x,u)| \lesssim 1+|u|;
				\quad \text{and}
			\end{equation}
		\end{itemize}
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{ineq:g_lip}]
			(Lipschitz condition)
			for any $T>0$, uniformly for
			$\omega \in \Omega \setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$,
			$(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $u,v \in \mathbb{R}$,
			\begin{equation}
				|b(\omega, t,x,u) - b(\omega, t,x,v)|+|\sigma(\omega, t,x,u)- \sigma(\omega, t,x,v)| \lesssim |u - v|.
			\end{equation}
		\end{itemize}
		Then, there exists a 
		mild-solution $(u_t)_{t\geq 0}$ to the SPDE \eqref{eq:spde}, and the solution is unique up to indistinguishability.
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:comparison}
		Suppose the same assumptions as in Theorem \ref{thm:main2} hold. Let $(u_t)_{t\ge 0}$ and $(u'_t)_{t \ge 0}$ be the mild-solutions with initial conditions $u_0=f\in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ and  
        $u_0' = f' \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ respectively.
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item \label{part:main2 weak comparison} (Comparison principle)
			Suppose  \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound} and \eqref{eq:con}  hold. If
			$f(x) \ge f'(x)$ for  $x \in {\mathbb X}$, then ${\mathbb P}\bigl(u_t(x)\ge u'_t(x)\ \text{for all }t\ge 0 \text{ and } x\in{\mathbb X}\bigr)=1$.
			\item \label{part:main2 strong comparison}
			(Strong comparison principle) Suppose   \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound}, \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation} and \eqref{eq:con}    hold. If $f(x) \ge f'(x)$ for  $x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $f(x_0) >f'(x_0)$ for some $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$,
			then ${\mathbb P}\bigl(u_t(x)> u'_t(x)\ \text{for all }t> 0 \text{ and } x\in{\mathbb X}\bigr)=1$.
			\item  \label{part:main2 strictly positivity}
			(Strict positivity)
			Suppose   \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound}, \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation} and \eqref{eq:con}    hold, and that almost surely,
			\begin{align}\label{cond:pos_zero}
				b(t,x,0) \ge 0\quad \text{ and }\quad \sigma(t,x,0) = 0 \quad\text{for all }(t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}.
			\end{align}
            If $f$ is non-negative and $f(x)>0$ for some $x\in {\mathbb X}$, then 
                        ${\mathbb P}\bigl(u_t(x)> 0\ \text{for all }t> 0 \text{ and } x\in{\mathbb X}\bigr)=1$.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:sub_g_mmd_framework}
		Let $V,\Psi:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be doubling functions satisfying \eqref{eq:2doublingfunctions}.
		Let $({\mathbb X},d,m,\mathcal E,\mathcal F)$ be an MMD that admits heat kernels, satisfies $\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)$ and has volume growth profile $V$.
		Let $\mathcal L$ be the operator associated with $(\mathcal E,\mathcal F)$.
		Fix $x_0\in{\mathbb X}$ and set $h(x):=\exp(d(x_0,x))$ for $x\in{\mathbb X}$.
		Let $\gamma\geq 0$ and let $K_\gamma$ be as in \eqref{eq:K0} and \eqref{eq:Kgamma}.
		Suppose there exists $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$ such that
		\begin{itemize}
			\item if $\gamma = 0$, then 
			\[
			\Parentheses*{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(t)}}^{-1}\lesssim t^{-\alpha_0},\qquad\text{uniformly in }t\in(0,1];
			\]
			\item else if $\gamma \neq 0$, then 
			\[
			\int_0^\infty \frac{s^\gamma}{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(t+s)}}\,\frac{\mathrm ds}{s}\lesssim t^{-\alpha_0},\qquad\text{uniformly in }t\in(0,1].
			\]
		\end{itemize}
		Let stochastic basis $(\Omega, \mathscr{F}, (\mathscr{F}_t)_{t\geq 0}, \mathbb P, W)$, drift coefficient $b$, noise coefficient
		$\sigma$, and initial value $f$ be given as in \eqref{eq:SB}--\eqref{eq:IV} with $K$ being replaced by $K_\gamma$.
		Suppose there exists a $\mathbb P$-null subset $\mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$ of $\Omega$ such that \eqref{eq:LiG} and \eqref{ineq:g_lip} hold.
		Then, there exists a unique (up to indistinguishability) strong solution $(u_t)_{t\geq 0}$ to the SPDE \eqref{eq:spde}.
				Furthermore, if in addition \eqref{eq:con} and \eqref{cond:pos_zero} hold, then the unique strong solution satisfies the strict positivity stated in Theorem~\ref{thm:comparison}.
		In particular, for the Parabolic Anderson model
		\[
		\partial_t u = \mathcal L u + u\dot W_t,\qquad u(0,\cdot)=u_0,
		\]
		with either white noise $K_0$ or colored noise $K_\gamma$ under the respective conditions above,
		the unique strong solution satisfies
		\[
		\mathbb P\Parentheses*{u(t,x)>0\text{ for all }t>0,\;x\in{\mathbb X}}=1
		\]
		whenever $u_0\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem},+}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ is not identically zero.
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:sub-g-general}
		Let \(V,\Psi : [0,\infty) \to [0,\infty)\) be doubling functions such that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:sub_g_escape_vol_constrain}
			\frac{R^2}{r^2} \lesssim \frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)} \lesssim \frac{R\,V(R)}{r\,V(r)},\qquad\text{uniformly in } 0<r\le R.
		\end{align}
		Then there exists an unbounded (that is, infinite diameter) MMD space
		\[
		({\mathbb X},d,m,\mathcal E,\mathcal F)
		\]
		that satisfies the full sub-Gaussian kernel estimate \(\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)\), and the metric measure space $({\mathbb X},d,m)$ has volume growth profile $V$.
		By tracing the proofs in \cite{murugan2024diffusions}, one sees that the metric space $({\mathbb X},d)$ here is a complete locally compact metric space (see \cite[Proofs of Theorem 2.7  and Proposition 3.18]{murugan2024diffusions}).
		Note that the doubling property of $\Psi$ alone is weaker than the scale function definition (with two-sided exponents $\beta_1,\beta_2$) given earlier in this subsection; the two-sided polynomial bounds for $\Psi$ follow from \eqref{ineq:sub_g_escape_vol_constrain} together with the doubling property and are not assumed a priori.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{Eg:PolySubGaussianHK}
		In many standard examples we have $\Psi(r)\asymp r^{d_w}$ for some $d_w \ge 2$ and $V(r) \asymp r^{d_h}$ for some $d_h \ge 1$; in that case this setting corresponds to the usual sub-Gaussian heat kernel estimate. Examples include
		\begin{itemize}
			\item  $\mathbb{R}^n$ with the usual Gaussian heat kernel, in which case $d_h = n$ and $d_w = 2$. 
			\item  metric graphs with volume doubling property, with $d_h = 1$ and $d_w = 2$;  see \cite{MR4278798}. 
			\item (Unbounded) Sierpi\'{n}ski gasket in $\mathbb{R}^n$ with $d_h = \log_2(n+1)$ and $d_w = \log_2(n+3)$; see \cites{barlow1988brownian,barlow1998diffusions}.
		\end{itemize}
		
		Under these choices of $\Psi$ and $V$, the standing assumptions in Section~\ref{sec:backgroup_setups} are satisfied when the parameters meet appropriate conditions (see Appendix~\ref{sec:appendix}). In particular, for white noise ($\gamma=0$) one needs $d_h<d_w$; for colored noise $K_\gamma$, the condition reads
		\[
		\frac{d_h}{d_w} > \gamma > \max\Braces*{0,\frac{d_h}{d_w}-1}{,}
		\]
		which is consistent with the conditions for the resolvability of Parabolic Anderson models in \cite[Theorem 1.1]{Zbl08159034}  and the discussion thereafter.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:fractionalPAM}
		Let $({\mathbb X},d,m,\mathcal E,\mathcal F)$, $\mathcal L$ and $1 \le d_h \le d_w$ be as above and let $\beta \in (d_h/d_w, 1)$.
		Fix an $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$ and let $h(x):= 1+d(x_0,x)$ and let $\dot{W}$ be the space-time white noise on $\mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$.
		For any $u_0 \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem},+}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ that is not identically zero, there exists a unique solution to
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:fractionalPAM}
			\partial_t u = -(-\mathcal L)^\beta u + u \dot{W},\qquad u(0,\cdot) = u_0.
		\end{equation}
		Furthermore, $\mathbb P \left(u(t,x)>0 \,\text{ for }\, t>0,\, x \in {\mathbb X} \right) =1$.
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:local_manifolds}
Suppose $({\mathbb X},d,m)=(M,d_g,m)$ and $\mathcal L=\tfrac12\Delta_M$ are the Riemannian manifold and operator  described above  such that \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} holds.
Suppose the function $h$, the constant $\gamma$ and the measure $K$ are as in the paragraph containing \eqref{eq:manifold_gamma}-\eqref{eq:manifold_covariance}.
Then, $(M,d,m)$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:entropy bound}, $h$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and \eqref{ineq:ref_regular}, the heat semigroup $(P_t)_{t\geq0}$ is Feller, $K$ is a symmetric, non-negative definite Radon measure, and \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}, \eqref{cond:time_holder}{,}
\eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}{, \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound} and \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation}} are all satisfied.
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{ex:local_manifolds}
 By \cite[Theorem~2.7]{hebisch2001relation}, a sufficient criterion for \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} consists of \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume} and a constant $C_P>0$ such that
 \[
  \int_{B_r(x)}|v-v_{B_r(x)}|^2\,dm
  \leq C_P r^2\int_{B_r(x)}|\nabla v|^2\,dm,
  \qquad v_B:=\frac1{m(B)}\int_B v\,dm,
 \]
 for every $x\in M$, $0<r\leq1$ and $v\in C_c^\infty(M)$. This includes the following manifolds.
 \begin{enumerate}
  \item Every compact connected smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary satisfies \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian}; see \cite[Theorem~6.1, p.~428]{saloff1992uniformly}. Here $h$ is bounded and $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{M,h}=C(M)$.
  \item Real hyperbolic space $\mathbb H^n$, $n\geq2$, satisfies \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} by \cite[Theorem~6.1, p.~428]{saloff1992uniformly}.
         \item Let $M=\mathbb R^n$, let $g_{\mathrm E}$ be the Euclidean metric, and let $g=(g_{ij})$ be a smooth Riemannian metric on $M$ such that, for some $a\geq1$,
  \begin{align}
   a^{-1}g_{\mathrm E}\leq g\leq a g_{\mathrm E}.
   \label{eq:manifold_metric_comparison}
  \end{align}
  Set
  $d=d_g,\,m(\mathrm dx)=\sqrt{\det g(x)}\mathrm dx,
   \, \mathcal L=\tfrac12\operatorname{div}_g\nabla_g$. 
    By \eqref{eq:manifold_metric_comparison}, the global parabolic Harnack inequality for $g_{\mathrm E}$ transfers to $g$ by \cite[Theorem~5.5.9, pp.~168--169]{saloff2002aspects}. Its equivalence with two-sided Gaussian heat-kernel estimates follows from \cite[Theorems~5.5.1 and~5.5.3, pp.~157--161]{saloff2002aspects}. Then \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} follows from \eqref{eq:manifold_metric_comparison} and the fact that $m(B_r(x))\asymp r^n$.

   \end{enumerate}
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:stochastic_fubini}
		Suppose that $(\Omega, \mathscr{F}, (\mathscr{F}_t)_{t\geq 0}, \mathbb P, W)$ is a stochastic basis given as in \eqref{eq:SB}. 
		Let  $(E, \mathscr E, \nu)$
		be a $\sigma$-finite measure space and let $T\geq 0$. 
		Let $\mathcal P_T$ denote the restriction of $\mathcal P$ to $\Omega\times[0,T]$.
		Let $\phi:=(\phi(t,\cdot, \theta): t \ge 0, \theta \in E )$ be a predictable random field in $\mathcal H_{K}$
		such that the map
		\[
			(\omega,t,\theta)\longmapsto [\phi(\omega,t,\cdot,\theta)]_K
		\]
		is $\mathcal P_T\otimes\mathscr E$-measurable with values in $\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K$, and
		\begin{align} 
			\Norm*{\phi}_{L^1_{E} L^2_{\Omega\times[0,T]}\mathcal H_{K}}:=
			\int_{E} {\mathbb E}\left[\int_0^T \Norm*{\phi(t,\cdot, \theta)}^2_{\mathcal H_{K}} \mathrm dt\right]^{1/2} \nu(\mathrm d\theta) <\infty.
		\end{align} 
		Then, there exists a real-valued measurable function  $(\Phi_T(\omega,\theta))_{\omega\in \Omega, \theta \in E}$ on 
		$(\Omega \times E, \mathscr{F}_T \otimes \mathscr E)$ such that
		\[
		\Phi_T(\cdot, \theta)=\iint_0^T \phi(t,x,\theta)W(\mathrm dt,\mathrm dx), \quad \text{a.s.,}\quad \text{for $\nu$-almost every $\theta\in E$},
		\]
		and that
		\[
		\int_{E} \Phi_T(\cdot, \theta) \nu(\mathrm d\theta) = \iint_0^T \left( \int_{E} [\phi(t,x,\theta)]_K\,\nu(\mathrm d\theta)\right) W(\mathrm dt,\mathrm dx),\quad \text{a.s.}
		\]
		Here the inner integral on the right-hand side is a Bochner integral in $\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:mtg+mink_cs}
		Suppose that $(\Omega, \mathscr{F}, (\mathscr{F}_t)_{t\geq 0}, \mathbb P, W)$ is a stochastic basis given as in \eqref{eq:SB}. 
		Then for every $p\geq 2$, uniformly for every $t\geq 0$ and real-valued predictable random field $(\Phi(s,y))_{s\geq 0, y\in {\mathbb X}}$ 
		such that 
		\[
		\|\Phi\|_{L_{[0,t]}^2 \mathcal H_{K} L^p_\Omega} :=\left(\int_0^t \left\|\left\|\Phi(s,\cdot)\right\|_{L^p_{\Omega}}\right\|_{\mathcal H_{K}}^2 \mathrm ds \right)^\frac{1}{2} < \infty, 
		\]
		we have
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:mtg+mink+cs}
			\left\|\iint_0^t \Phi(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy)\right\|_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\lesssim \|\Phi\|_{L_{[0,t]}^2 \mathcal H_{K} L^p_\Omega}.
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:hk longtime}
		Suppose that $\lambda \in \mathbb{R}$ and 
		$\tilde \alpha \in (0,1)$ satisfy \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} with $\alpha$ being replaced by $\tilde \alpha$,
		then there exist $\kappa, \tilde \kappa >0$ so that 
		uniformly for every $t>0$ and $x\in {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:hk_m long time}
			\| G_t(\cdot,x)h(\cdot)^\lambda\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \lesssim e^{\kappa t}h(x)^\lambda,
		\end{align}
		and
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:hk_k long time}
			\| G_t(\cdot,x)h(\cdot)^\lambda\|_{\mathcal H_{K}}^2 
			\lesssim t^{-\tilde \alpha}
			e^{\tilde \kappa t}h(x)^{2\lambda}.
		\end{align}
		Let $\beta\in [0,1)$ and $\xi_1,\xi_2>0$ be given as in \eqref{cond:time_holder}. For each $T>0$, it holds uniformly in $t,t' \in (0,T]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:time_holder_long_time}
			\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \lesssim  \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1 \wedge 1}}{\Parentheses*{t\wedge t'}^\beta}.
		\end{align}
		Moreover, it holds uniformly in $t\in(0,T]$ and $x,x'\in{\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\le1$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:spatial_holder_long_time}
			\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x)-G_t(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\lesssim \frac{d(x,x')^{\xi_2}}{t^\beta}.
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:hk_abs}
		Let $\xi_1,\, \xi_2 \in (0,1]$ be as in \eqref{cond:time_holder}, then
		for any $T>0$ and $\lambda \in [0,1/2]$, it holds uniformly for 
		$t,t'$ in $[0,T]$
		and  $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$,
		that 
		\begin{align}
			h(x)^{-\lambda}\| h(\cdot)^{\lambda}\Parentheses*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot, \cdot; t', x')} \|_{L^1_{[0,T)} L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\lesssim  |t-t'|^{{{\xi_1 \wedge 1}\over 2}} + d(x,x')^{{\xi_2 \over 2}\wedge 1}. \label{ineq:hk abs holder m}
		\end{align}
		Let $\alpha\in (0,1)$ and $\beta \in [0,1)$ be as in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} and \eqref{cond:time_holder} respectively.  
		There exist $\widetilde \xi_1,\widetilde \xi_2 \in (0,1)$, so that for every $\lambda_1\in[0,1/2]$ and $T>0$,
		it holds uniformly for $t,t'$ in $[0,T]$ and  $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that
		\begin{align}
			h(x)^{-2\lambda_1}\| h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}\Absolute*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot, \cdot; t', x') }\|^2_{L^2_{[0,T)}\mathcal H_{K}}
			\lesssim |t-t'|^{2\widetilde \xi_1} + d(x,x')^{2\widetilde \xi_2}. \label{ineq:hk abs holder k}
		\end{align}
		Moreover, there exist $\xi\in(0,1]$ and $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$ such that, for every $\lambda\in[0,1/2]$ and $T>0$, uniformly for $x,x'\in{\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\le1$ and $0\le t\le t'\le T$,
		\begin{align}
			\int_0^{t'}\left\|\left|G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)-G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')\right|h(\cdot)^\lambda\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2\,\mathrm ds
			\lesssim t'^{1-\alpha_0}\left(|t-t'|^\xi+d(x,x')^\xi\right)h(x)^{2\lambda}.
			\label{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted}
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:tight_tem_moment}
		Let $X$ be a real-valued random field on  $\mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}$.
		Suppose
		that for each  $\lambda\in (0,1]$, there exists $p_0 = p_0(\lambda) \ge 2$ so that for all $p \ge p_0$, it holds uniformly in $x\in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb E}\left[|X(0,x)|^p\right] \lesssim h(x)^{\lambda}. 
		\end{align}
		Suppose further that there exist
		$\tilde \xi_1,\tilde \xi_2 \in (0,1]$
		so that for each $T>0$, $\lambda \in (0,1]$ and $p \ge 2$,  uniformly for 
		$t,t'$ in $[0,T]$ with $|t-t'| \le 1$,
		$x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $y_1,y_2 \in B_1(x)$,
		\begin{align}\label{cond:tight_moment}
			{\mathbb E}\left[\left| X(t,y_1) - X(t',y_2) \right| ^p\right] \lesssim  \left( |t'-t|^{\tilde \xi_1} + d(y_1,y_2)^{\tilde \xi_2}\right)^p h(x)^{\lambda}.
		\end{align}
		Then,
		the random field $X$ admits a continuous modification $\tilde X$ taking values in $\mathcal C_{\mathbb R_+}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:PsiM}
		Suppose that both $(B, \Sigma)$ and $(B', \Sigma')$ are Duhamel-integrable pairs. Then 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item $\Psi_1(B')$ is a modification of $\Psi_1(B)$ provided $B'$ is a predictable modification of $B$; 
			\item $\Psi_2(\Sigma')$ is a modification of $\Psi_2(\Sigma)$ provided $\Sigma'$ is a predictable modification of $\Sigma$.
		\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:CMS} 
		For every
		$\lambda \in \mathbb R$, $p \ge 1$ and $A\geq 0$, $(\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp}, \|\cdot\|_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}^{\sharp}})$ is a Banach space. 
		In particular, $(\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}, d_{\lambda,p,A})$ is a complete metric space.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:b_contract_combined}
		Let $\lambda \ge0$ and $\tilde \alpha \in (0,1)$ satisfy \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} with $\alpha$ being replaced by $\tilde \alpha$.
		Let $p \ge 2$ and $g \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$. When $\lambda=0$, assume in addition that $g\in\mathcal C_{\mathbb X}^{\mathrm b}$. For each $U \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$ and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$, define
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:BuSu}
			B_U(t,x):= b(t,x,U(t,x)),\qquad \Sigma_U(t,x):= \sigma(t,x,U(t,x)).
		\end{equation}
		Then  for each $U\in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$, $(B_U,\Sigma_U)$ is a Duhamel-integrable pair, and  $\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U) \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$.
		Moreover,
		uniformly in $ U,\, V \in \mathbb M_{\lambda, p }$, $A\ge 1$ and $g \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$, we have 
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U) - \Psi(g,B_V, \Sigma_V)}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p, A}} 
			\lesssim  A^\frac{\tilde \alpha - 1}{2} 
			\Norm*{U - V}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p, A}}.
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:wellposted}
		Suppose that  $\lambda \in (0,1]$ (or $\lambda=0$ if $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$) and $p \ge 2$. Then there exists a $U \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$, unique up to predictable modification,
		such that $\Phi(U)$ is a  predictable modification of $U$.
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
From \eqref{eq:pp}, we know that $\Phi(U) \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$.
		Note that for any $U \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{ f}$,
		\begin{equation}
			\Phi(U)(0,x)
			= \Psi(f, B_U, \Sigma_U)(0,x) 
			= f(x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
		\end{equation}
		Therefore, 
		$\Phi: U \mapsto \Phi(U)$ is a map from $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{f}$ to $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{f}$.
		From Lemma \ref{lem:PsiM} and \eqref{eq:Modification} we can verify that, for any $U \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{f}$
		and any 
		predictable
		modification $U'$ of $U$, $\Phi(U')$ is a 
		predictable
		modification of 
		$\Phi(U)$.
		Therefore, we can define a map $\Phi^\sharp$ from $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}$ to $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}$ such that 
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:Phisharp}
			\Phi^\sharp([U]_{\mathrm{p}}) = [\Phi(U)]_{\mathrm{p}}, \quad \forall U\in \mathbb M^f_{\lambda, p}.
		\end{equation}
		Recall that $\alpha\in (0,1)$ is given by \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}.
		Note that uniformly for $A\ge 1$ and $U,V \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$,
		\begin{align}\label{eq:PhiD}
			&\|\Phi(U) - \Phi(V)\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}} 
			=\Norm*{\Psi(f,B_U,\Sigma_U) - \Psi(f, B_V, \Sigma_V)}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p, A}} 
			\\&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:b_contract_combined}}}\lesssim A^{\frac{\alpha - 1}{2}} \|U - V\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}}. 
		\end{align}
		From the fact that $\alpha \in (0,1)$, there exists a large $A_0>0$ such that for any $A\geq A_0$ and $U,V \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{f}$,
		\begin{equation} \label{eq:PC}
			\|\Phi(U) - \Phi(V)\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}} \leq \frac{1}{2}\|U-V\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}}.
		\end{equation}
		
		Now, for any $A\geq A_0$ and $\mathbf U, \mathbf V$ in $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}$, taking representatives $U\in \mathbf U\cap\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$ and $V\in \mathbf V\cap\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$ respectively, it holds that 
		\begin{align}
			&d_{\lambda,p, A}\Parentheses*{\Phi^\sharp(\mathbf U), \Phi^\sharp(\mathbf V)} 
			= \Norm*{\Phi^\sharp([U]_{\mathrm{p}}) - \Phi^\sharp([V]_{\mathrm{p}})}_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}^{\sharp}} 
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:Phisharp}}{=} \Norm*{ [\Phi(U)]_{\mathrm{p}} - [\Phi(V)]_{\mathrm{p}} }_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}^{\sharp}} 
			=\Norm*{ [\Phi(U) - \Phi(V)]_{\mathrm{p}} }_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}^{\sharp}} 
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:NormSharp}}{=} \left\| \Phi(U) - \Phi(V)\right\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}}  
			\overset{\eqref{eq:PC}}\leq \frac{1}{2}\left\|U-V\right\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}}
			\overset{\eqref{eq:NormSharp}}{=} \frac{1}{2}\left\|[U-V]_{\mathrm{p}}\right\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}^\sharp}
			\\&= \frac{1}{2}\left\|[U]_{\mathrm{p}}-[V]_{\mathrm{p}}\right\|_{\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p,A}^\sharp}
			=  \frac{1}{2}d_{\lambda,p, A}\Parentheses*{\mathbf U, \mathbf V} .
		\end{align}
		
		Now noticing Lemma \ref{lem:CMS}, $\Phi^\sharp$ is a contraction map from the complete metric space $(\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}, d_{\lambda,p,A})$ to $( \mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}, d_{\lambda,p,A})$ for every $A\geq A_0$. 
		By the Banach fixed-point theorem, 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:UMf}] there exists a unique element $\mathbf U$ in $\mathbb{M}_{\lambda,p}^{\sharp,f}$ such that $\Phi^\sharp(\mathbf U) = \mathbf U$.
		\end{itemize}
		Let $U\in \mathbf U\cap \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$ be a representative of the $\mathbf U$ in \eqref{eq:UMf}, then by \eqref{eq:Phisharp}, $[\Phi(U)]_{\mathrm{p}} = [U]_{\mathrm{p}}$. This proves the existence part of the desired result.
		Suppose that there is any other $U'\in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^f$ such that $[\Phi(U')]_{\mathrm{p}} = [U']_{\mathrm{p}}$, then by \eqref{eq:Phisharp}, $\Phi^\sharp([U']_{\mathrm{p}}) = [U']_{\mathrm{p}}$, and by \eqref{eq:UMf}, $[U']_{\mathrm{p}} = [U]_{\mathrm{p}}$.
		This proves the uniqueness part of the desired result. We are done.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:MfT}
		There exists  $U \in \mathbb M^{f}$, unique up to  predictable modification, such that $\Phi(U)$ is a predictable modification of  $U$. If, in addition, $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$, then $U\in\mathbb M^f_{0,p}$ for every $p\geq 2$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $(\lambda_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ be a sequence in $(0,1]$ such that $\lambda_n \downarrow 0$, and let $(p_n)_{n\in \mathbb N}$ be a sequence in $[2,\infty)$ such that $p_n \uparrow \infty$. 
		From \eqref{eq:MM}, we know that $\mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_{n+1}, p_{n+1}}\subset \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_{n}, p_{n}}$ for each $n\in \mathbb N$.
		From Proposition \ref{prop:wellposted} for each $n\in \mathbb N$, there exists a 
		$U^{(n)}\in \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_n, p_n}$, unique up to  predictable modification, such that $\Phi(U^{(n)})$ is a  predictable modification of $U^{(n)}$. 
		For each $n\in \mathbb N$, since $U^{(n+1)}\in \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_{n+1}, p_{n+1}} \subset \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_{n}, p_{n}}$ and $\Phi(U^{(n+1)})$ is a  predictable modification of $U^{(n+1)}$, it follows that $U^{(n+1)}$ is a  predictable modification of $U^{(n)}$. 
		From this and induction, $U^{(n)}$ is a  predictable modification of $U:= U^{(1)}$ for each $n\in \mathbb N$. 
		
		
		For any $\lambda \in (0,1]$ and $p\geq 2$, by taking $n\in \mathbb N$ large enough such that $\lambda_n \leq \lambda$ and $p_n \geq p$, we have $U \in \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda_{n}, p_{n}} \subset \mathbb M^{f}_{\lambda, p}$. 
		Therefore, $U \in \mathbb M^{f}$ and $[U]_{\mathrm p} = [\Phi(U)]_{\mathrm p}$.
		This proves the existence part of the desired result.
		Suppose that there is any other $U' \in \mathbb M^f$ satisfying that $[U']_{\mathrm p} = [\Phi(U')]_{\mathrm p}$, then from the fact that $\mathbb M^f \subset \mathbb M^f_{\lambda_1, p_1}$, it must hold that $[U']_{\mathrm p} = [U^{(1)}]_{\mathrm p} = [U]_{\mathrm p}$.
		This proves the uniqueness part of the desired result.

		Finally, suppose that $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$ and let $p\geq 2$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:wellposted} with $\lambda=0$, there exists $U_0\in\mathbb M^f_{0,p}$ such that $\Phi(U_0)$ is a predictable modification of $U_0$. Since $h\geq1$, both $U_0$ and $U$ belong to $\mathbb M^f_{1,p}$, so the uniqueness part of Proposition~\ref{prop:wellposted} with $\lambda=1$ (and the same $p$) shows that $U$ is a predictable modification of $U_0$. Hence, by Fubini's theorem, for every $x\in{\mathbb X}$, $\Norm*{U(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}=\Norm*{U_0(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}$ for $\Lambda$-a.e.\ $t\in[0,T]$, and thus $\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{0,p}}=\Norm*{U_0}_{\mathbb M_{0,p}}<\infty$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:existence of C_tem modification}
		Suppose that $U \in \mathbb M^{f}$ is  the random field given as in Corollary  \ref{cor:MfT},
		then $U$ admits a  predictable modification $\tilde U$ taking values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.  In addition, $\Phi(U)$ admits a modification in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ and  there exists $\xi \in (0,1)$ so that for any $T>0$, $p \ge 2$ and $\lambda \in (0,1]$, it holds uniformly in $t,t'\in [0,T]$, $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$, 
		\begin{align}
			&\Norm*{\Psi_1(B_U)(t,x) - \Psi_1(B_U)(t',x')}_{L^p_\Omega}+\Norm*{\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t,x) - \Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t',x')}_{L^p_\Omega} \\
			&\lesssim \Parentheses*{|t-t'|^\xi +d(x,x')^\xi }h(x)^\lambda.
		\end{align}
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
\setcounter{step}{0}\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
		Notice that $U$ is the predictable modification of 
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:PhiU}
			\Phi(U) := \Psi_0(f) + \Psi_1(B_U) + \Psi_2(\Sigma_U).
		\end{equation}
		To prove the desired result for this proposition, we only need to show that each term on the right hand side of \eqref{eq:PhiU} admits a modification taking values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
		In fact, if this has been shown, then $\Phi(U)$ admits a modification $\tilde \Phi(U)$ taking values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$. 
		And since the continuous random field $\tilde \Phi(U)$ is predictable, by \eqref{eq:Modification}, it is also a predictable modification of both $\Phi(U)$ and $U$. 
		
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
		From \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}, we know that $\Psi_0(f)$ takes values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ already.
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
		We will show that $\Psi_1(B_U)$ admits a 
		modification taking values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]} \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
		Let $\xi_1, \xi_2$
		be the constants given as in 
		\eqref{cond:time_holder}.
		Let $\lambda_0 \in [0,1]$ (where $\lambda_0=0$ is allowed only if $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$, in which case $U\in\mathbb M_{0,p}$ by Corollary~\ref{cor:MfT}) and $p \geq 2$ be arbitrary. 
		Define $\lambda := \lambda_0 / p \in [0,1/2]$. 
		Note that uniformly  for $(s,y)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $\omega \in \Omega\setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$, 
		\begin{align}
			\Absolute*{B_U(s,y)} + \Absolute*{\Sigma_U(s,y)}&= \Absolute*{b(s,y, U(s,y))} 
			+ \Absolute*{\sigma(s,y,U(s,y))}
			\\&{\overset{\eqref{eq:LiG}}\lesssim} 1 + |U(s,y)|. \label{eq:BUles}
		\end{align}
		Therefore, we have the existence of a $\Lambda\otimes m$-null subset $\mathrm N_{\refstepcounter{c}{\thec}\label{c:BUh}}$ of $[0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ such that uniformly for every $(s,y)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}\setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:BUh}}$, 
		\begin{align}
			&\Norm*{B_U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\overset{\eqref{eq:BUles}}{\lesssim} \Norm*{1 + |U(s,y)|}_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\overset{\text{Minkowski}}\leq 1 + \Norm*{U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega}
			\\& \leq h(y)^{\lambda} + \Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p}} h(y)^{\lambda}
			\lesssim h(y)^{\lambda}. \label{eq:BUp}
		\end{align}
		
		Uniformly for every $t,t'\in [0,T]$ and $x,x'\in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$,
		\begin{align}
			& \mathbb E\left[\left|\Psi_1(B_U)(t,x) - \Psi_1 (B_U)(t',x')\right|^p\right]^{\frac{1}{p}} = \left\|\Psi_1(B_U)(t,x) - \Psi_1 (B_U)(t',x')\right\|_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\\&= \left\| \int_0^T \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} \left(G(s,y;t,x) - G(s,y;t',x')\right) B_U(s,y) m(\mathrm dy)\right\|_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\\&\overset{\text{Minkowski}}\le  \left\| \left| G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')\right| \left\|B_U(\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{L^p_\Omega} \right\|_{L^1_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}}
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:BUp}} \lesssim \Norm*{\Absolute*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')}h(\cdot )^\lambda }_{L^1_{[0,T]\times {\mathbb X}}} 
			\\&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk_abs} }}\lesssim  \Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\Parentheses*{\xi_1 \wedge 1}\over 2} +  d(x,x')^{\frac{\xi_2}{2}\wedge 1} }h(x)^\lambda 
			\\&= \Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\Parentheses*{\xi_1 \wedge 1}\over 2} +  d(x,x')^{\frac{\xi_2}{2}\wedge 1} }h(x)^{\lambda_0/p}     
		\end{align}
		Since $\Psi_1(B_U)(0,\cdot) \equiv 0$ is deterministic, and since $p\geq 2$ and $\lambda_0 \in (0,1]$ are chosen arbitrarily, from Lemma \ref{lem:tight_tem_moment}, $\Psi_1(B_U)$ admits a modification taking values in  $\mathcal C_{[0,T]} \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ as desired for this step.
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. } We will show that $\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)$ admits a predictable modification taking values in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]} \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
										Let $\lambda_0 \in [0,1]$ (where $\lambda_0=0$ is allowed only if $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$, in which case $U\in\mathbb M_{0,p}$ by Corollary~\ref{cor:MfT}) and $p\geq 2$ be arbitrary.
		Take $\lambda_1:=\lambda_0/p\in[0,1/2]$ with $p\lambda_1 \leq \lambda_0$.
		Pay attention that \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} holds with $\lambda$ being replaced by $\lambda_1$.
		
		Uniformly for every $(t,x)$ and $(t',x')$ in $[0, T]\times {\mathbb X}$, we have
		\begin{align}
			&\mathbb E\left[\left|\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t,x) - \Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t',x')\right|^p \right]^{1/p}
			\\&= \Norm*{\iint_0^T \Parentheses*{G(s,y;t,x)-G(s,y;t',x')} \Sigma_U(s,y)W(\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy)}_{L^p_\Omega}
			\\&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs}}} \lesssim \left\|\left(G(\cdot, \cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')\right)  \Sigma_U \right\|_{L^2_{[0,T]} \mathcal H_K L_\Omega^p}
			\\&=\Parentheses*{\int_0^T \Norm*{
					\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} 
					\Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}
				}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds}^{1/2}. \label{ineq:continuity stochastic part }
		\end{align}
		Note that we have the existence of a $\Lambda \otimes K$-null subset $\mathrm N_{\refstepcounter{c}{\thec}\label{c:SigmaNull}}$ of $[0,T]\times {\mathbb X}^2$ such that uniformly for every $(s,x,y)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}^2 \setminus {\mathrm N_{\ref{c:SigmaNull}}}$, 
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,x)}_{L^p_\Omega} \cdot \Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega} 
			&{\overset{\eqref{eq:BUles}}{\lesssim}} \Norm*{1 + |U(s,x)|}_{L^p_\Omega} \cdot \Norm*{1 + |U(s,y)|}_{L^p_\Omega}
			\\& {\overset{\text{Minkowski}}\leq} \Parentheses*{ 1 + \Norm*{U(s,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}} \cdot \Parentheses*{ 1 + \Norm*{U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega}}
			\\& \leq \Parentheses*{h(x)^{\lambda_1} + \Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda_1, p}} h(x)^{\lambda_1}}\cdot \Parentheses*{h(y)^{\lambda_1} + \Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda_1, p}} h(y)^{\lambda_1}}\\
			&\lesssim h(x)^{\lambda_1} h(y)^{\lambda_1}. \label{eq:SUp}
		\end{align}
		Therefore, uniformly for every $(t,x)$ and $(t',x')$ in $[0, T]\times {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$, we have
		\begin{align}
			&\mathbb E\left[\left|\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t,x) - \Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(t',x')\right|^p \right]^{1/p}
			\\&\overset{\eqref{ineq:continuity stochastic part }}\lesssim \Parentheses*{\int_0^T \Norm*{
					\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} 
					\Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}
				}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds}^{1/2}
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:SUp}}\lesssim \Parentheses*{\int_0^T \Norm*{
					\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} 
					h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}
				}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds}^{1/2}
			\\& \overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk_abs} }}\lesssim  h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\widetilde \xi_1} + d(x,x')^{\widetilde \xi_2}} 
			\lesssim 
			h(x)^{\lambda_0/p}
			\Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\widetilde \xi_1} + d(x,x')^{\widetilde \xi_2}}
		\end{align}
		for some $\widetilde\xi_1,\, \widetilde\xi_2 \in(0,1]$, where we used the fact that 
		$h(x) \ge 1$ for all 
		$x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $p\lambda_1 \leq \lambda_0$ in the last line.
		Since $\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)(0,\cdot) \equiv 0$ is deterministic, and since $p \ge 2$ and $\lambda_0\in (0,1]$ 
		were
		chosen arbitrarily  independent of $\tilde \xi_1$ and $\tilde \xi_2$, from Lemma \ref{lem:tight_tem_moment}, $\Psi_2(\Sigma_U)$ admits a modification 
		taking values in 
		$\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ as desired for this step.
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
		Combining results from
		Steps 1, 2, 3 and 4
		with the argument at the beginning of this proof, we know that the desired result for this proposition holds.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{eq:TX}
		If predictable random fields $U$ and $V$ in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ satisfy that $[U]_{\mathrm p} = [V]_{\mathrm p}$, then they are indistinguishable, that is, almost surely $U(t,x) = V(t,x)$ for every $(t,x)\in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The condition $[U]_{\mathrm p} = [V]_{\mathrm p}$ says that
		\begin{equation}
			U(\omega,t,x) = V(\omega, t, x), \quad \text{$\mathbb P\otimes \Lambda$-a.e. $(\omega, t)\in \Omega \times [0,T]$,}\quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
		\end{equation}
		By Fubini's theorem, this implies that 
		\begin{equation}
			U(\omega,t,x) = V(\omega, t, x), \quad \text{$\mathbb P$-a.s. $\omega\in \Omega$,}\quad \text{$\Lambda$-a.e.~$t\in [0,T]$}, \quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
		\end{equation}
		Since ${\mathbb X}$ is separable, we know the existence of a countable dense subset ${\mathbb X}_0$ of ${\mathbb X}$. 
		Now the above display implies that for $\Lambda$-almost every $t\in [0,T]$,
		\begin{equation} \label{eq:CD}
			U(\omega,t,x) = V(\omega, t, x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}_0, \quad \text{$\mathbb P$-a.s. $\omega\in \Omega$}.
		\end{equation}
		Denote by $A$ the collection of $t\in [0,T]$ such that \eqref{eq:CD} holds. Then $\Lambda(A^{\mathrm c}) = 0$, where $A^{\mathrm c} := [0,T]\setminus A$.
		Denote by $(\mathcal O_k)_{k=1}^\infty$ a countable topological base of $[0,T]$.
		For every $k\in \mathbb N$, since $\Lambda(\mathcal O_k)>0$, we must have $\mathcal O_k \not\subset A^{\mathrm c}$, and therefore, $\mathcal O_k\cap A \neq \emptyset$. 
		Taking $t_k \in \mathcal O_k\cap A \neq \emptyset$ for each $k\in \mathbb N$, we obtain a countable dense subset $A_0:=\{t_k:k\in \mathbb N\}$ of $[0,T]$ such that 
		\begin{equation}
			U(\omega,t,x) = V(\omega, t, x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}_0, \quad \text{$\mathbb P$-a.s. $\omega\in \Omega$,}\quad t\in A_0.
		\end{equation}
		This implies that 
		\begin{equation}
			U(\omega,t,x) = V(\omega, t, x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}_0, \quad t\in A_0, \quad \text{$\mathbb P$-a.s. $\omega\in \Omega$}.
		\end{equation}
		Finally, since $U$ and $V$ are continuous random fields on $[0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$, we obtain the desired result for this lemma.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
\setcounter{step}{0}\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }\label{step:LT}
		Since $T>0$ was chosen arbitrarily, we only need to show the well-posedness of SPDE \eqref{eq:spde} up to time $T$.
		That is, we only need to show that there exists an up-to-indistinguishability unique $(\mathscr F_t)_{t\geq 0}$-adapted $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued random field $(u_t(x))_{t\in [0,T], x\in {\mathbb X}}$ such that, for every $t\in [0,T]$ and $x\in {\mathbb X}$, \eqref{eq:mild2} holds almost surely.
		It is then standard to extend this unique solution to infinite time. 
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
		We show in this step the existence of $(u_t(x))_{t\in [0,T], x\in {\mathbb X}}$ as desired in Step \ref{step:LT}. 
		Let the predictable random fields $U$ and $\widetilde U$ be given as in Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification}.
		Note that 
		\begin{equation}
			[\tilde U]_{\mathrm p} = [U]_{\mathrm p} = [\Phi(U)]_{\mathrm p} 
			\overset{\eqref{eq:Phisharp}}{=} \Phi^\sharp([U]_{\mathrm p}) =  
			\Phi^\sharp([\tilde U]_{\mathrm p}) = [\Phi(\tilde U)]_{\mathrm p}.
			\label{eq:Upm}
		\end{equation}
		Therefore, $\tilde U$ is also a random field as desired in Corollary \ref{cor:MfT}.
		As a consequence of this and Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification}, 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:PU}] $\Phi(\tilde U)$ admits a modification in $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$, denoted by $\tilde \Phi(\tilde U)$.
		\end{itemize}
		Now, since both $\tilde U$ and $\tilde \Phi(\tilde U)$ are $\mathcal C_{[0,T]}\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued predictable random fields and 
		\begin{equation}
			[\tilde U]_{\mathrm p} 
			\overset{\eqref{eq:Upm}}{=} [\Phi(\tilde U)]_{\mathrm p} 
			\overset{\eqref{eq:PU}}{=} [\tilde \Phi(\tilde U)]_{\mathrm p},
		\end{equation}
		by Lemma \ref{eq:TX}, $\tilde U$ and $\tilde \Phi(\tilde U)$ are indistinguishable.
		From this and \eqref{eq:PU}, $\tilde U$ is a modification of $\Phi(\tilde U)$.
		The desired existence result for this step now follows by taking $(u_t(x))_{t\in [0,T], x\in {\mathbb X}} = \tilde U$.
		
		\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. } 
		We prove the uniqueness of the random field $(u_t(x))_{t\in [0,T], x\in {\mathbb X}}$ as desired in Step \ref{step:LT} following the argument outlined in \cite[Theorem 2.2]{MR1271224}. 
		To do this, let $U$ and $V$ be two such random fields, and let $\lambda \in (0,1]$ be arbitrary. We only need to show that $U$ and $V$ are indistinguishable.
		For the rest of this proof, $n$ always denotes an integer satisfying $n>\|f\|_{(\lambda)}$. We set
		\begin{align*}
			\tau_n:= { T\wedge }\inf\left\{
			t\in [0,T]:\,
			\Norm*{U(t,\cdot)}_{ (\lambda)
			} \ge n \text{ or } \Norm*{V(t,\cdot)}_{(\lambda)} \ge n \right\}.
		\end{align*}
		Noticing that $t\mapsto \|U(t,\cdot)\|_{(\lambda)}$ and $t\mapsto \|V(t,\cdot)\|_{(\lambda)}$ are almost surely continuous on $[0,T]$, it is easy to see that
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:tnT}
			\lim_{n\to \infty}\tau_n = T, \quad \text{a.s.}
		\end{equation}
		For every $t\in[0,T]$, $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and $n\in\mathbb N$, define
		\[
			U_n(t,x):=\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}U(t,x),
			\qquad
			V_n(t,x):=\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}V(t,x).
		\]
		Since $\tau_n$ is a stopping time, $t\mapsto\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}$ is adapted and left-continuous, and hence predictable; thus $U_n$ and $V_n$ are predictable.
		Clearly, for every $n\in \mathbb N$, \[\sup_{t \in [0,T]}\Parentheses*{\Norm*{U_n(t,\cdot)}_{(\lambda)}+ \Norm*{V_n(t,\cdot)}_{(\lambda)}} \le 2n, \quad \text{a.s.} \] and in particular $U_n, V_n \in \mathbb M_{\lambda, 2}$.
		For every $n\in \mathbb N$, define predictable functions $b^{(n)}$ and $\sigma^{(n)}$ on $\Omega \times [0,T] \times {\mathbb X} \times \mathbb R$ such that for every $t\in [0,T], x\in {\mathbb X}$ and $u\in \mathbb R$, 
		\begin{align}
			&(b^{(n)}(\omega, t, x, u))_{\omega \in \Omega} := b^{(n)}(t,x,u):= b(t, x, u)\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq \tau_n\}},
			\\&(\sigma^{(n)}(\omega, t, x, u))_{\omega \in \Omega} :=\sigma^{(n)}(t, x, u) := \sigma(t, x, u) \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq \tau_n\}}.
		\end{align}
		Pay attention that, almost surely for every $n\in \mathbb N$, $t\in [0,T]$, $x\in {\mathbb X}$ and $u,v\in \mathbb R$, we have
		\begin{equation}
			|b^{(n)}(t,x,u)| + |\sigma^{(n)}(t,x,u)|  \leq |b(t,x,u)| + |\sigma(t,x,u)|
		\end{equation}
		and
		\begin{align}
			&|b^{(n)}(t,x,u) - b^{(n)}(t,x,v)| + |\sigma^{(n)}(t,x,u)- \sigma^{(n)}(t,x,v)|  
			\\&= |b(t,x,u) - b(t,x,v)|\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq \tau_n\}} + |\sigma(t,x,u)- \sigma(t,x,v)|  \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq \tau_n\}} 
			\\&\leq  |b(t,x,u) - b(t,x,v)| + |\sigma(t,x,u)- \sigma(t,x,v)|.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, for every $n\in \mathbb N$, 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)] the linear growth condition \eqref{eq:LiG} and the Lipschitz condition \eqref{ineq:g_lip} still hold with $b$, and $\sigma$, being replaced by $b^{(n)}$, and $\sigma^{(n)}$, respectively.
		\end{itemize} 
		For any $n\in \mathbb N$, $\tilde U \in \mathbb M_{\lambda, 2}$, $t\in [0,T]$ and $x\in {\mathbb X}$, define
		\begin{equation}
			B^{(n)}_{\tilde U}(t,x):= b^{(n)}(t,x, \tilde U(t,x)) \quad \text{and} \quad \Sigma^{(n)}_{\tilde U}(t,x):= \sigma^{(n)}(t,x, \tilde U(t,x)).
		\end{equation}
		Now, for every $n\in \mathbb N$, since all the conditions of Lemma \ref{lem:b_contract_combined} are met with $(\tilde \alpha, p ,g, b, \sigma)$ being replaced by $(\alpha, 2, f, b^{(n)}, \sigma^{(n)})$, we conclude that 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)] for each $\tilde U\in \mathbb M_{\lambda,2}$, $(B^{(n)}_{\tilde U},\Sigma^{(n)}_{\tilde U})$ is a Duhamel-integrable pair, and  $\Psi(f,B^{(n)}_{\tilde U},\Sigma^{(n)}_{\tilde U}) \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,2}$; moreover, 
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:uA}] uniformly in $ \tilde U, \tilde V \in \mathbb M_{\lambda, 2}$ and $A\ge 1$, we have 
			\begin{align}
				\Norm*{\Psi(f,B^{(n)}_{\tilde U},\Sigma^{(n)}_{\tilde U}) - \Psi(f,B^{(n)}_{\tilde V},\Sigma^{(n)}_{\tilde V})}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, 2, A}} 
				\lesssim  A^\frac{\alpha - 1}{2} 
				\Norm*{\tilde U - \tilde V}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, 2, A}}.
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
		In fact, we first note that for every $t\in [0,T]$ and $x\in {\mathbb X}$, almost surely,
		\begin{align} 
			&U(t,x) - V(t,x) 
			\\&= \iint_0^t G(s,y;t,x) b(s,y, U(s,y)) \mathrm ds m(\mathrm dy) 
			\\&\quad + \iint_0^t G(s,y;t,x) \sigma(s,y, U(s,y))W(\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy) 
			\\& \quad - \iint_0^t G(s,y;t,x) b(s,y, V(s,y)) \mathrm ds m(\mathrm dy) 
			\\& \quad - \iint_0^t G(s,y;t,x) \sigma(s,y, V(s,y))W(\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy). \label{eq:CF}
		\end{align}
		Since for every $x\in {\mathbb X}$, almost surely, both sides of \eqref{eq:CF} are continuous in $t\in [0,T]$,
		we have actually
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:Cv}] for every $x\in {\mathbb X}$, almost surely, \eqref{eq:CF} holds for every $t\in [0,T]$.
		\end{itemize}
		Define $Z_n:=U_n-V_n\in\mathbb M_{\lambda,2}$ for every $n\in\mathbb N$.
		Therefore, for every sufficiently large $n\in\mathbb N$, $t\in[0,T]$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}$, almost surely,
		\begin{align}
			&Z_n(t,x)=U_n(t,x)-V_n(t,x)
			\\&=\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}\big(U(t,x)-V(t,x)\big)
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:Cv}}{=} \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}
			\iint_0^{t} G(s,y;t,x) b^{(n)}(s,y, U_n(s,y)) \mathrm ds m(\mathrm dy)
			\\&\qquad + \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}
			\iint_0^{t} G(s,y;t,x) \sigma^{(n)}(s,y, U_n(s,y))W(\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy)
			\\& \qquad - \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}
			\iint_0^{t} G(s,y;t,x) b^{(n)}(s,y, V_n(s,y)) \mathrm ds m(\mathrm dy)
			\\& \qquad - \mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}
			\iint_0^{t} G(s,y;t,x) \sigma^{(n)}(s,y, V_n(s,y))W(\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy)
			\\&=\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}
			\left[\Psi(f, B^{(n)}_{U_n}, \Sigma^{(n)}_{U_n})
			-\Psi(f, B^{(n)}_{V_n}, \Sigma^{(n)}_{V_n})\right](t,x).
			\label{eq:UnVn}
		\end{align}
		We obtain that, uniformly for every sufficiently large $n\in \mathbb N$ and $A\geq 1$,
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{Z_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}
			&\overset{\eqref{eq:UnVn}}{\leq}
			\Norm*{\Psi(f,B^{(n)}_{U_n},\Sigma^{(n)}_{U_n})
			-\Psi(f,B^{(n)}_{V_n},\Sigma^{(n)}_{V_n})}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}\\
			&\overset{\eqref{eq:uA}}\lesssim
			A^\frac{\alpha - 1}{2}
			\Norm*{U_n-V_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}
			=A^\frac{\alpha - 1}{2}\Norm*{Z_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, there exists an $A\geq 1$ sufficiently large such that
		$\Norm*{Z_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}
		\leq \frac{1}{2}\Norm*{Z_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}$
		for every sufficiently large $n\in\mathbb N$, which implies that
		\begin{align}
			\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{\Lambda\text{-a.e. }t\in[0,T]}
			e^{-At}h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{Z_n(t,x)}_{L^2_\Omega}
			=\Norm*{Z_n}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,2,A}}=0.
		\end{align}
		Thus, for every $x\in{\mathbb X}$,
		\[
			\mathbf 1_{\{t\leq\tau_n\}}\big(U(t,x)-V(t,x)\big)=0
			\quad\text{for $\mathbb P\otimes\Lambda$-almost every $(\omega,t)$.}
		\]
		Let ${\mathbb X}_0$ be a countable dense subset of ${\mathbb X}$.
		By Fubini's theorem, on a common event of probability one, for every sufficiently large $n\in\mathbb N$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}_0$,
		\[
			U(t,x)=V(t,x)
			\quad\text{for $\Lambda$-almost every $t\in[0,T]$ satisfying $t\leq\tau_n(\omega)$.}
		\]
		Since $n>\|f\|_{(\lambda)}$, the continuity used in the definition of $\tau_n$ gives $\tau_n>0$ almost surely.
		The joint continuity of $U-V$ therefore first gives equality for every $t\in[0,\tau_n]$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}_0$, and then, by density, for every $t\in[0,\tau_n]$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}$.

		Let $\Omega_0$ be a probability-one event on which $\tau_n\uparrow T$, as in \eqref{eq:tnT}, and $U-V$ is jointly continuous. Since $(\tau_n)_{n\in\mathbb N}$ is nondecreasing, for some sufficiently large $n_0\geq1$,
		\begin{align}
			&\Omega_0\cap\left\{U(t,x)=V(t,x),\ \forall\,(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}\right\}\nonumber\\
			&\qquad=\Omega_0\cap\bigcap_{n\geq n_0}\left\{U(t,x)=V(t,x),\ \forall\,(t,x)\in[0,\tau_n]\times{\mathbb X}\right\}.
			\label{eq:continuity of sets_temp}
		\end{align}
		Since $\mathbb P(\Omega_0)=1$ and the events in the intersection above decrease with $n$, continuity from above of $\mathbb P$ gives
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:Zn1}
			\begin{aligned}
			1&=\lim_{n \to \infty}\mathbb P\left(
			U(t,x)=V(t,x),\
			\forall\,(t,x) \in [0,\tau_n]\times{\mathbb X}
			\right)\\
			&=\mathbb P\left( U(t,x)=V(t,x),\ \forall\,(t,x) \in [0,T]\times{\mathbb X} \right).
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		Therefore, $U$ and $V$ are indistinguishable random fields as desired.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:moment}
		Let $u$ be the  mild-solution given by Theorem~\ref{thm:main2}, and let $p\ge2$. Then, for every $T>0$ and every $\lambda\in(0,1]$, and also for $\lambda=0$ if $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:moment_every_t}
			\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{u(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}<\infty.
		\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Fix $T>0$, and let $U$ and $\tilde U$ be as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:main2}, constructed on $[0,T]$. By the uniqueness part of Theorem~\ref{thm:main2}, $u=\tilde U$ on $[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$ up to indistinguishability, and by \eqref{eq:Upm}, $\tilde U$ is a predictable modification of $U$. Hence, by Corollary~\ref{cor:MfT}, $\Norm*{u}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}=\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}<\infty$ for every $\lambda\in(0,1]$, and also for $\lambda=0$ if $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm b}_{\mathbb X}$.
		
		Now fix $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and $t\in[0,T]$. We see that the bound $h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{u(s,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}\le\Norm*{u}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}$ holds for $\Lambda$-a.e.\ $s\in[0,T]$. Hence, we may choose such $s=t_j$ with $t_j\to t$. Since $u$ has continuous paths in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ and $m$ has full support, $u(t_j,x)\to u(t,x)$ almost surely, and Fatou's lemma gives
		\begin{align*}
			h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{u(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}\le\liminf_{j\to\infty}h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{u(t_j,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}\le\Norm*{u}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}.
		\end{align*}
		Taking the supremum over $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$ proves \eqref{ineq:moment_every_t}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:continuity_initial}
		Let $(f_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$  be a sequence converging to $f$ in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
		For each $n\in \mathbb N$, let $u^{(n)}$ be the unique strong solution to \eqref{eq:spde} with $f$ being replaced by $f_n$. Then for any $p \ge 2$, $\lambda \in (0,1]$ and $T>0$, we have
		$\|u^{(n)} - u\|_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p}(T)} \to 0$ when $n\uparrow \infty$.
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Fix $p \ge 2$, $\lambda \in (0,1]$ and $T>0$. 
		Define $v^{(n)}_t(x):= u^{(n)}_t(x) - u_t(x)$ for every $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $n\in \mathbb N$.
		Uniformly for every $(t,x)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $n\in \mathbb N$, 
		\begin{align}
			&\Norm*{v^{(n)}_t(x)}_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\\&= \Bigg\| P_t^*(f_n-f)(x) +   \int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x) \Parentheses*{b(s,y, u^{(n)}_s(y)) - b(s,y, u_s(y)) } m(\mathrm dy) 
			\\& \qquad + \iint_0^t G_{t-s}(y,x)\Parentheses*{\sigma(s,y, u^{(n)}_s(y)) - \sigma(s,y, u_s(y)) }W(\mathrm ds\mathrm dy)\Bigg\|_{L^p_\Omega}
			\\&\overset{\text{Minkowski}}{\leq}  P_t^*(|f_n-f|)(x) +  \int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x) \Norm*{b(s,y, u^{(n)}_s(y)) - b(s,y, u_s(y)) }_{L^p_\Omega} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\& \qquad + \Norm*{\iint_0^t G_{t-s}(y,x)\Parentheses*{\sigma(s,y, u^{(n)}_s(y)) - \sigma(s,y, u_s(y)) }W(\mathrm ds\mathrm dy)}_{L^p_\Omega}
			\\&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs}}}{\lesssim} P_t^*(|f_n-f|)(x) +  \int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x) \Norm*{b(s,y, u^{(n)}_s(y)) - b(s,y, u_s(y)) }_{L^p_\Omega} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\& \qquad + \Parentheses*{\int_0^t \Norm*{ G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)\Norm*{\sigma(s,\cdot, u^{(n)}_s(\cdot)) - \sigma(s,\cdot, u_s(\cdot)) }_{L^p_\Omega} }_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds}^{1/2}
			\\&\overset{\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim}P_t^*(|f_n-f|)(x) +  \int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x) \Norm*{v^{(n)}_s(y)}_{L^p_\Omega} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\& \qquad + \Parentheses*{\int_0^t \Norm*{ G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)\Norm*{v^{(n)}_s(\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega} }_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds}^{1/2}. \label{eq:vntx}
		\end{align}
		We denote the three terms on the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:vntx} by $I_0^{(n)}(t,x)$, $I_1^{(n)}(t,x)$ and $I_2^{(n)}(t,x)$, respectively.
		
		It holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $n \in \mathbb N$  that 
		\begin{align}
			&
			I_0^{(n)}(t,x) 
			= \int_{\mathbb X} G_t(y,x)|f_n(y)-f(y)| m(\mathrm dy)
			\le \int_{\mathbb X} G_t(y,x) h(y)^{\lambda} 
			\|f_n - f\|_{(\lambda)}m(\mathrm dy)
			\\&=  \Norm*{G_t(\cdot, x)h(\cdot)^\lambda}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \|f_n - f\|_{(\lambda)} 
			\overset{
				\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}
			}{\lesssim} h(x)^\lambda \Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)}. \label{eq:vntx0}
		\end{align}
		Similarly, it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$  and $n\in \mathbb N$ that 
		\begin{align}
			&
			I_1^{(n)}(t,x) 
			\le \iint_0^t  G_{t-s}(y,x) h(y)^\lambda 
			\Norm*{\Norm*{
					v^{(n)}_s(\cdot)
				}_{L^p_\Omega}}_{(\lambda)} \mathrm ds m(\mathrm d y)
			\\& \overset{\text{Fubini}}{=} \int_0^t \Parentheses*{\int G_{t-s}(y,x) h(y)^\lambda m(\mathrm dy)} \Norm*{\Norm*{v^{(n)}_s(\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}}_{(\lambda)} \mathrm ds 
			\overset{
				\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}
			}{\lesssim}  h(x)^\lambda\int_0^t  
			\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds. \label{eq:vntx1}
		\end{align}
		Finally, it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$  and $n\in \mathbb N$ that
		\begin{align}
			&
			I_2^{(n)}(t,x) 
			\leq \Parentheses*{\int_0^t \Norm*{ G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)h(\cdot)^\lambda \Norm*{\Norm*{v^{(n)}_s(\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}}_{(\lambda)} }_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds}^{1/2}
			\\
			&\leq \Parentheses*{\int_0^t \|G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) h(\cdot)^\lambda\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \left\|\|v^{(n)}_s(\cdot)\|_{L^p_\Omega}\right\|_{(\lambda)}^2 \mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}
			\\&\overset{
				\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}
			}{\lesssim} h(x)^\lambda \left(\int_0^t (t-s)^{-\alpha} 
			\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}^2
			\mathrm ds \right)^\frac{1}{2}. \label{eq:vntx2}
		\end{align}
		
		Combining \eqref{eq:vntx}, \eqref{eq:vntx0}, \eqref{eq:vntx1} and \eqref{eq:vntx2}, we see that uniformly in $\tau \leq t$ in $[0,T]$, $x\in {\mathbb X}$ and $n\in \mathbb N$, 
		\begin{align}
			&h(x)^{-\lambda} \Norm*{v^{(n)}_{\tau}(x)}_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\overset{\eqref{eq:vntx}}{\lesssim} h(x)^{-\lambda} I_0^{(n)}(\tau,x) + h(x)^{-\lambda} I_1^{(n)}(\tau,x) + h(x)^{-\lambda} I_2^{(n)}(\tau,x)
			\\&\overset{ \eqref{eq:vntx0}, \eqref{eq:vntx1}, \eqref{eq:vntx2}}{\lesssim} \Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^\tau \Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds + \Parentheses*{\int_0^\tau (\tau-s)^{-\alpha} 
				\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}^2
				\mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}
			\\&= \Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^\tau \Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds + \Parentheses*{\int_0^\tau s^{-\alpha} 
				\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(\tau-s)}^2
				\mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}
			\\&\leq  \Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^t \Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds + \Parentheses*{\int_0^t s^{-\alpha} 
				\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(t-s)}^2
				\mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}
			\\&=  \Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^t \Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds + \Parentheses*{\int_0^t {(t-s)}^{-\alpha} 
				\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}^2
				\mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}. \label{eq:hxl}
		\end{align}
		Then uniformly in $t\in [0,T]$ and $n\in \mathbb N$,
		\begin{align}
			&\|v^{(n)}\|_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(t)}^2 = \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{\text{$\Lambda$-a.e. $\tau \in [0,t]$}} h(x)^{-2\lambda} \Norm*{v^{(n)}_\tau(x)}^2_{L^p_\Omega} 
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:hxl}}{\lesssim} \Parentheses*{\Norm*{f_n - f}_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^t \Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
				\mathrm ds + \Parentheses*{\int_0^t {(t-s)}^{-\alpha} 
					\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}^2
					\mathrm ds }^\frac{1}{2}}^2
			\\&\overset{\text{Jensen}}\lesssim  \Norm*{f_n - f}^2_{(\lambda)} + \int_0^t \Norm*{v^{(n)}}^2_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}
			\mathrm ds + \int_0^t {(t-s)}^{-\alpha} 
			\Norm*{v^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(s)}^2
			\mathrm ds.
		\end{align}
		For $t \in [0,T]$, set $U_n(t):=\|v^{(n)}\|_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}(t)}$, which is finite by Corollary~\ref{cor:moment}. By Gr\"{o}nwall's inequality (see for example, \cite{chu1967gronwall}), we obtain uniformly in $t \in [0,T]$ that 
		\begin{align}
			U_n(t)^2 \lesssim \|f_n - f\|_{(\lambda)}^2\left(1+ \int_0^t \left( 1+ (t-s)^{-\alpha} \right) \exp\left( \int_0^s(1+(s-\tau)^{-\alpha})\mathrm d\tau  \right)\mathrm ds\right)
		\end{align}
		which converges to zero uniformly in $t \in [0,T]$ as $n \to \infty$ since $\alpha \in (0,1)$.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:approx_estimates}
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item \label{lem:approx_estimates1} 
			Let $\alpha \in (0,1)$ be given as in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}. Then there exists $\kappa \in \mathbb{R}$, independent of $\varepsilon$, such that
			uniformly in $t>0$, $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$,
			\begin{align}\label{ineq:R_bound}
				\left\|r_t^\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2
				\lesssim t^{-\alpha}e^{\kappa t},\qquad
				\left\|r_t^\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\right\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
				\lesssim e^{\kappa t}.
			\end{align}
			\item  For each $t >0$,
			\begin{align}\label{lim:p_approx}
				\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0}\sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \int_0^t \left\| \left|\int_{\mathbb X}  r_s^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy) -  G_s(\cdot, x)
				\right|\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K}\,\mathrm ds = 0.
			\end{align}
			\item For each $T>0$, there exists a $c_T \in (0,1]$, and a function $\varepsilon \to \gamma_\varepsilon$ with $\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0} \gamma_\varepsilon = 0$, so that,  uniformly in $(t,x) \in (0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon >0$, it holds that
			\begin{align}\label{ineq:p_approx_m}
				\int_{\mathbb X} \left|r_t^\varepsilon(y,x)  - G_t(y,x)\right| m(\mathrm dy)\lesssim e^{-c_T\frac{t}{\varepsilon}} +  \frac{\gamma_\varepsilon}{t^\beta},
			\end{align}
			where $\beta$ is as in \eqref{cond:time_holder}.
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:non_negtive}
		Suppose that
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero}] for any $T>0$, uniformly for $\omega \in \Omega \setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$
			and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$,
			\begin{align}
				b(\omega,t,x,0) \geq 0, \qquad \sigma(\omega,t,x,0) = 0.
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{ineq:non_negative_approx_coef_ineq}]
			there exists an $\eta >0$ 
			so that for any $T>0$, 
			uniformly in $\omega \in \Omega \setminus \mathrm N_{\ref{c:Lip}}$, $u\in \mathbb R$,
			and $(t,x,y) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}^2$,
			\begin{align}
				\Absolute*{b(\omega,t,x,u) - b(\omega,t,y,u)} \lesssim d(x,y)^\eta (1+|u|),\\
				\Absolute*{\sigma(\omega,t,x,u) - \sigma(\omega,t,y,u)} \lesssim d(x,y)^\eta |u|;
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
		and that $f$ is non-negative.
		Then  $u$ is non-negative.
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
In light of Proposition \ref{prop:continuity_initial} and Lemma \ref{lem:C_temp_density_0}, it is enough to assume that $f$ is bounded, continuous, and non-negative.
		
		For $\varepsilon>0$ and $(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$, define $W^\varepsilon(t,x)=:W^\varepsilon_x(t)$ via
		\begin{align}\label{def:white_noise_cov}
			W^\varepsilon(t,x) := \iint_0^t  G_\varepsilon(y,x)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy).
		\end{align}
		Observe that for each $(t,x),\, (t',x') \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$, 
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb E}\left[W^\varepsilon(t,x)W^\varepsilon(t',x')\right] &= \Parentheses*{t\wedge t'}\left \langle Q\left[G_\varepsilon(\cdot, x)\right]_K, \left[G_\varepsilon(\cdot,x') \right]_K \right \rangle_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_K}\\
			&:=\Parentheses*{t'\wedge t} k_\varepsilon(x,x').
		\end{align}
		By \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}, we see there is some $\alpha \in (0,1)$ so that $\sup_{x}k_\varepsilon(x,x)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\alpha}$ uniformly in $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$.
		
		For each fixed $x\in{\mathbb X}$, the process $(W^\varepsilon(t,x),\,t\geq0)$ is a Brownian motion with 
		\begin{align}\label{eq:q_var_ep}
			\left[\int_0^\cdot W^\varepsilon(\mathrm ds,x)\right]_t =\left[ \iint_0^\cdot  G_\varepsilon(y , x)W(\mathrm dy,\mathrm ds)\right]_t = t k_\varepsilon(x,x)
		\end{align}
		where $([M]_t)_{t \ge 0}$ is the quadratic variation process of a martingale $(M_t)_{t\ge0}$. 

		Let $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$ and recall $\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}$, $P^{\varepsilon,*}$, and the density $r_t^\varepsilon$ defined after \eqref{approx_kernel}. Formally, we consider the following equation with initial condition $u_\varepsilon(0,x) = f(x)$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathrm d u_\varepsilon(t,x)
			={}&\left(\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}u_\varepsilon(t,x)+b(t,x,u_\varepsilon(t,x))\right)\mathrm dt+\sigma(t,x,u_\varepsilon(t,x))W^\varepsilon(\mathrm dt,x)
		\end{align*}
		whose mild form is given by
		\begin{align}\label{eq:cont_1d_mild}
			u_\varepsilon(t,x) = P^{\varepsilon,*}_t f(x) +&  \int_0^t e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}b\Parentheses*{s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x)}\mathrm ds\nonumber\\
			&+\int_0^t\mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)b(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y))m(\mathrm dy)\nonumber\\
			&+\int_0^t e^{- (t-s)/\varepsilon}\sigma(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x)) W^\varepsilon(\mathrm ds,x)\nonumber\\
			&+\int_{\mathbb X}\int_0^t r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\sigma(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y))W^\varepsilon(\mathrm ds,y)m(\mathrm dy).
		\end{align}
		One can show that \eqref{eq:cont_1d_mild} has a $\mathcal C_{[0,T]} \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued solution satisfying
		\begin{align}\label{boud: lp_loc_both}
			\sup_{0 < \varepsilon\le 1}\sup_{0 \le t \le T} \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} {\mathbb E} \left[|u_\varepsilon(t,x)|^2\right] < \infty,\qquad \forall T>0.
		\end{align}
		 Since $\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}$ is bounded and
		\[
			P_t^{\varepsilon,*}-I=\int_0^t\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}P_s^{\varepsilon,*}\mathrm ds,
		\]
		ordinary and stochastic Fubini (Lemma \ref{thm:stochastic_fubini}), conditions \eqref{eq:LiG}, \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}, \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}, \eqref{ineq:R_bound}, and the bound \eqref{boud: lp_loc_both} ensure that  \eqref{eq:cont_1d_mild} satisfies
		\begin{align}\label{eq:cont_1d_sde}
			u_\varepsilon(t,x)=f(x)&+\int_0^t\left(\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}u_\varepsilon(s,x)+b(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x))\right)\mathrm ds\nonumber\\
			&+\int_0^t\sigma(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x))W^\varepsilon(\mathrm ds,x).
		\end{align}
		The last term is a one-dimensional It\^{o} integral. Hence, by \eqref{def:white_noise_cov} and \eqref{eq:cont_1d_sde}, $\left(u_\varepsilon(t,x)\right)_{t\geq0}$ is a continuous semimartingale for each fixed $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}$.
		
		Let $\varphi(u) := - \left(u \wedge 0\right) = u^-,\, u \in \mathbb{R} $, which is convex. By It\^o--Meyer--Tanaka's formula (\cite[Proposition 9.2]{le2016brownian}), we see for each $t \ge0$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:u_epsilon negative part}
			\begin{aligned}
				{\mathbb E}[\varphi(u_\varepsilon(t,x))]= \varphi(f(x)) &- {\mathbb E}\left[\int_0^t \mathbf 1_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0} \left(\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*} u_\varepsilon(s,x) +b(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x)) \right)\mathrm ds\right]\\
				&- {\mathbb E}\left[\int_0^t \mathbf 1_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0} \,\sigma(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x))\mathrm dW_x^\varepsilon(s) -\frac12L^0_x(t)\right]
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where for each $x \in {\mathbb X}$, $\left(L_x^0(t)\right)_{t \ge 0 }$ is the local time of $(u_\varepsilon(t,x))_{t \ge 0}$ at zero, which is non-negative and non-decreasing. Now,  
		conditions \eqref{ineq:g_lip} and \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero} imply that there is some $L\ge0$ which is the common Lipschitz constant for $b$ and $\sigma$ on $[0,T]$. Hence, for $r\le0$ and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$, $b(t,x,r)\ge -L|r|$. 
		By the definition of  $\mathcal L^{\varepsilon,*}$, we get the second term on the right hand side  of \eqref{eq:u_epsilon negative part} (including the minus sign) is smaller than
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:u_epsilon second}
			\begin{aligned}
				&{\mathbb E}\left[\int_0^t \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{1}_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0} u_\varepsilon(s,x)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\boldsymbol{1}_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0}P_\varepsilon^* u_{\varepsilon}(s)(x) +L\,\boldsymbol{1}_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0} |u_\varepsilon(s,x)| \mathrm ds\right]\\
				\le& \left(L +1/\varepsilon\right) \int_0^t {\mathbb E}[\varphi(u_\varepsilon(s,x))]\,\mathrm ds-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G(\varepsilon,y,x){\mathbb E}\left[ \boldsymbol{1}_{u_\varepsilon(s,x) \le 0}u_\varepsilon(s,y)\right]\,m(\mathrm dy)\,\mathrm ds
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where $\boldsymbol{1}_A(\cdot)$ denotes the indicator function on the set $A$.  By \cite[Proposition 9.9]{le2016brownian}, we see for each $x \in {\mathbb X}$, it holds almost surely that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:localtime epsilon}
			L^0_x(t) &\,\,= \lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}
			\frac{1}{\delta}\int_0^t 1_{\left\{0\le u_\varepsilon(s,x)\le \delta \right\}}\mathrm d\left[ u_\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\right]_s \nonumber\\
				&\,\,=\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}
				\frac{1}{\delta} \int_0^t 1_{\left\{0\le u_\varepsilon(s,x)\le \delta \right\}}\sigma\big(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x)\big)^2 \mathrm d\left[ W_x^\varepsilon(\cdot)\right]_s\nonumber\\
				&\overset{
				\eqref{ineq:g_lip}, \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero}}{\le} L^2\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}
				\frac{1}{\delta}\int_0^t 1_{\left\{0\le u_\varepsilon(s,x)\le \delta \right\}} \delta^2\mathrm d\left[ W_x^\varepsilon(\cdot)\right]_s\nonumber\\
				&\overset{\eqref{eq:q_var_ep}}{\le} L^2 k_\varepsilon(x,x) \lim_{\delta \downarrow 0}
				\frac{1}{\delta} \int_0^t  1_{\left\{0\le u_\varepsilon(s,x)\le \delta \right\}} \delta^2 \mathrm ds\nonumber\\
			&\,\,=0.
		\end{align}
		In addition, we see by \eqref{boud: lp_loc_both}, \eqref{eq:q_var_ep} and the Lipschitz bound on $\sigma$ that the stochastic integral in \eqref{eq:u_epsilon negative part} is square-integrable and hence has mean zero.
		Inserting \eqref{eq:u_epsilon second} and \eqref{ineq:localtime epsilon} into equation \eqref{eq:u_epsilon negative part} and recalling $\varphi(f) \equiv 0$ on ${\mathbb X}$ since $f \ge 0$, we see that 
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb E}[\varphi(u_\varepsilon(t,x))] &\le ( 
			L +1/\varepsilon) \int_0^t {\mathbb E}\left[ \varphi(u_\varepsilon(s,x))\right] \mathrm ds \\
			&\quad \quad\quad+  (1/\varepsilon) \int_0^t\mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G(\varepsilon,y,x){\mathbb E}\left[\varphi(u_\varepsilon(s,y)
			)\right]m(\mathrm dy).
		\end{align}
		By condition \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} when $\lambda = 0$, we may take the supremum over $x \in {\mathbb X}$ on both sides and apply Gronwall's lemma to see that $\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} {\mathbb E}\left[\varphi(u_\varepsilon(t,x))\right] = 0$ for $t\in[0,T]$. Since $T>0$ is arbitrary, we see for each $(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$, $u_\varepsilon(t,x)\ge0$ almost surely.
		
		Let $(u_t)_{t\geq0}$ be the unique solution to \eqref{eq:spde}. We will show that $\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0}u_\varepsilon = u$ in an appropriate sense. By Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}, Lemma \ref{lem:approx_estimates} together with the bound \eqref{boud: lp_loc_both}, we may apply stochastic Fubini (Lemma \ref{thm:stochastic_fubini}) to see
		\begin{align}
			&\int_{\mathbb X}\int_0^t  r_{t - s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\sigma\big(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y)\big) W^{\varepsilon}(\mathrm ds,y) \,m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad-\iint_0^t G(t-s,z,x)\sigma\big(s,z,u(s,z)\big) W( \mathrm dz, \mathrm ds)\\
			=&  \iint_0^t \left(\int_{\mathbb X}  r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\sigma\big(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y) \big) G_\varepsilon(z,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)\right)\,W(\mathrm dz,\mathrm ds)\\
			&\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad\quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad -\iint_0^t  G(t - s,z,x)\sigma\big(s,z,u(s,z)\big) W(\mathrm dz, \mathrm ds)\\
			=&\iint_0^t \left(\int_{\mathbb X}  r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \big(\sigma\big(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y) \big)-\sigma\big(s,y,u(s,y) \big) \big) G_\varepsilon(z,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)\right)\,W(\mathrm dz,\mathrm ds)\\
			&\quad+\iint_0^t\left(\int_{\mathbb X}  r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \big(\sigma\big(s,y,u(s,y) \big)-\sigma\big(s,z,u(s,z) \big)\big) G_\varepsilon(z,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)\right)\,W(\mathrm dz,\mathrm ds)\\
			&\quad+\iint_0^t\bigg(\int_{\mathbb X}  r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\sigma\big(s,z,u(s,z) \big) G_\varepsilon(z,y)\,m(\mathrm dy) \\
			&\qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad \qquad- G(t - s,z,x)\sigma\big(s,z,u(s,z)\big)\bigg)\,W(\mathrm dz,\mathrm ds).
		\end{align}
		We obtain uniformly in  $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}
			&{\mathbb E}\left[|u_\varepsilon(t,x) - u(t,x)|^2\right] \\
			\lesssim &\left|P^{\varepsilon,*}_tf(x) - P_t^*f(x)\right|^2 + {\mathbb E}\left[\left|\int_0^t e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon} b(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x) )\,\mathrm ds \right|^2\right]\\
			&+{\mathbb E}\left[\left|\int_0^t \mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\Big(b(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y)) - b(s,y,u(s,y))\Big) m(\mathrm dy)\, \right|^2\right]\\
			&+ {\mathbb E}\left[ \left|\int_0^t \mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} \Big(r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) - G(t-s,y,x) \Big) b(s,y,u(s,y)) \,m(\mathrm dy)\right|^2\right]\\
			&+ \int_0^t e^{-2(t-s)/\varepsilon} \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}{\mathbb E}\left[\sigma(s,x,u_\varepsilon(s,x))^2\right] \varepsilon^{-\alpha}\mathrm ds\\
			&+\int_0^t \Norm*{\Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\Absolute*{\sigma(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y))-\sigma(s,y,u(s,y))} G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy)}_{L^2_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
			&+\int_0^t \Norm*{ \Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \Absolute*{\sigma(s,y,u(s,y)) - \sigma(s,\cdot,u(s,\cdot))} G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy)}_{L^2_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
			&+\int_0^t {\mathbb E}\left[\left\|\left(\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t - s}^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy) - G(t-s,\cdot,x) \right) \sigma(s,\cdot,u(s,\cdot))\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K} \right] \mathrm ds\\
			:=& \sum_{i = 1}^8 J_i(\varepsilon,t,x).
		\end{align}
		Using \eqref{ineq:p_approx_m} of Lemma \ref{lem:approx_estimates} and the boundedness of $f$, we see that, for each $T>0$, there exists $c_T>0$ such that, uniformly in sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$ and $t\in(0,T]$,
		\begin{align}
			\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}J_1(\varepsilon,t,x)
			\lesssim e^{-c_Tt/\varepsilon}+\frac{\gamma_\varepsilon}{t^\beta},
		\end{align}
		where $\beta$ is as in \eqref{cond:time_holder}.
		At $t=0$, $P_0^{\varepsilon,*}f=P_0^*f=f$, and hence $J_1(\varepsilon,0,x)=0$.

		Since $f$ is bounded, we see by Corollary~\ref{cor:moment} with $\lambda = 0$ and $p=2$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:u_uniform_second_moment}
			\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}{\mathbb E}\bigl[|u(t,x)|^2\bigr]<\infty.
		\end{align}
		Together with \eqref{ineq:p_approx_m}  and Minkowski's inequality,
		\begin{align}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}J_4(\varepsilon,t,x)\lesssim(\varepsilon+\gamma_\varepsilon)^2\longrightarrow0,\qquad \text{ as }\varepsilon\downarrow0.
		\end{align}
		Also, by \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} with $\lambda=0$, \eqref{eq:LiG}, and \eqref{boud: lp_loc_both}, we obtain, uniformly in $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$,
		\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}J_2(\varepsilon,t,x)\lesssim\varepsilon^2,\qquad
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}J_5(\varepsilon,t,x)\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-\alpha},
		\end{align*}
		where $\alpha\in(0,1)$ is the exponent in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}.
		Hence, 
		\begin{align}
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}\big(J_2(\varepsilon,t,x)+J_5(\varepsilon,t,x)\big)=0.
		\end{align}
		By \eqref{eq:LiG}, \eqref{ineq:u_uniform_second_moment}, and Lemma \ref{lem:approx_estimates},
		\begin{align}
			\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0} \sup_{t \in [0,T]} \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} J_8(\varepsilon,t,x) &\lesssim \lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0}\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}\int_0^T\left\|\left|\int_{\mathbb X} r_s^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy) - G(s,\cdot,x)\right|\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K} \mathrm ds \\ &\overset{\eqref{lim:p_approx}}{=}0.
		\end{align}
		Let $U_\varepsilon(t) = \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \Norm*{u_\varepsilon(t,x) - u(t,x)}_{L^2_\Omega}^2$. By Minkowski's inequality, uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon >0$,
		\begin{align}
			J_3(\varepsilon,t,x) &\overset{\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim} \int_0^t \left( \int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)m(\mathrm dy)\right)^2U_\varepsilon(s) \mathrm ds\\
			&\,\overset{\eqref{ineq:R_bound}}{\lesssim} \int_0^tU_\varepsilon(s)\mathrm ds\\
			&\,\,\,\lesssim \int_0^t (t-s)^{-\alpha}U_\varepsilon(s) \mathrm ds.
		\end{align}
		By Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs} and Minkowski's inequality, it holds uniformly in  $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$ that
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:J6 step 1}
			\begin{aligned}
				J_6(\varepsilon,t,x) &\,\,\,\lesssim \int_0^t \Norm*{\Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\Absolute*{\sigma(s,y,u_\varepsilon(s,y))-\sigma(s,y,u(s,y))} G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy)}_{L^2_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
				&\overset{\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim} \int_0^t\Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)\Absolute*{U_\varepsilon(s) }^\frac{1}{2}G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy)}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
				&\,\,\,= \int_0^t \Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy)}^2_{\mathcal H_K} U_\varepsilon(s)\, \mathrm ds.
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		Let $\widetilde \kappa \in \mathbb{R}$ be given by Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}. By the semigroup property, uniformly in $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ and $(t,x) \in (0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:mollified_conv_H_norm}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_t^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy)}^2_{\mathcal H_K} &\,\,= \Norm*{e^{-t/\varepsilon}\sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{\Parentheses*{t/\varepsilon}^n}{n!} G(\varepsilon (n+1),\cdot,x)}^2_{\mathcal H_K}\\
				&\overset{\text{Minkowski\&Jensen}}{\le} \Parentheses*{e^{-t/\varepsilon}\sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!} \Norm*{G\Parentheses*{\varepsilon(n+1),\cdot,x}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2}\\
				&\overset{\eqref{ineq:hk_k long time} }{\lesssim} e^{-t/\varepsilon}\sum_{n = 0}^\infty \frac{(te^{\varepsilon\widetilde\kappa}/\varepsilon)^n}{n!} (\varepsilon(n+1))^{-\alpha}\\
				&\,\,\le e^{-t/\varepsilon + te^{\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa}/\varepsilon} \Parentheses*{e^{-te^{\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa}/\varepsilon}\sum_{n = 0}^\infty \frac{\Parentheses*{te^{\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa}/\varepsilon}^{n+1}}{(n+1)!} t^{-1}e^{-\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa}}^{\alpha}\\
				&\,\,\lesssim t^{-\alpha} \exp \left(\frac{te^{\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa} - t}{\varepsilon} \right)\\
				&\lesssim t^{-\alpha},
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where Jensen's inequality was used in the second and fourth lines, $\alpha\in(0,1)$ is from \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}, and there exists $\widetilde \kappa_0 \in \mathbb{R}$ such that, for all $t \ge 0$,
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{\varepsilon \in (0,1]}\exp\Parentheses*{\frac{te^{\varepsilon \widetilde\kappa} - t}{\varepsilon}} \le e^{\widetilde \kappa_0 t}.
		\end{align*}
		Inserting this back into the inequality \eqref{ineq:J6 step 1}, we see uniformly in $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			J_6(\varepsilon,t,x) \lesssim \int_0^t (t-s)^{-\alpha} U_\varepsilon(s) \mathrm ds.
		\end{align*}
		
		
		Now we consider $J_7$. By Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime} and Minkowski's inequality, for each $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$ and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}
			J_7(\varepsilon,t,x) &\le \int_0^t \left\|\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \|\sigma(s,y,u(s,y)) - \sigma(s,\cdot,u(s,\cdot))\|_{L^2_\Omega} G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy) \right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds.
		\end{align}
		By the boundedness of $f$, \eqref{ineq:u_uniform_second_moment}, \eqref{ineq:spatial_holder_long_time}, and the calculations in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification} with $p=2$ and $\lambda=0$, there exists $\xi'\in(0,1)$ such that, uniformly in $s\in(0,T]$ and $y,z\in{\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:u_bounded_spatial_increment}
			\Norm*{u(s,y)-u(s,z)}_{L^2_\Omega}
			\lesssim \left(\frac{d(y,z)^{\xi'}}{s^\beta}\right)\wedge1.
		\end{align}
		Set $\xi:=\eta\wedge\xi'$. For $d(y,z)\le1$, uniformly in $s\in(0,T]$,
		\begin{align}
			&{\quad} \Norm*{\sigma(s,y,u(s,y))-\sigma(s,z,u(s,z))}_{L^2_\Omega}\\
			&{\le} \Norm*{\sigma(s,y,u(s,y))-\sigma(s,z,u(s,y))}_{L^2_\Omega}
			+\Norm*{\sigma(s,z,u(s,y))-\sigma(s,z,u(s,z))}_{L^2_\Omega}\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:non_negative_approx_coef_ineq},\,\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim}}d(y,z)^\eta\Norm*{u(s,y)}_{L^2_\Omega}+\Norm*{u(s,y)-u(s,z)}_{L^2_\Omega}\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:u_bounded_spatial_increment}}{\lesssim}}
			\left(\frac{d(y,z)^\xi}{s^\beta}\right)\wedge1,
		\end{align}
		where $\beta\in[0,1)$ is as in \eqref{cond:time_holder}.
		For $d(y,z)>1$, \eqref{ineq:g_lip}, \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero}, and \eqref{ineq:u_uniform_second_moment} give the cap by $1$. Thus the last estimate holds for all $y,z\in{\mathbb X}$.
		
		Therefore,  it holds uniformly in $x,z \in {\mathbb X}$ and $\delta \in (0,1]$ that
		\begin{align}
			&\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \|\sigma(s,y,u(s,y)) - \sigma(s,z,u(s,z))\|_{L^2_\Omega} G_\varepsilon(z,y)m(\mathrm dy)\\
			\lesssim &\left( \left(\frac{\delta^\xi}{s^\beta}\wedge 1\right)\int_{d(z,y)\le \delta} + \int_{d(z,y) > \delta}\right) r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(z,y)m(\mathrm{d}y)
		\end{align}
		Hence, for each $T>0$, it holds uniformly in $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$ and $(t,x)\in(0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align}
			J_7(\varepsilon,t,x) \lesssim \int_0^{t} J_{7,1}(\varepsilon,t,s,x) + J_{7,2}(\varepsilon,t-s,x) \mathrm d s 
		\end{align}
		where 
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:J71}
			\begin{aligned}
