				J_{7,1}(\varepsilon,t,s,x)&\,\,: = \left(\frac{\delta^\xi}{s^\beta}\wedge 1 \right) \Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy)}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \\
				&\overset{\eqref{ineq:mollified_conv_H_norm}}{\lesssim} \left(\frac{\delta^\xi}{s^\beta}\wedge 1 \right)(t-s)^{-\alpha},
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		uniformly in $\delta, \varepsilon \in (0,1]$, $0<s<t\le T$, $x \in {\mathbb X}$,  and 
		\begin{align}
			J_{7,2}(\varepsilon,t-s,x):&\,\,= \left\|\int_{\mathbb X} \mathbf 1_{B_\delta(y)^c}(\cdot) r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y) m(\mathrm dy)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2\\
			&\,\,\le  \Parentheses*{\int_{\mathbb X}  r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x) \Norm*{\mathbf 1_{B_\delta(y)^c}(\cdot) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)}_{\mathcal H_K} m(\mathrm dy)}^2\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:R_bound}}{\lesssim} \sup_{y \in {\mathbb X}} \Norm*{\mathbf 1_{B_\delta(y)^c}(\cdot) G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)}_{\mathcal H_K}^2,
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $\delta,\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, $0<s<t\le T$, and $x\in{\mathbb X}$. Here we used Minkowski's inequality.
		
		
		Fix $\theta\in(0,1]$ so that $\alpha+\theta\beta<1$. Since $a\wedge1\le a^\theta$ for every $a\ge0$, \eqref{ineq:J71} gives, uniformly in $(t,x)\in (0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $\delta, \varepsilon \in (0,1)$,
		\begin{align}
			\int_0^t J_{7,1}(\varepsilon,t,s,x) \mathrm ds
			&\lesssim \int_0^t\left(\frac{\delta^\xi}{s^\beta}\wedge 1 \right)(t-s)^{-\alpha}\mathrm ds \\
			&\le \delta^{\xi\theta}\int_0^t s^{-\theta\beta}(t-s)^{-\alpha}\mathrm ds\\
			&= \delta^{\xi\theta}t^{1-\alpha-\theta\beta}
			\int_0^1 r^{-\theta\beta}(1-r)^{-\alpha}\mathrm dr\\
			&\lesssim \delta^{\xi\theta}.
		\end{align}
		Hence, it holds uniformly in  $\delta \in (0,1]$ that 
		\begin{align}
			\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}J_7(\varepsilon,t,x)
			&\lesssim \delta^{\xi\theta}+T\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\sup_{y\in{\mathbb X}}
			\left\|\mathbf1_{B_\delta(y)^c}(\cdot)G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2
			\overset{\eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound}}{\le}\delta^{\xi\theta}.
		\end{align}
		Since $\delta>0$ is arbitrary, we see $\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0 }\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} J_7(\varepsilon,t,x) = 0.$
		
		
		Since $u_\varepsilon(0,x)=u(0,x)=f(x)$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$, we have $U_\varepsilon(0)=0$. Collecting all terms, for every $t\in(0,T]$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, we have
		\begin{align}
			&U_\varepsilon(t) \lesssim \int_0^t (t-s)^{-\alpha}U_\varepsilon(s) \mathrm ds +H_\varepsilon(t)+e^{-c_T \frac{t}{\varepsilon}} + \frac{\gamma_\varepsilon}{t^\beta}
		\end{align}
		for some $H_\varepsilon(t)$ with $\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0} \sup_{t \in [0,T]} H_\varepsilon(t) = 0.$
		By the Volterra--Gr\"{o}nwall inequality (see for example, \cite{chu1967gronwall}), we obtain, uniformly in $t\in(0,T]$ and all sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$,
		\begin{align}
			U_\varepsilon(t) &\lesssim H_\varepsilon(t) + e^{-c_T \frac{t}{\varepsilon}} + \frac{\gamma_\varepsilon}{t^\beta} \\
			&\quad\quad\quad+ \int_0^t \left( H_\varepsilon(s) + e^{-c_T \frac{s}{\varepsilon}} + \frac{\gamma_\varepsilon}{s^\beta}\right)(t-s)^{-\alpha} \exp \left( \int_s^t (t-\tau)^{-\alpha} \mathrm d\tau\right) \mathrm ds
		\end{align}
		which converges to zero for each $t>0$ by the dominated convergence theorem. 
		Since $u_\varepsilon(t,x)\ge 0$ almost surely for each $(t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$, we see that $u(t,x)\ge 0$ almost surely for each $(t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$.
		Thus, by separability of $\mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$ and the joint continuity of $u$, we see $u$ is non-negative.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:approximation of coefficients}
		Under the standing assumptions of this section, suppose that $f$ is bounded and \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero} holds.
		Then there exist sequences $(b_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ and  $(\sigma_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ of predictable functions
		on the product space $\Omega \times \mathbb R_+ \times {\mathbb X} \times \mathbb R$ so that 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:FineNConditions}] for each $n\in \mathbb N$, \eqref{eq:LiG}, \eqref{ineq:g_lip}, \eqref{eq:con}, \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero} and \eqref{ineq:non_negative_approx_coef_ineq} hold with $b$ and $\sigma$ being replaced by $b_n$ and $\sigma_n$; and that
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:Approximation}] for every $T>0$ and $\lambda>0$,
			\begin{align}
				\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}
				h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{u^{(n)}(t,x)-u(t,x)}_{L^2_\Omega}=0,
			\end{align}
			where, for each $n\in \mathbb N$, $(u^{(n)}_t)_{t\geq 0}$ is the unique strong solution to the equation
			\begin{align}\label{eq:n}
				\begin{cases}
					\partial_t u^{(n)}_t(x) 
					= \mathcal L^* u^{(n)}_t(x)+b_n\Parentheses*{t,x,u^{(n)}_t(x)} + \sigma_n\Parentheses*{t,x,u^{(n)}_t(x)} \dot{W}_t(x), \quad (t,x)\in \mathbb R_+\times {\mathbb X},
					\\u^{(n)}_0(x) = f(x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
				\end{cases}
			\end{align}
		\end{itemize}
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:non-negative2}
		Suppose that $f$ is non-negative and bounded, and that \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero} holds.
		Then, the unique strong solution $u$ to the SPDE  \eqref{eq:spde} is non-negative.
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Let sequences $(b_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ and  $(\sigma_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ of predictable functions on the product space $\Omega \times \mathbb R_+ \times {\mathbb X} \times \mathbb R$ be given as in Proposition \ref{prop:approximation of coefficients}. 
		For every $n\in \mathbb N$, by Proposition \ref{thm:non_negtive}, \eqref{eq:FineNConditions}, and the condition that $f$ is non-negative, we obtain that $u^{(n)}$, the unique strong solution to the equation \eqref{eq:n}, is non-negative. 
		By \eqref{eq:Approximation}, for each fixed $(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$,
		$u^{(n)}(t,x)\to u(t,x)$ in $L^2_\Omega$. Since every $u^{(n)}(t,x)$ is non-negative,
		the closedness of the non-negative cone in $L^2_\Omega$ implies that $u(t,x)\geq0$ almost surely.
		Thus, by joint continuity and separability of $\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$, $u$ is non-negative.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Define an adapted $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued continuous process $(v_t)_{t \ge 0}$ via
		\begin{align}
			v_t(x) = u_t(x) - u'_t(x),\qquad (t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}.
		\end{align}
		Note that for every $T>0$ and  $(t,x)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$, almost surely,
		\begin{align}
			v_t(x) = P^*_t f_0(x)
			&+ \int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X}  G(s,y;t,x) \widetilde b(s,y, v_s(y)) 
			m(\mathrm d y) \\
			&+ \iint_0^t  G(s,y;t,x) \widetilde \sigma(s,y,v_s(y)) 
			W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy), 
			\label{eq:vtx}
		\end{align}
		where $f_0 := f - f' \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ and for all $(s,y) \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$ and $r\in \mathbb R$ 
		\begin{align}
			(\tilde b(\omega,s,y,r))_{\omega \in \Omega} := 
			\widetilde b(s,y,r)&:= b\Parentheses*{s,y,
				r
				+u_s'(y)} - b(s,y,u'_s(y))\\
			(\tilde \sigma(\omega,s,y,r))_{\omega \in \Omega} := 
			\widetilde \sigma(s,y,
			r)&:= \sigma\Parentheses*{s,y, r+u_s'(y)} - \sigma(s,y,u'_s(y)).
		\end{align}
		Since $u'$ is adapted with continuous
		$\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued paths, it is predictable; hence
		$\widetilde b$ and $\widetilde\sigma$ are predictable. Moreover,
		\eqref{eq:con} and the continuity of $u'_t$ show that they also satisfy
		\eqref{eq:con}.
		We can verify that 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:tildeL}] $\tilde b$ and $\tilde \sigma$ satisfy the linear growth condition \eqref{eq:LiG} and the Lipschitz condition \eqref{ineq:g_lip} with $b$ and $\sigma$ being replaced by $\tilde b$ and $\tilde \sigma$.  
		\end{itemize}
		In fact, for every $T>0$, uniformly for every $\omega\in \Omega\setminus\mathrm{N}_{\ref{c:Lip}}$, $t\in [0,T]$, $r,r'\in \mathbb R$ and $x\in {\mathbb X}$, it holds that
		\begin{align}
			|\tilde b(\omega, t,x,r) |+|\tilde\sigma(\omega, t,x,r)| 
			&= |b\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r+u'(\omega, t, x)} - b(\omega,t,x,u'(\omega, t, x))| 
			\\& \qquad +|\sigma\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r+u'(\omega, t, x)} - \sigma(\omega,t,x,u'(\omega, t, x))| 
			\\& \overset{\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim} |r|, \label{eq:tbtsr}
		\end{align}
		and that
		\begin{align}
			&\quad |\tilde b(\omega, t,x,r) - \tilde b(\omega, t,x,r')|+|\tilde \sigma(\omega, t,x,r)-\tilde \sigma(\omega, t,x,r')| 
			\\&=| b\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r+u'(\omega, t, x)}  - b\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r'+u'(\omega, t, x)}  | 
			\\&\qquad +| \sigma\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r+u'(\omega, t, x)}  - \sigma\Parentheses*{\omega,t,x,r'+u'(\omega, t, x)}  |
			\\&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:g_lip}}{\lesssim} }|r - r'|.
		\end{align}
		In particular,  Theorem \ref{thm:main2}, \eqref{eq:tildeL} and \eqref{eq:vtx} now say that $(v_t)_{t\geq 0}$ is the unique strong solution to the SPDE
		\begin{equation}
			\begin{cases}
				\partial_t v_t(x) 
				= \mathcal L^* v_t(x) + \tilde b\left(t, x, v_t(x)\right) + \tilde\sigma\left(t, x ,v_t(x)\right) \dot W_t(x),
				\quad t \in \mathbb R_+,\, x \in {\mathbb X},
				\\ v_0(x) = f_0(x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
			\end{cases}
		\end{equation}
		
		Since $f_0\in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ is non-negative, we can take a sequence $(f^{(k)}_0)_{k \in \mathbb N}$ of non-negative bounded elements in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ which converges to $f_0$ in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
		From Theorem \ref{thm:main2} and \eqref{eq:tildeL}, for each $k\in \mathbb N$, there exists a unique strong solution $(v^{(k)}_t)_{t\geq 0}$ to the SPDE 
		\begin{equation}
			\begin{cases}
				\partial_t v^{(k)}_t(x) 
				= \mathcal L^* v^{(k)}_t(x) + \tilde b\left(t, x, v^{(k)}_t(x)\right) + \tilde\sigma\left(t, x ,v^{(k)}_t(x)\right) \dot W_t(x),
				\quad t \in \mathbb R_+,\, x \in {\mathbb X},
				\\ v^{(k)}_0(x) = f_0^{(k)}(x), \quad x\in {\mathbb X}.
			\end{cases}
		\end{equation}
		By \eqref{eq:tildeL} and Proposition \ref{prop:continuity_initial}, we know that 
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:v}
			\lim_{k\to \infty}\|v^{(k)}  -v\|_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p}(T)} = 0
		\end{equation}
		for every $p \ge 2$, $\lambda >0$ and $T>0$.
		
		Taking $r=0$ in \eqref{eq:tbtsr}, we obtain
		$\widetilde b(t,x,0)=\widetilde\sigma(t,x,0)=0$, and hence
		\begin{itemize}
			\item[\refstepcounter{equation}(\theequation)\label{eq:tildeLM}] $\tilde b$ and $\tilde \sigma$ satisfy  \eqref{ineq:non_negativity_coef_bound_at_zero} with $b$ and $\sigma$ being replaced by $\tilde b$ and $\tilde \sigma$.  
		\end{itemize}
		Now, for each $k\in \mathbb N$, by Proposition \ref{prop:non-negative2}, \eqref{eq:tildeLM}, and the fact that $f^{(k)}_0\in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ is non-negative and bounded, we have that $v^{(k)}$ is non-negative.  
		Combining this with the separability of ${\mathbb X}$, continuity of $v$  and \eqref{eq:v}, we see that $v=u-u'$ is also non-negative as desired.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:product metric entropy}
		Suppose $(\mathcal X,d_\mathcal X)$ and $\Parentheses*{\mathcal Y,d_\mathcal Y}$ are metric spaces with finite radius such that for some $\beta_\mathcal X,\beta_\mathcal Y >0$, it holds uniformly in $\varepsilon\in (0,1]$ that 
		\begin{align}
			D(\mathcal X,d_\mathcal X; \varepsilon)\lesssim \varepsilon^{-\beta_\mathcal X},\qquad D(\mathcal Y,d_\mathcal Y; \varepsilon)\lesssim \varepsilon^{-\beta_\mathcal Y}.
		\end{align}
		Suppose $\xi_\mathcal X,\xi_\mathcal Y \in (0,1]$ and let $\mathcal S:= \mathcal X \times \mathcal Y$ equipped with the metric $d_\mathcal S\Parentheses*{(x,y),(x',y')}:= d_\mathcal X(x,x')^{\xi_\mathcal X}+d_\mathcal Y(y,y')^{\xi_\mathcal Y}$. Then 
		\begin{align}
			D\Parentheses*{\mathcal S,d_\mathcal S; \varepsilon} \lesssim \varepsilon^{-\Parentheses*{\frac{\beta_\mathcal X}{\xi_\mathcal X}+\frac{\beta_\mathcal Y}{\xi_\mathcal Y}}},\quad \text{ uniformly in }\varepsilon \in (0,1].
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
\label{prop:MLDP}
		Let $T,R >0$, $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$ and assume the following assumptions hold:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item There is some $\beta_0 > 0$ so that $D(B_R(x_0),d; \varepsilon) \lesssim \varepsilon^{-\beta_0}$ uniformly for $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$, where $D$ is defined at the beginning of this subsection.
			\item $g_{t,x}(s,y):=g(t,s,x,y)$ on $ \mathbb{R}_+\times \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X} \times {\mathbb X}$ and $\eta(s,y)$ on $\mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}$ are non-negative deterministic real-valued functions. There are $\xi_1,\xi_2 \in (0,1]$ such that the supremum below is finite, and we set
			\begin{align}\label{ineq:ldp local holder type condition}
				B:= \|Q\|\sup_{t,t' \in [0,T],\, x,x' \in B_R(x_0)} \frac{\int_0^\infty \left\| \left|g_{t,x}(s,\cdot) - g_{t',x'}(s,\cdot)\right| \eta(s,\cdot)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds}{\left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R}\right)^{\xi_1} + \left|\frac{t-t'}{T}\right|^{\xi_2}}.
			\end{align}
			Moreover, suppose there exists $(t_*,x_*)\in[0,T]\times B_R(x_0)$ such that
			\begin{align}
				\int_0^\infty
				\Norm*{g_{t_*,x_*}(s,\cdot)\eta(s,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K}^2
				\mathrm ds<\infty.
			\end{align}
			\item $\sigma$ is a predictable random field on $\mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}$ so that 
			\begin{align}
				\left|\sigma(t,x) \right| \le \eta(t,x),\quad \text{almost surely for all } (t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}.
			\end{align}
		\end{enumerate}
		For $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times B_R(x_0)$, let
		\begin{align}\label{eq:s_conv}
			N(t,x):= \int_0^\infty \int_{\mathbb X} g(t,s,x,y)\sigma(s,y)  W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy).
		\end{align}
		Then there is a jointly continuous modification $\widetilde{N}(t,x)$ of $N(t,x)$ on $[0,T] \times B_R(x_0)$, so that for  $c:=  4\sum_{n= 1}^\infty  \sqrt{n}2^{-n/2} $, the following inequality holds uniformly in $\lambda > 0$, with an implicit constant independent of $T$, $B$ and $\sigma$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:MLDP_local}
			{\mathbb P} \left( \sup_{(t,x),(t',x') \in [0,T]\times B_R(x_0)} \left| \widetilde{N}(t,x) - \widetilde{N}(t',x') \right| > \lambda\right) \lesssim \exp\left(- \frac{\lambda^2}{2c^2B}\right).
		\end{align}
		When $B=0$, the right-hand side of \eqref{ineq:MLDP_local} is understood as $e^{-\infty}=0$.
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:mod_cont_sconv}
		Under the assumptions and notations of Proposition \ref{prop:MLDP}, for all \(t,t'\in[0,T]\), \(x,x'\in B_R(x_0)\), and \(\lambda>0\),
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:s_cont1}
			\mathbb{P} \left(\left|N(t,x) - N(t',x') \right| > \sqrt{B}\lambda \right) &\le 4 \exp\left(\frac{ -\,\lambda^2}{2\left( \left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R} \right)^{\xi_1}+  \left| \frac{t-t'}{T}\right|^{\xi_2}\right)}\right).
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
If $B=0$, then the stochastic-integral isometry, Lemma \ref{lem:HS}, \eqref{ineq:ldp local holder type condition}, and $|\sigma|\le\eta$ imply that $N(t,x)-N(t',x')=0$ in $L^2(\Omega)$ for every $(t,x),(t',x')\in[0,T]\times B_R(x_0)$, so \eqref{ineq:s_cont1} is immediate. Hence, we may assume $B>0$.
		
		For $t,t'\in[0,T]$, $x,x'\in B_R(x_0)$, and $\tau\ge0$, define the difference martingale
		\begin{align*}
			M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\tau)
			:=\int_0^\infty\int_{\mathbb X} \mathbf 1_{[0,\tau]}(s)
			\big(g_{t,x}(s,y)-g_{t',x'}(s,y)\big)\sigma(s,y)
			W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy).
		\end{align*}
		Then $\big(M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\tau)\big)_{\tau\ge0}$ is a continuous martingale. By  Lemma \ref{lem:HS}, it holds almost surely for every $\tau\ge0$ that
		\begin{align}
			\left[ M_{(t,t'),(x,x')} \right]_\tau &= \int_0^\tau \Norm*{Q^{1/2}\left[\left(g_{t,x}(s,\cdot) - g_{t',x'}(s,\cdot)\right) \sigma(s,\cdot)\right]_K}_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}}^2 \mathrm ds\\
			&\,\,\le \|Q\|\int_0^\tau \left\| \left(g_{t,x}(s,\cdot) - g_{t',x'}(s,\cdot)\right)\sigma(s,\cdot)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
			&\,\,\le \|Q\|\int_0^\tau \left\| \left|g_{t,x}(s,\cdot) - g_{t',x'}(s,\cdot)\right| \eta(s,\cdot)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm ds\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:ldp local holder type condition}}{\le} \left( \left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R} \right)^{\xi_1}+  \left| \frac{t-t'}{T}\right|^{\xi_2}\right) B.
		\end{align}
		The last bound shows that this martingale has finite energy and is bounded in $L^2(\Omega)$. Hence, by the martingale convergence theorem and the stochastic-integral isometry,
		\begin{align*}
			M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\tau)
			\longrightarrow M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\infty)
			=N(t,x)-N(t',x')
		\end{align*}
		almost surely and in $L^2(\Omega)$ as $\tau\to\infty$. By the Dambis--Dubins--Schwarz theorem \cite[Chapter~V, Theorem~(1.7)]{revuz1999continuous}, after enlarging the probability space if necessary, there is a Brownian motion $(\mathcal B_r)_{r\ge0}$ such that
		\(M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\tau)=\mathcal B_{[M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}]_\tau}\) for every $\tau\ge0$. Therefore, martingale convergence and the reflection principle give
		\begin{align}
			\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N(t,x)-N(t',x')\right| > \sqrt{B}\lambda\right)
			&=\mathbb{P}\left(\left|M_{(t,t'),(x,x')}(\infty)\right| > \sqrt{B}\lambda\right)\\
			&\le \mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{0 \le r \le  \left( \left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R} \right)^{\xi_1}+  \left| \frac{t-t'}{T}\right|^{\xi_2}\right) B} |\mathcal B_r| > \sqrt{B}\lambda\right)\\
			&\le 4 \exp\left(\frac{ -\,\lambda^2}{2\left( \left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R} \right)^{\xi_1}+  \left| \frac{t-t'}{T}\right|^{\xi_2}\right)}\right).
		\end{align}
		Where we used the standard Gaussian tail bound: if $X$ is a centered Gaussian random variable with variance $\nu>0$, then
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left(|X|> \lambda \right) \le 2 e^{- \frac{\lambda^2}{2\nu}}.
		\end{align}
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
If $B=0$, then the same zero-increment argument shows that $\widetilde N(t,x):=N(t_*,x_*)$ is a jointly continuous modification of $N$ on $[0,T]\times B_R(x_0)$. Consequently, the probability in \eqref{ineq:MLDP_local} is zero for every $\lambda>0$, and the conclusion follows from the convention above. Hence, suppose $B>0$ for the remainder of the proof.
		
		Since $\xi_1,\xi_2 \in (0,1]$, we may define the set ${\mathcal T}:= [0,T] \times B_R(x_0)$ and the new metric $d_{\mathcal T}$ on $\mathcal T$ by 
		\begin{align}
			d_{\mathcal T}((s,x),(s',x')):= \frac{1}{2} \left(\left(\frac{d(x,x')}{2R} \right)^{\xi_1}+ \left|\frac{s-s'}{T} \right|^{\xi_2}\right),\quad (s,x),\, (s',x') \in \mathcal T.
		\end{align}
		Then $({\mathcal T}, d_\mathcal T)$ is a metric space with diameter  at most $1$.   Recall $D(\mathcal T,d_\mathcal T; \varepsilon)$ defined right before Lemma \ref{lem:product metric entropy}, by Lemma \ref{lem:product metric entropy}, we see for  $\beta = \frac{1}{\xi_2} + \frac{\beta_0}{\xi_1}$, there is a positive constant $\refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:lld_tem1} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}}>0$, depending on the covering constant in assumption~(1) and on $R,\beta_0,\xi_1,\xi_2$, so that 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:comb_entro}
			D({\mathcal T},d_{\mathcal T}; \varepsilon) \le \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} \varepsilon^{-\beta},\quad  \text{ uniformly in }0<\varepsilon  <1.
		\end{align}
		Next, we construct a countable dense sequence of points in $\mathcal T$.
		Denote $B_{\mathcal T}(t,r)$ as the open ball in ${\mathcal T}$ centered at $t \in {\mathcal T}$ with radius $r>0$ under the metric $d_{\mathcal T}$.
		Pick any $t_0 \in {\mathcal T}$ and let ${\mathcal T}_0 = \{t_0\}$.
		For $n \ge 1$, we can find  ${\mathcal T}_n := \{x_i\}_{i \in I_n}$ with $|I_n| \le \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} 2^{\beta n}$, where $|I_n|$ denotes the cardinality of $I_n$,  and
		\begin{align}
			{\mathcal T} \subset \bigcup_{i \in I_n} B_{\mathcal T}\left(x_i,\frac{1}{2^{n}}\right).
		\end{align}
		Finally, we let ${\mathcal T}_\infty := \bigcup_{n \ge 0} {\mathcal T}_n$ which is dense in ${\mathcal T}$. 
		
		Now we define the chaining map $\pi_n : {\mathcal T}_{n} \to {\mathcal T}_{n-1}$ for $n \ge 1$ that ‘chains' the points that are close together. By construction, for any $n \ge 1$ and $t \in {\mathcal T}_n$, there is $t_{n-1} \in {\mathcal T}_{n-1}$ so that $d_{\mathcal T}(t,t_{n-1}) \le 2^{-n+1}.$ For each $t \in {\mathcal T}_n$, we choose exactly one such $t_{n-1} \in {\mathcal T}_{n-1}$ and define $\pi_n(t):= t_{n-1}$ and  set $\pi_0(t_0) = t_0$.
		
		For $\rho > 2^{2\beta }$, we define the  events
		$$
		A_\rho := \bigcap_{n \ge 1} \left\{\left|N(t) - N(\pi_n(t)) \right| \le c_n(\rho),\quad \text{ for all } t \in {\mathcal T}_n\right\}
		$$
        where $c_n(\rho) = 2\sqrt{Bn\ln(\rho)}\,2^{-n/2}$.
		Observe that on the event $A_\rho$, for any $n \ge 1$ and $t \in {\mathcal T}_n$, where $\pi_{n+1}\circ \dots \circ \pi_n$ denotes the identity map,
			\begin{align}
				\left|N(t) - N(t_0)\right| &\le \sum_{i = 1}^n \left|N\left(\pi_{i+1}\circ \dots \circ \pi_n(t)\right) - N\left(\pi_i \circ \dots \circ \pi_n(t) \right) \right|\\
				&\le 2\sqrt{B \ln (\rho)} \sum_{i = 1}^\infty  \sqrt{i2^{-i}},
			\end{align}
		and hence  
		\begin{align}\label{ineq: sup distance a rho}
			\sup_{t,t' \in {\mathcal T}_\infty}\left|N(t) - N(t') \right| \le 4\sqrt{B\ln (\rho)} \sum_{n \ge 1}\sqrt{n}2^{-n/2}.
		\end{align}
		Then by Lemma \ref{lem:mod_cont_sconv},
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left( A^c_\rho\right) &\,\,\,\le \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \sum_{t \in {\mathcal T}_n} {\mathbb P} \left(\left|N(t) - N(\pi_n(t)) \right| > c_n(\rho)\right)\\
			&\,\,\,\le 4 \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \sum_{t \in {\mathcal T}_n} \exp\left(-  \frac{c_n(\rho)^2}{4d_{\mathcal T}(t,\pi_n(t)) B} \right)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:comb_entro}}{\le} 4\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}}\sum_{n = 1}^\infty 2^{\beta n} \exp \left(-  n \ln(\rho)/2 \right)\\
			&\,\,\,= 4 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty\left( 2^\beta  \rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)^n\\
			&\,\,\,= \frac{4 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} 2^\beta }{ \rho^{\frac{1}{2}}- 2^\beta }.
		\end{align}
		
		Fix $\lambda>0$ and set $\rho:=\exp(\lambda^2)$. Let $c:=  4\sum_{n = 1}^\infty  \sqrt{n}2^{-n/2} $. If $\rho > 2^{2\beta +2}$, then by \eqref{ineq: sup distance a rho},
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:mldp_last}
			{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{t,t' \in {\mathcal T}_\infty}\left|N(t) - N(t') \right| > c \lambda \sqrt{B} \right) \le {\mathbb P} \left( A_\rho^c\right) \le  \frac{4 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} 2^\beta }{ \rho^{\frac{1}{2}}- 2^\beta } \le \frac{8 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} 2^\beta}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}} = \frac{8 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} 2^{\beta}}{\exp\left(\lambda^2/2\right)}.
		\end{align}
		If $\rho  \in \left[1, 2^{2\beta+2}\right]$, we see $\exp \left(-\frac{\lambda^2}{2} \right)\ge 2^{-\beta -1}$.
		Hence $8 \max\left\{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}},1 \right\}2^\beta \exp\left(- \lambda^2/2 \right) \ge 1$.
		So we get for all $\lambda >0$
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{t,t' \in {\mathcal T}_\infty}\left|N(t) - N(t') \right| > c \lambda \sqrt{B} \right) \le \frac{8\max\left\{ \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:lld_tem1}}} ,1\right\} 2^{\beta}}{\exp\left(\lambda^2 / 2 \right)}.
		\end{align}
			Finally, integrating \eqref{ineq:s_cont1} yields, for every $p\ge1$ and $z,z'\in\mathcal T$,
			\[
				\mathbb E\left[|N(z)-N(z')|^p\right]\le C_p B^{p/2}d_{\mathcal T}(z,z')^{p/2}.
			\]
			Choosing $p>2\beta$, \cite[Theorem 2.3.1]{khoshnevisan2002multiparameter} gives a continuous modification. Since ${\mathcal T}_\infty$ is countable, on a common full-measure event the modification agrees with $N$ on ${\mathcal T}_\infty$, and by density and continuity the preceding dense-set supremum bound transfers to the supremum over all of ${\mathcal T}$, yielding the desired inequality.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:exp_ineq_elementary}
		Suppose $y >0$ and $x \ge 1$, then
		$\exp\left( - yx\right) \le  \frac{\exp\left( - y/2\right)}{xy}$.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:mldp_global}
		Suppose the conditions of Proposition \ref{prop:MLDP} hold, $({\mathbb X},d)$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} and  $h:{\mathbb X} \to [1,\infty)$ is a weight function.
		Let  $\lambda_0>0$ be as in \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and $\lambda > 0$.
			Assume there is $\alpha > 0$ so that for each $T>0$,  the following inequality holds uniformly in $  (t,x) \in (0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$,
			\begin{align}\label{ineq:var_bound_h}
				\sup_{s,s' \in [0,t],\, y,y' \in B_1(x)} \frac{\int_0^\infty \left\| \left|g_{s,y}(\tau,\cdot) - g_{s',y'}(\tau,\cdot)\right| \eta(\tau,\cdot)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm d\tau}{\left(\frac{d(y,y')}{2}\right)^{\xi_1} + \left|\frac{s-s'}{t}\right|^{\xi_2}}
				\lesssim h(x)^{2\lambda - \lambda_0}t^\alpha.
			\end{align}
			Let $N(t,x)$ be as in \eqref{eq:s_conv}, and assume that the continuous modification provided by Proposition \ref{prop:MLDP} satisfies $N(0,\cdot)\equiv0$ almost surely. Then, for each $T>0$, there exists $ \refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:gld_4} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}(T,\lambda) > 0$ so that uniformly for $t \in (0,T]$ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$,
			\begin{align}
				{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,t]\times {\mathbb X}} |N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > \varepsilon \right) \lesssim  \varepsilon^{-2} t^\alpha \exp\left( - \frac{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}}{2t^\alpha}\varepsilon^2\right).
			\end{align}
			For $t=0$, the left hand side is zero, so the estimate is trivial.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We see from condition \eqref{ineq:ref_regular} that for each $\lambda  \ge 0$, there is $C_\lambda>0$ so that for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:w_ball_reg}
			C_\lambda h(x)^{-\lambda} \ge \sup_{z \in B_1(x)} h(z)^{-\lambda} \ge h(y)^{-\lambda},\quad \text{ for all } y \in B_1(x).
		\end{align}
			Let $\lambda, \lambda_0$ be as in \eqref{ineq:var_bound_h}. By Proposition \ref{prop:MLDP}, applied on the time interval $[0,t]$, with $R=1$ and $x_0=x$, there is $\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}(\lambda,T)>0$ so that, uniformly in $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$ and $(t,x) \in (0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$,
			\begin{align}
				{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,y) \in [0,t] \times B_1(x)} |N(s,y)|h(y)^{-\lambda } > \varepsilon \right)&\overset{\eqref{ineq:w_ball_reg}}{\le} {\mathbb P} \left( \sup_{(s,y) \in [0,t] \times B_1(x)} |N(s,y)| \cdot C_\lambda h(x)^{-\lambda}> \varepsilon \right) \\
				&\,\,\,= {\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,y) \in [0,t] \times B_1(x)} |N(s,y)| > C_\lambda^{-1}h(x)^\lambda\varepsilon\right)\\
				&\,\,\,\le {\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{\substack{s,s'\in[0,t]\\y,y'\in B_1(x)}} |N(s,y)-N(s',y')| > C_\lambda^{-1}h(x)^\lambda\varepsilon\right)\\
				&\overset{\eqref{ineq:MLDP_local}}{\lesssim} \exp \left(- \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}} \frac{h(x)^{\lambda_0}}{t^\alpha}\varepsilon^2\right)\\
				& \,\,\,\lesssim \varepsilon^{-2} t^\alpha \exp\left( - \frac{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}}{2t^\alpha}\varepsilon^2\right)h(x)^{-\lambda_0},
			\end{align}
			where the assumption $N(0,y) = 0$ for all $y \in {\mathbb X}$ was used in the third line by taking $(s',y')=(0,y)$; in the last inequality we used Lemma \ref{lem:exp_ineq_elementary}, absorbed constants into the implicit constant, and used the fact $h(x) \ge 1$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$.
		Now let $\{x_n\}_{n \in \mathcal I}\subset {\mathbb X}$ so that  \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} holds.
		Then we see uniformly in $t \in [0,T]$ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in   [0,t]\times {\mathbb X}}|N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > \varepsilon \right) &= {\mathbb P} \left(\bigcup_{n \in \mathcal I} \left\{\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,t] \times B_1(x_n)}|N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} >\varepsilon  \right\}\right)\\
			&\le \sum_{n \in \mathcal I} {\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,t] \times B_1(x_n)}|N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} >\varepsilon  \right)\\
			&\lesssim \varepsilon^{-2} t^\alpha \sum_{n \in \mathcal I} \exp\left( - \frac{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}}{2t^\alpha}\varepsilon^2\right)h(x_n)^{-\lambda_0}\\
			&\lesssim \varepsilon^{-2}t^\alpha \exp \left( - \frac{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_4}}}}{2t^\alpha} \varepsilon^2 \right).
		\end{align}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:m_ldp_g}
		Under the assumptions of Theorem  \ref{thm:main2} with $\lambda_0>0$ as in \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}{, let} $\alpha \in (0,1)$, $\lambda>0$ and $\eta:\mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X} \to \mathbb{R}_+$ be a deterministic measurable function. Suppose there exist $\xi_1,\xi_2\in(0,1]$ so that, for each $T>0$, the following inequality holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in (0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$:
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:var_bound_h_k}
			\sup_{s,s' \in [0,t],\, y,y' \in B_1(x)} \frac{\int_0^{s\vee s'} \left\|\left|G_{s - \tau}(\cdot,y) - G_{s' - \tau}(\cdot,y')\right|\eta(\tau,\cdot)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \mathrm d\tau}{d(y,y')^{\xi_1} + \left|s-s'\right|^{\xi_2}}
			\lesssim h(x)^{2\lambda - \lambda_0}t^{1-\alpha}.
		\end{align}
		 Let $T>0$ and suppose $(f(t,x),\, (t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X})$ is a predictable random field so that   $|f(t,x)| \le \eta(t,x)$ almost surely for all $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$. If we let 
		\begin{align}
			N(t,x):= \int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)f(s,y)W(\mathrm dy\,\mathrm ds),\quad (t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X},
		\end{align}
		then  there exists $ \refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:gld_3} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_3}}}(T,\lambda) > 0$ so that 
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,t]\times {\mathbb X}} |N(s,x)|h(x) ^{-\lambda}> \varepsilon \right) \lesssim \varepsilon^{-2} t^{1-\alpha} \exp\left( - \frac{\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:gld_3}}}}{2t^{1-\alpha}}\varepsilon^2\right),
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $t\in (0,T],\, \varepsilon\in (0,1)$.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:metric_entropy_Lp_chain}
	Let $(\mathcal T, d_{\mathcal T})$ be a complete metric space of diameter at most $2$, suppose  there exists $c_0>0$ so that $D(\mathcal T, d_{\mathcal T}; \varepsilon) \le c_0 \varepsilon^{-\mathfrak{b}}$ for all $\varepsilon \in (0,2)$. Suppose $B>0$, $p >\mathfrak{b}$ and  $(N(t))_{t \in \mathcal T}$ is an almost surely continuous random field on $\mathcal T$ such that
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:metric_entropy_Lp_chain_moment_bound}
		\Norm*{N(t) - N(t')}_{L^p_\Omega} \le B d_{\mathcal T}(t,t'),\quad \text{ for all } t,t' \in \mathcal T.
	\end{align}
	Then there exists $\refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:kol_constant} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}}(p,\mathfrak{b},c_0)>0$ so that for every $t_0 \in \mathcal T$, 
	\begin{align*}
		\Norm*{\sup_{t \in \mathcal T} |N(t) - N(t_0)|}_{L^p_\Omega} \le \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} B.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:unit_scale_stochastic_part_bound}
		Let $u$ be the $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued solution to \eqref{eq:spde_driftless} with $f \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ so that $|f(x)| \le 2$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$. Recall $Z$ as in \eqref{eq:nt}. Then for each $\lambda >0$, there exists $p_0 = p_0(\lambda) \ge 2$ such that for all $p \ge p_0$, there exists $\kappa := \kappa(p,\lambda)>0$ so that for all $N >0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb P \left( \sup_{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times {\mathbb X}} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > N \right) \le S_0 \Parentheses*{\frac{\kappa}{N}}^p.
		\end{align*}
		In particular, the constants depend on the noise coefficient $\sigma$ only through the implicit constant $L>0$ in \eqref{ineq:g_lip} with $T=1$.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Note that since $f$ is continuous and bounded by $2$, we have $|u(0,x)|  = |f(x)| \le 2$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$. Let $\{x_n\}_n \subset {\mathbb X}$ be as in \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and set $\mathcal T_n := [0,1] \times \overline{B_1(x_n)}$ for each $n \in \mathcal I$ and $d_\mathcal T((t,x),(t',x')) := \frac12\left(|t-t'|^{\xi} + d(x,x')^{\xi}\right)$ for all $(t,x),(t',x') \in \mathcal T_n$, where $\xi \in (0,1]$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification}. By examining the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification}, we see for each $\lambda'\in(0,1/2)$, there exists $\refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}(p,\lambda')>0$ so that for all $n \in \mathcal I$ and $(t,x),(t',x') \in \mathcal T_n$, it holds that
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{Z(t,x) - Z(t',x')}_{L^p_\Omega} \le \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}L h(x)^{\lambda'} \left(|t-t'|^{\xi} + d(x,x')^{\xi}\right) = 2\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}} L h(x)^{\lambda'}d_{\mathcal T}((t,x),(t',x')),
		\end{align*}
		where $L$ is as in \eqref{ineq:g_lip} with $T=1$. First let $\lambda\in(0,1/2]$, and take $p$ sufficiently large so that $\lambda':=\lambda-\lambda_0/p\in(0,1/2)$.
		
		Since the implicit constant in \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} is independent of $x \in {\mathbb X}$, we see by Lemma \ref{lem:metric_entropy_Lp_chain} and Proposition \ref{prop:existence of C_tem modification}, by choosing $p > \mathfrak{b}$,  there exists $\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}}(p,\mathfrak{b})>0$ so that for each $n \in \mathcal I$, upon taking $t_0=(0,x_n)$ and using $Z(0,x_n)=0$,
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{\sup_{(t,x) \in \mathcal T_n} |Z(t,x)|}_{L^p_\Omega} \le 2\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}L h(x_n)^{\lambda'}.
		\end{align}
		This implies 
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\sup_{(t,x) \in \mathcal T_n} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda}}_{L^p_\Omega}  \le 2\mathfrak{c}\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}L h(x_n)^{\lambda' - \lambda}= 2\mathfrak{c}\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}L h(x_n)^{-\lambda_0/p}.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, by Markov's inequality, we see for each $N>0$, it holds that 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb P \left(\sup_{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times {\mathbb X}} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > N \right) &\le \mathbb P \left(\bigcup_{n \in \mathcal I} \left\{\sup_{(t,x) \in \mathcal T_n} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > N \right\}\right)\\
			&\le \sum_{n \in \mathcal I} \mathbb P \left(\sup_{(t,x) \in \mathcal T_n} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda} > N \right)\\
			&\le \sum_{n \in \mathcal I} N^{-p} \Norm*{\sup_{(t,x) \in \mathcal T_n} |Z(t,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda}}_{L^p_\Omega}^p\\
			&\le \sum_{n \in \mathcal I} N^{-p} \left(2\mathfrak{c}\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:kol_constant}}} \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:moment_for_stochastic_integral}}}L\right)^p h(x_n)^{-\lambda_0}\\
			&:= S_0 \Parentheses*{\frac{\kappa}{N}}^p,
		\end{align*}
		which is the desired result for $\lambda\in(0,1/2]$. If $\lambda>1/2$, then $h\ge1$ implies $h^{-\lambda}\le h^{-1/2}$, so the estimate follows from the case $\lambda=1/2$.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{prop:one_step_estimates}
		Let $\lambda \in (0,1/2]$, $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$, $M,t>0$ and $0<r_0<R$ be arbitrary but fixed. Let $N_0=N_0(x_0,t,M,r_0,R)$ be as in \eqref{ineq:ip_explicite}, and denote $H:= \sup_{x \in B_R(x_0)}h(x)^{\lambda+\lambda_0/2}$.
		
		Suppose $\sigma(t,x,0)=0$ almost surely for all $(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$, and let $L$ be a Lipschitz constant for $\sigma$ on $[0,1]$.
		Then, for all sufficiently large $p \ge2$, all $N \ge4 \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:semi_group_norm}}}$, and all sufficiently large $Q\ge 1$ (independent of $N$ and $\sigma$), there exists $\kappa := \kappa(p,\lambda) >0$ such that the following holds uniformly for $r\in[r_0,R-M/Q]$: if $u$ is the solution to \eqref{eq:spde_driftless} with initial condition $g \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ satisfying $\mathbf 1_{B_r(x_0)}(x) \le g(x) \le 2$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$, then
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb{P}\left(u(s,x) \ge  \frac{1}{2N_0} \mathbf 1 _{B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)}(x),\,\, \forall(s,x) \in \left[\frac{t}{Q},\frac{2t}{Q}\right]\times {\mathbb X}\right) \ge 1- \varepsilon_{N,Q,p}
		\end{align*}
		where 
		\begin{align*}
			\varepsilon_{N,Q,p} = \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_pre}}}S_0 \Parentheses*{LNHN_0}^2 \Parentheses*{\frac{t}{Q}}^{1-\alpha_0} \exp \left(-\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_rate}}}\Parentheses*{LNHN_0}^{-2} \Parentheses*{\frac{Q}{t}}^{1-\alpha_0} \right) + S_0 \Parentheses*{\frac{\kappa}{N}}^p,
		\end{align*}
		for some $\refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:one_step_pre},\,\refstepcounter{constant}\ensuremath{C_{\theconstant}}\label{c:one_step_rate}>0$, independent of $L,N,Q,N_0$, where $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$ is as in Lemma~\ref{lem:hk_abs}, \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted}.
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
For each $N >0$, let us define 
		\begin{align}
			\tau_N:=\inf\left\{ t>0: \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{u(t,x)}h(x)^{-\lambda} > N \right\},
		\end{align}
		which is a stopping time by the continuity of sample paths. Define for each $N >0$ that 
		\begin{align}
			Z_N(t,x):= \int_0^{t } \int_{\mathbb X} \mathbf 1_{\{s<\tau_N\}}G_{t-s}(y,x) \sigma(s,y,u(s,y)) W(\mathrm dy,\mathrm ds),\quad (t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}.
		\end{align}
		Note that for $s < \tau_N\wedge 1$, by \eqref{ineq:g_lip} and the fact that $\sigma(t,x,0) = 0$ almost surely for all $(t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+ \times {\mathbb X}$, we see
		\begin{align}
			|\sigma(s,y,u(s,y))| \le L |u(s,y)| \le L N h(y)^{\lambda},\quad \text{ for all } (s,y) \in [0,\tau_N\wedge 1)\times {\mathbb X}.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, by Corollary \ref{lem:m_ldp_g} applied to $(LN)^{-1}Z_N$, with $\eta=h^\lambda$, $\lambda$ replaced by $\lambda+\lambda_0/2$, $\alpha=\alpha_0$, $T=1$, and $\xi_1=\xi_2$ equal to the H\"older exponent in Lemma \ref{lem:hk_abs} \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted} (which gives its hypothesis), with $\varepsilon= (2LNN_0H)^{-1}$ (we may take $L\ge1$, so that $\varepsilon<1$), there exist $\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_pre}}},\, \ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_rate}}}>0$ so that for all $N \ge 1$ and sufficiently large $Q \ge 1$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:one_step_noise_LDP}
			&{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}} |Z_N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda- \lambda_0/2} > (2N_0H)^{-1} \right)\\
			 \le &\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_pre}}}S_0 \Parentheses*{LNHN_0}^2 \Parentheses*{\frac{t}{Q}}^{1-\alpha_0}\exp \left(-\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_rate}}}\Parentheses*{LNHN_0}^{-2} \Parentheses*{\frac{Q}{t}}^{1-\alpha_0} \right).
		\end{align}

		Since $g(x) \ge \mathbf{1}_{B_r(x_0)}(x)$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$, we see by \eqref{ineq:ip_explicite} that for all $s \in [t/Q,2t/Q]$ and $x \in B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:in_proof_inst_prop}
			P_s^*g(x) \ge \int_{B_r(x_0)} G_s(y,x) m(\mathrm dy) \ge \frac{1}{N_0}.
		\end{align}
		By weak comparison with the zero solution (Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} \eqref{part:main2 weak comparison}), there is a full-measure event
		\begin{align*}
			\Omega_+:=\{u(s,x)\ge0\text{ for all }(s,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}\}.
		\end{align*}
		On the event $\{\tau_N>2t/Q\}$, one has $u(s,x) = P^*_sg(x) +Z_N(s,x)$ for all $(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}$. Hence, on the event
		$$
		\mathcal A_Q:=\left\{\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}} |Z_N(s,x)|h(x)^{-\lambda- \lambda_0/2} \le  (2N_0H)^{-1}\right\}\bigcap \left\{\tau_N>\frac{2t}{Q}\right\}\bigcap\Omega_+,
		$$
		we see for all  $x \in B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)$, it holds for all $s \in [t/Q,2t/Q]$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:one_step_inclusion_relation}
			 u(s,x) \overset{\eqref{ineq:in_proof_inst_prop}}\ge \frac{1}{N_0} - \Absolute*{Z_N(s,x)} \ge \frac{1}{N_0} - \sup_{x \in B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)}\frac{h(x)^{\lambda+\lambda_0/2}}{2HN_0} \ge \frac{1}{2N_0}.
		\end{align}
		Since $u\ge0$ on $\Omega_+$ we see 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal A_Q \subset \left\{u(s,x) \ge  \frac{1}{2N_0} \mathbf 1 _{B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)}(x),\,\, \forall(s,x) \in \left[\frac{t}{Q},\frac{2t}{Q}\right]\times {\mathbb X} \right\}.
		\end{align*}		
		By the continuity of sample paths, we see 
		$$
    		\left\{\tau_N \le \frac{2t}{Q}\right\}\subset \left\{\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{u(s,x)}h(x)^{-\lambda } \ge N\right\}.
		$$
		Since $g(x) \le 2$ for all $x\in {\mathbb X}$, we see $P^*_sg(x) \le 2\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:semi_group_norm}}}$ for all $s \in [0,1]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$. So if $N \ge 4\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:semi_group_norm}}}$, then for large $Q>0$ so that $2t/Q\le 1$, 
		$$
			\left\{\sup_{(s,x) \in[0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X} }\Absolute*{u(s,x)}h(x)^{-\lambda } \ge N \right\} \subset \left\{\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{Z(s,x)}h(x)^{-\lambda } \ge N/2 \right\}.
		$$
		We see by Lemma \ref{lem:unit_scale_stochastic_part_bound} that, for $p\ge p_0(\lambda)$, with $p_0(\lambda)$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:unit_scale_stochastic_part_bound}, there exists $\kappa = \kappa(p,\lambda) >0$, enlarged by a factor of $2$ if necessary, so that  
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb P\left(\tau_N \le\frac{2t}{Q} \right) \le \mathbb P \left(\sup_{(s,x) \in [0,2t/Q]\times {\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{Z(s,x)}h(x)^{-\lambda} \ge \frac{N}{2}\right)\le S_0 \Parentheses*{\frac{\kappa}{N}}^p.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, by \eqref{ineq:one_step_noise_LDP}, the preceding bound, and the fact that $\mathbb P(\Omega_+)=1$, we see 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb{P}\left(u(s,x) \ge  \frac{1}{2N_0} \mathbf 1 _{B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)}(x)\, \forall(s,x) \in \left[\frac{t}{Q},\frac{2t}{Q}\right]\times {\mathbb X}\right)
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:one_step_inclusion_relation}}{\ge}\mathbb P \left( \mathcal A_Q\right)\\
			&=1- \mathbb P \left(\mathcal A_Q ^c\right)\ge 1 - \varepsilon_{N,Q,p}
		\end{align*}
		which is the desired inequality.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
To prove Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} \eqref{part:main2 strong comparison}, let $u$ and $u'$ be as in Theorem~\ref{thm:comparison}~\eqref{part:main2 strong comparison}, and denote $v_t(x) : =u_t(x) - u'_t(x)$ and $f_0(x):= f(x) - f'(x)$ for $t > 0$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$. Then $v$ solves the integral equation
		\begin{align}
			v_t(x) = P_t^*f_0(x) + &\int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\widetilde{b}(s,y,v_s(y)) m(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}s\\
			&+\int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\widetilde{\sigma}(s,y,v_s(y))W(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s),
		\end{align}
		for $t>0$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$, where for $r\in \mathbb{R}$,
		\begin{align}
			\widetilde{b}(t,x,r) &:= b(t,x,r +u'_t(x)) - b(t,x,u'_t(x))\\ \widetilde{\sigma}(t,x,r) &:= \sigma(t,x,r +u'_t(x)) - \sigma(t,x,u'_t(x)). \label{eq:tilde_coef_diff}
		\end{align}
		By \eqref{ineq:g_lip}, $\widetilde b$ and $\widetilde\sigma$ satisfy the same local Lipschitz condition, and $\widetilde b(t,x,0)=\widetilde\sigma(t,x,0)=0$ almost surely for all $(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$.

		By Theorem~\ref{thm:comparison}~\eqref{part:main2 weak comparison} and the argument of \cite[pp.~427--428, equations~(4.17)--(4.22)]{MR1271224}, it is enough to consider \eqref{eq:spde_driftless} and assume that, for some $x_0\in{\mathbb X}$ and $\beta,a>0$, the initial value $f$ satisfies
		\begin{align}
			2\beta\ge f(x)\ge\beta\mathbf1_{B_a(x_0)}(x),\qquad x\in{\mathbb X}.
		\end{align}
		
		Let $t,M>0$ be arbitrary but fixed, and let $L>0$ be a Lipschitz constant for $\sigma$ in \eqref{ineq:g_lip} on $[0,t+1]$. Fix $\lambda\in(0,1/2]$ and any $\gamma\in(0,(1-\alpha_0)/2)$, where $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$ is as in Lemma~\ref{lem:hk_abs}, \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted}.
		By \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation}, we see there exists $N_0 = N_0(x_0,t,M,a/2,a+M) \ge 1$ so that for all sufficiently large $Q\ge 1$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\inf_{s \in [t/Q,2t/Q]} \inf_{r \in [a/2,a+M]} \inf_{x \in B_{r+M/Q}(x_0)}\int_{B_r(x_0)} G_s(y,x)m(\mathrm dy) \ge \frac{1}{N_0}.
		\end{align*}
		Let $H:=\sup_{B_{a+M}(x_0)}h(x)^{\lambda+\lambda_0/2}$ and let $p \ge 2$ be sufficiently large as in Proposition \ref{prop:one_step_estimates} and  assume in addition that $p > 1/\gamma$. Define
		\begin{align*}
			\varepsilon_{Q}: =\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_pre}}}S_0 \Parentheses*{LQ^\gamma HN_0}^2 \Parentheses*{\frac{t}{Q}}^{1-\alpha_0}\exp \left(-\ensuremath{C_{\ref{c:one_step_rate}}}\Parentheses*{LQ^\gamma HN_0}^{-2} \Parentheses*{\frac{Q}{t}}^{1-\alpha_0} \right) + S_0 \Parentheses*{\frac{\kappa}{Q^\gamma}}^p.
		\end{align*}
		We note that 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:strict_pos_Qepsilon_to_0}
			\lim_{Q \to \infty}Q \varepsilon_Q = 0. 
		\end{align}
		Indeed, since $\gamma < \frac{1-\alpha_0}{2}$ and $p > 1/\gamma$, we see for some $C>0$, it holds uniformly in $Q\ge 1$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			Q \varepsilon_Q \lesssim Q^{\alpha_0+2\gamma} \exp \left(-C Q^{1-\alpha_0 - 2\gamma}\right) +Q^{-p\gamma +1}
		\end{align*}
		which converges to $0$ as $Q \to \infty$, which verifies \eqref{ineq:strict_pos_Qepsilon_to_0}.

		For $Q\in \mathbb{N}$ sufficiently large, set $s_j=jt/Q$ for $j=0,\dots,Q+1$, and, for $j=0,\dots,Q$, set
		\begin{align*}
			\zeta_j:=\frac{jM}{2Q^2},\qquad B_j:=B_{a+\frac{jM}{Q}-\zeta_j}(x_0),\qquad \theta_j:=\beta(2N_0)^{-j}.
		\end{align*}
		For $k=1,\dots,Q$, set $B_k':=B_{a+\frac{(k-1)M}{Q}-\zeta_k}(x_0)$ and define
		\begin{gather}\label{def:strict_pos_set_A_J_def}
			\mathcal A_k:=\Braces*{u(s,x)\ge\theta_k\mathbf 1_{B_k}(x)\ \forall(s,x)\in\Brackets*{s_k,s_{k+1}}\times{\mathbb X}},\\
			J_j:=\Braces*{u(s_j,\cdot)\ge\theta_j\mathbf 1_{B_j}(\cdot)},\qquad j=0,\dots,Q.
		\end{gather}
		Since $\theta_0=\beta$, $B_0=B_a(x_0)$, and $f\ge\beta\mathbf 1_{B_a(x_0)}$, we have $J_0=\Omega$.

		For each sufficiently large $Q\in \mathbb{N}$ and $k = 1,\dots, Q$, let $\varphi_{k-1} :{\mathbb X} \to [0,1]$ be a continuous function so that $\varphi_{k-1}\equiv 1$ on the closure of $B_k'$ and $\varphi_{k-1}\equiv 0$ outside of $B_{k-1}$. 

		Define for $k = 1,\dots, Q$ that $\sigma_{k}(s,x,r):=\theta_{k-1}^{-1}\sigma(s_{k-1}+s,x,\theta_{k-1}r)$ for $r \in \mathbb{R}$, which is predictable with respect to the filtration $(\mathcal F_{s_{k-1}+s})_{s \ge 0}$ and satisfies \eqref{ineq:g_lip} on $[0,1]$ with the same Lipschitz constant $L>0$ (since $s_{k-1}+1\le t+1$) and $\sigma_k(t,x,0) = 0$ almost surely for all $(t,x) \in \mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}$.
		
		Therefore, by Theorem \ref{thm:main2}, there exists a unique continuous $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued solution to the integral equation with the shifted noise,
		\begin{align*}
			w_k(t,x) = P^*_t\varphi_{k-1}(x)+ \int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\sigma_{k}(s,y,w_k(s,y))W\Parentheses*{s_{k-1}+\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy}.
		\end{align*}
		Denote 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathcal B_k:=\left\{w_k(s,x) \ge \frac{1}{2N_0}\mathbf 1_{B_k}(x),\, \forall (s,x) \in \left[\frac{t}{Q},\frac{2t}{Q} \right]\times {\mathbb X}\right\}.
		\end{align*}
		For all large $Q$, the radius of $B_k'$ lies in $[a/2,a+M-M/Q]$ and the radius of $B_k'$ plus $M/Q$ is exactly the radius of $B_k$. Hence Proposition \ref{prop:one_step_estimates}, applied with $r$ equal to the radius of $B_k'$ and $N=Q^\gamma$, gives
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:strict_pos_one_step_estimates}
			\mathbb P \left(\mathcal B_k\right) \ge 1 - \varepsilon_{Q}.
		\end{align}
		We claim that 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:strict_pos_main_step_claim}
			\mathcal A_{k}^c \cap J_{k-1} \subset \mathcal B_k^c\quad \text{and hence }\mathbb{P}\left( \mathcal A_{k}^c \cap J_{k-1} \right) \le \varepsilon_Q.
		\end{align}
		Now define $v_k(t,x):= \theta_{k-1}w_k(t,x)$ for $(t,x) \in \mathbb R_+\times {\mathbb X}$. Then $v_k$ is the solution to the following shifted integral equation:
		\begin{align*}
			v_k(t,x)= P^*_t\Parentheses*{\theta_{k-1}\varphi_{k-1}}(x) + \int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\sigma(s_{k-1}+s,y,v_k(s,y))W\Parentheses*{s_{k-1}+\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy}.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, on the event $\mathcal B_k$, we have 
		\begin{align*}
			v_k(s,x) \ge \theta_{k-1} (2N_0)^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{B_k}(x) = \theta_{k}\mathbf{1}_{B_k}(x),\qquad \text{ for all }(s,x)\in \left[\frac{t}{Q},\frac{2t}{Q} \right]\times {\mathbb X}.
		\end{align*}

		Now we set $\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}:= \mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}} \theta_{k-1}\varphi_{k-1}$, then $\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}$ is $\mathcal F_{s_{k-1}}$ measurable since $J_{k-1}\in \mathcal F_{s_{k-1}}$ and it belongs to $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$. We see 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}}v_k(t,x) &= P_t^*\widetilde{\varphi}_k(x)+ \int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\mathbf{1}_{J_{k -1}}\sigma(s_{k-1}+s, y, v_k(s,y))W\Parentheses*{s_{k-1}+\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy}\\
			&=P_t^*\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(x) + \int_0^t\int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s}(y,x)\sigma(s_{k-1}+s,y,\mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}}v_k(s,y))W\Parentheses*{s_{k-1}+\mathrm ds, \mathrm dy},
		\end{align*}
		where we used the fact that $\sigma(t,x,0) = 0$ in the last line. In other words, $\mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}}v_k$ is a solution to the shifted equation with initial condition $\widetilde{\varphi}_k$.  

		By Corollary \ref{cor:MfT}, \eqref{ineq:bcc D pair explicit}, and \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}, Lemma \ref{thm:stochastic_fubini} applies. Thus, splitting the mild equation at $s_{k-1}$ and using the semigroup property, we obtain, for $s\ge0$,
		\begin{align*}
			u(s_{k-1}+s,x)
			&=P_{s_{k-1}+s}^*f(x)+\int_0^{s_{k-1}}\int_{\mathbb X} G_{s_{k-1}+s-r}(y,x)\sigma(r,y,u_r(y))W(\mathrm dr,\mathrm dy)\\
			&\quad+\int_{s_{k-1}}^{s_{k-1}+s}\int_{\mathbb X} G_{s_{k-1}+s-r}(y,x)\sigma(r,y,u_r(y))W(\mathrm dr,\mathrm dy)\\
			&=P_s^*\big(u(s_{k-1},\cdot)\big)(x)+\int_0^s\int_{\mathbb X} G_{s-r}(y,x)\sigma(s_{k-1}+r,y,u_{s_{k-1}+r}(y))W\Parentheses*{s_{k-1}+\mathrm dr,\mathrm dy}.
		\end{align*}
		Thus $(u(s_{k-1}+s,\cdot))_{s\ge0}$ solves the same shifted equation with initial condition $u(s_{k-1},\cdot)\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem},+}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ almost surely. It holds for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			u(s_{k-1},x) \ge \mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}} u(s_{k-1},x) \ge  \mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}}\theta_{k-1} \mathbf{1}_{B_{k-1}}(x) \ge \widetilde{\varphi}_k(x),
		\end{align*}
		where we used the fact that $\widetilde \varphi_{k}$ is supported on $B_{k-1}$. 
		        Set $\mathcal G_s:=\mathcal F_{s_{k-1}+s}$ for $s\ge0$. The shifted noise is a (generalized) Wiener process relative to $(\mathcal G_s)_{s\ge0}$, and the shifted coefficient satisfies the same assumptions. The same contraction, nonnegativity, and truncation arguments extend Theorem~\ref{thm:main2} and Theorem~\ref{thm:comparison}~\eqref{part:main2 weak comparison} to $\mathcal G_0$-measurable $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$-valued initial conditions $g$ satisfying
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:random_initial_moment}
			\sup_{x\in{\mathbb X}}h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{g(x)}_{L^p_\Omega}<\infty,
			\qquad \lambda\in(0,1],\quad p\ge2,
		\end{equation}
		with the initial ordering understood in the almost sure sense. The bound \eqref{ineq:random_initial_moment} holds for $u(s_{k-1},\cdot)$ by Corollary~\ref{cor:moment}, and for $\widetilde\varphi_k$ since $0\le\widetilde\varphi_k\le\theta_{k-1}$.
                
        		Therefore, by the comparison principle (Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} \eqref{part:main2 weak comparison}), we see
		\begin{align*}
			u(s_{k-1}+s,x) \ge \mathbf{1}_{J_{k-1}}v_k(s,x),\qquad \forall (s,x) \in\mathbb{R}_+\times {\mathbb X}. 
		\end{align*}
		This implies, on $J_{k-1}$, we have $\mathcal A^c_k \subset \mathcal B_k^c$, combined with \eqref{ineq:strict_pos_one_step_estimates}, we see \eqref{ineq:strict_pos_main_step_claim} holds. 

		Observe that for sufficiently large $Q\in \mathbb{N}$, 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:strict_pos_last_inclusion}
			\cap_{k = 1}^Q\mathcal A_k \subset \left\{ u_s(x)>0\ \text{on }\Brackets*{\tfrac t2,t}\times B_{a+\tfrac M4}(x_0)\right\}.
		\end{align}
		Indeed, on $\cap_{k = 1}^Q\mathcal A_k$, for each $s \in [t/2,t]$, there is some $Q/2 -1\le k < Q$ so that $s \in [s_{k},s_{k+1}]$, and hence for sufficiently large $Q \in \mathbb{N}$,
		\begin{align*}
			B_{a+ \frac{M}{4}}(x_0) \subset B_{a+ \frac{kM}{Q} - \frac{kM}{2Q^2}}(x_0) \subset B_k,\qquad \frac{Q}{2}-1 \le k <Q. 
		\end{align*}
		Then $\mathcal A_k$ gives $u(s,x) \ge\theta_k>0$ on $B_k$, which implies \eqref{ineq:strict_pos_last_inclusion}.

		Since $J_0=\Omega$ and, for $k\ge2$, $\mathcal A_{k-1} \subset J_{k-1}$ and $\bigcap_{j<k}\mathcal A_j \subset \mathcal A_{k-1}$, we see for all sufficiently large $Q \in \mathbb{N}$,
		\begin{align*}
			1-{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\bigcap_{k=1}^Q\mathcal A_k}
			\ =\ {\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\bigcup_{k=1}^{Q}\mathcal A_k^c}
			\ &=\ \sum_{k=1}^{Q}{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\mathcal A_k^c\cap\bigcap_{j<k}\mathcal A_j}\\
			\ &\le\ \sum_{k=1}^{Q}{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\mathcal A_k^c\cap J_{k-1}}
			\ \overset{\eqref{ineq:strict_pos_main_step_claim}}{\le }\ \sum_{k=1}^{Q}{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\mathcal B_k^c}
			\ \le\ Q\,\varepsilon_Q.
		\end{align*}
		whence
		\[
			{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{u_s(x)>0\ \text{on }\Brackets*{\tfrac t2,t}\times B_{a+\tfrac M4}(x_0)}\ \overset{\eqref{ineq:strict_pos_last_inclusion}}{\ge}\ \limsup_{Q \uparrow \infty} {\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{\bigcap_{k=1}^Q\mathcal A_k}\ \ge\ 1-\lim_{Q \uparrow \infty}Q\,\varepsilon_Q \overset{\eqref{ineq:strict_pos_Qepsilon_to_0}}{=} 1.
		\]
		Apply this with $t_n=2^n$, $n\in\mathbb Z$, and $M_m=m$, $m\in\mathbb N$, and intersect the resulting countable family of probability-one events. Since the union of the intervals $[2^{n-1},2^n]$ covers $(0,\infty)$ for $n \in \mathbb{Z}$ and the balls $B_{a+m/4}(x_0)$ exhaust ${\mathbb X}$ for $m \in \mathbb{N}$, we obtain
		\begin{align*}
			{\mathbb P}\Parentheses*{u(t,x)>0\ \text{for all }(t,x) \in (0,\infty) \times {\mathbb X} } = 1.
		\end{align*}
		This proves Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} \eqref{part:main2 strong comparison}.

		Finally, Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} \eqref{part:main2 strictly positivity} follows by applying \eqref{part:main2 strong comparison} to the solutions with initial conditions $f$ and $0$, since the latter is non-negative by Proposition \ref{prop:non-negative2} under \eqref{cond:pos_zero}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise}
		Let $V,\Psi : [0,\infty) \to [0,\infty)$ be doubling functions satisfying 
		\[
		\frac{R^2}{r^2} \lesssim \frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)} \lesssim \frac{R\,V(R)}{r\,V(r)},\qquad\text{uniformly in } 0<r\le R.
		\]
		Then $\Psi$ is a scale function.
		Let $({\mathbb X},d,m,\mathcal E,\mathcal F)$ be an MMD with heat kernel satisfying $\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)$ and having volume growth profile $V$. Fix an arbitrary $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$ and define weight function
		\[
		h(x) := \exp \Parentheses*{d(x_0,x)},\qquad x \in {\mathbb X}.
		\]
		Then the following hold:
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item \label{it_prop_subg_no_noise:1} The MMS $({\mathbb X},d,m)$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:entropy bound}, and the weight function $h$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and \eqref{ineq:ref_regular}. 
			\item \label{it_prop_subg_no_noise:2} Condition \eqref{cond:ctem continuous} and the first inequality in condition \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} hold. 
			\item \label{it_prop_subg_no_noise:3} Condition \eqref{cond:time_holder} holds. 
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- prop-13: body_md -->
\label{prop:sub_g_condition_noise}
		Let $V,\Psi$, and $({\mathbb X},d,m, \mathcal E, \mathcal F)$ and the function $h$ on ${\mathbb X}$ be as in Proposition \ref{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise}.  Suppose $\alpha_0 \in (0,1)$. 
		Then the following holds:  
		\begin{enumerate}
			\item \label{it_prop_subg_noise:1} If $\Parentheses*{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(t)}}^{-1} \lesssim t^{-\alpha_0}$ uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$, then the second inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} and \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound} hold with $\alpha$ being replaced by $\alpha_0$ and the underlying measure $K$ in the definition of $\mathcal H_K$ being replaced by $K_0$.
			\item \label{it_prop_subg_noise:2} If $\gamma\neq 0$ and it holds uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$ that 
			\begin{align*}
				\int_0^\infty \frac{s^\gamma}{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(t+s)}} \frac{\mathrm ds}{s} \lesssim t^{-\alpha_0},
			\end{align*}
			then the second inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} and \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound} hold with $\alpha$ being replaced by $\alpha_0$, and the underlying measure $K$ in the definition of $\mathcal H_K$ being replaced by $K_\gamma$. 
		\end{enumerate}
<!-- end prop-13 -->
<!-- prop-14: body_md -->
\label{prop:sub_g_strict_pos_condition}
		Let $V,\Psi$ and $({\mathbb X},d,m,\mathcal E,\mathcal F)$ be as in Proposition \ref{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise}. There exists a metric $\rho$ that is bi-Lipschitz equivalent to $d$ on ${\mathbb X}$ so that condition \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation} holds under $\rho$.
<!-- end prop-14 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{rmk:bi_lip_metric_remark}
		Since $\rho$ and $d$ are bi-Lipschitz equivalent, the heat kernel has similar $\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)$ under the metric $\rho$ and the conclusions of Propositions \ref{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise} and \ref{prop:sub_g_condition_noise} hold under the metric $\rho$ as well.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
From Propositions \ref{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise}--\ref{prop:sub_g_condition_noise} and the assumptions, we see all conditions of Theorem \ref{thm:main2} hold. Hence the well-posedness part of the result holds. In addition, by Proposition \ref{prop:sub_g_strict_pos_condition} and Remark \ref{rmk:bi_lip_metric_remark}, we see all conditions of Theorem \ref{thm:comparison} hold, which implies the strict positivity property.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem_CM:doubling_function_polynomial_bound}
		Suppose $V:\mathbb{R}_+\to \mathbb{R}_+$ is a doubling function as in \eqref{ineq:doubling_function}. Then there exists a constant $D>1$ such that
		\begin{align*}
			\frac{V(R)}{V(r)} \le D \Parentheses*{\frac{R}{r}}^{\log_2(D)},\qquad \text{ for }0<r\le R.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let $0<r\le R$, choose $n \in \mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}$ so that $2^{n-1} < \frac{R}{r}\le 2^n$, then by \eqref{ineq:doubling_function}, there exists $D>1$ so that 
		\begin{align*}
			V(R) \le V(2^n r) \le D^n V(r).
		\end{align*}
		Since $D^n = 2^{n \log_2(D)}$, we get for $0<r\le R$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\frac{V(R)}{V(r)} \le D^n \le D\Parentheses*{\frac{R}{r}}^{\log_2(D)}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound}
		Let $V,\Psi$ be as in Remark \ref{rem:sub-g-general}. Then there exists $\beta\ge 1$ so that 
		\begin{align*}
			\frac{V(R)}{V(r)} \lesssim \Parentheses*{\frac{R}{r}}^\beta,\qquad \text{ uniformly in }0<r\le R.
		\end{align*}
		In addition, for any $T>0$, it holds uniformly in $t \in (0,T]$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\sqrt{t} \lesssim \Psi^{-1}(t) \lesssim t^{\frac{1}{\beta+1}}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
By \eqref{ineq:sub_g_escape_vol_constrain} and Lemma \ref{lem_CM:doubling_function_polynomial_bound}, we see there exist $C,C'>1$ and $\beta>0$ so that 
		\begin{align*}
			C^{-1} \Parentheses*{\frac{R}{r}}^2 \le \frac{\Psi(R)}{\Psi(r)} \le C'\frac{RV(R)}{rV(r)} \le C\Parentheses*{\frac{R}{r}}^{\beta+1},\qquad \text{for }0<r\le R. 
		\end{align*}
		This implies $\beta \ge 1$. It also implies that for $0<t\le T$, 
		\begin{align*}
			C^{-1} \Parentheses*{\frac{T}{t}}^{\frac{1}{\beta+1}} \le \frac{\Psi^{-1}(T)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} \le C \Parentheses*{\frac{T}{t}}^{1/2}.
		\end{align*}
		We may now fix any $T>0$ and multiply this inequality by $\Psi^{-1}(t)$ to see the desired result.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
For \eqref{it_prop_subg_no_noise:1}, it is known that when a metric measure space $({\mathbb X},d,m)$ has volume growth profile $V$ for a doubling function $V$, then by \eqref{ineq_CM:volume_all_scales} a stronger version of \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} holds (cf. \cite[pp.~167--168]{kaenmaki2017measures}): there exists $b >0$ so that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq_CM:entropy_grwoth_sub_g_case}
			D_r\Parentheses*{B_R(x)} \lesssim \Parentheses*{\frac{r}{R}}^{-b},\qquad \text{uniformly in }x \in {\mathbb X}, \, 0<r<R.
		\end{align}
		It can be easily checked that condition \eqref{ineq:ref_regular} holds for exponential functions.
		For \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}, we see from \eqref{ineq_CM:entropy_grwoth_sub_g_case} by setting $r = 1$ that for each $N \in \mathbb{N}$, the ball $B_N(x_0)$, hence $A_N:= B_{N}(x_0) \backslash B_{N-1}(x_0)$, can be covered by $\lfloor CN^b\rfloor$ unit balls, where $C\ge1$ does not depend on $N$, denote their centers as $\{x^N_i\}_{i = 1}^{\lfloor CN^b\rfloor}$.
		Now define $I_N:=\{x_i^N: A_N \cap B_1(x_i^N) \neq \emptyset\}$.
		Clearly,
		\begin{align*}
			A_N \subset\bigcup_{x_i \in I_N}B_1(x_i),\qquad \Absolute*{I_N} \lesssim N^b \text{ uniformly in }N.
		\end{align*}
		Let $I = \cup_{N =1}^\infty I_N$, then 
		\begin{align*}
			{\mathbb X} = \bigcup_{N \in \mathbb{N}}A_N \subset \bigcup_{N \in \mathbb{N}}\bigcup_{x_i \in I_N}B_1(x_i) = \bigcup_{x_i \in I} B_1(x_i).
		\end{align*}
		Now consider 
		\begin{align*}
			\sum_{x_i \in I} \exp \Parentheses*{-d(x_0,x_i)} = \sum_{N = 1}^\infty \sum_{x_i \in I_N} \exp \Parentheses*{-d(x_0,x_i)}\lesssim\sum_{N = 1}^\infty e^{-(N-2)}N^b < \infty,
		\end{align*}
		which gives \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}. 
		
		For \eqref{it_prop_subg_no_noise:2}, we see from \cite[Lemma 4.8 \& Theorem 3.1]{barlow2012equivalence}, there exist $c_1,c_2>0$ so that for each $k \in \mathbb{N}$, 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq_CM:sub_g_exponential_moment_bound}
			\int_{\mathbb X} \exp \left(c_1 \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t/k)} \right)G_t(x,y) m(\mathrm dy) \le c_2^k,\qquad\text{ for all }x \in {\mathbb X},\, t>0. 
		\end{align}
		Note that for each $\lambda >0$, we may select a sufficiently large $k_\lambda  \ge 1$ so that $\frac{c_1}{\Psi^{-1}(t/k_\lambda)} \ge \lambda$ for all $t \in (0,1]$. Hence, consider for $t \in (0,1]$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			e^{-\lambda d(x_0,x)}\int_{\mathbb X} e^{\lambda d(x_0,y)}G_t(x,y) m(\mathrm dy)&\le \int_{\mathbb X} e^{\lambda d(x,y)}G_t(x,y)m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\le \int_{\mathbb X} \exp \left(c_1 \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t/k_\lambda)} \right) G_t(x,y) m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\le c_2^{k_\lambda},
		\end{align*}
		which implies the first inequality of \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}. 
		
		It remains to show \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}, that is, for any $f \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$, 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq_CM:ctemp_continuous_subg}
			\lim_{t \downarrow 0}\Norm*{P_tf - f}_{(\lambda)}	 = 0,\qquad \text{ for all } \lambda > 0. 
		\end{align}
		Let us denote $r(x):=d(x_0,x)$ for $x \in {\mathbb X}$, then $e^{-\lambda r(x)} f(x) \in \mathcal C^0_{\mathbb X}$ for any $\lambda >0$, hence uniformly continuous on ${\mathbb X}$. Next, we note that by \eqref{ineq_CM:volume_all_scales} and \cite[Theorem 3.2]{lierl2015scale}, we have $\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y)m(\mathrm dy) = 1$ for all $x \in {\mathbb X} $ and $t>0$.
		
		Let $\lambda >0$ be arbitrary but fixed,  denote $g(x):= e^{-\lambda r(x)/2}f(x)$ and consider
		\begin{align*}
			P_tf(x) - f(x)&=\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{g(y)e^{\lambda r(y)/2} - g(x)e^{\lambda r(x)/2}}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&=\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{\bigl(g(y)-g(x)\bigr)e^{\lambda r(y)/2}
				+
				g(x)\Parentheses*{e^{\lambda r(y)/2}-e^{\lambda r(x)/2}}}m(\mathrm dy).
		\end{align*}
		Hence 
		\begin{align*}
			e^{-\lambda r(x)}\Absolute*{P_tf(x) - f(x)} \le A_t(x)+B_t(x)
		\end{align*}
		where 
		\begin{align*}
			A_t(x) &:= \int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y)e^{-\lambda r(x)}\Absolute*{g(y) - g(x)}e^{\lambda r(y)/2}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			B_t(x) &:= \int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) e^{-\lambda r(x)}\Absolute*{g(x)}\, \Absolute*{e^{\lambda r(y) / 2} - e^{\lambda r(x) /2}} m(\mathrm dy).
		\end{align*}
		We will consider $B_t(x)$ first by noting 
		\begin{align*}
			e^{-\lambda r(x)}\Absolute*{e^{\lambda r(y)/2} - e^{\lambda r(x)/2}} = e^{-\lambda r(x)/2}\Absolute*{e^{\lambda (r(y) - r(x))/2} -1} \le e^{-\lambda r(x)/2}\Absolute*{e^{\lambda d(x,y)/2}-1}
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, 
		\begin{align*}
			B_t(x) &\le \Absolute*{g(x)}e^{-\lambda r(x)/2}\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{e^{\lambda d(x,y)/2}-1}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\mathrel{\le} \Norm*{g}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}}\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{e^{\lambda d(x,y)/2}-1}m(\mathrm dy)
		\end{align*}
		Let $c_1 > 0$ be as in \eqref{ineq_CM:sub_g_exponential_moment_bound} and denote $\eta_t:= c_1/ \Psi^{-1}(t)$.
		Let $\varepsilon >0$ be arbitrary but fixed, then choose $\delta >0$ so that $e^{\lambda \delta/2} - 1 < \varepsilon $.
		Consider
		\begin{align*}
			\int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{e^{\lambda d(x,y) /2} -1}m(\mathrm dy)  &= \Parentheses*{\int_{d(x,y) \mathrel{<} \delta} + \int_{d(x,y) \ge \delta}} G_t(x,y) \Parentheses*{e^{\lambda d(x,y) /2} -1}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&:=I_1(t,x) +I_2(t,x). 
		\end{align*}
		Note that $I_1(t,x) < \varepsilon $ for all $t>0$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$.
		Now, let $t_0>0$ be sufficiently small so that $\eta_t > \lambda$ for $0< t< t_0$.
		Hence for $d(x,y) \mathrel{\ge} \delta$ and $0<t<t_0$, we have
		\begin{align*}
			e^{\lambda d(x,y)/2} = e^{-(\eta_t -\lambda/2)\delta}e^{(\eta_t - \lambda/2) \delta}e^{\lambda d(x,y)/2} \mathrel{\le} e^{-(\eta_t - \lambda/2)\delta}e^{\eta_t d(x,y)}.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, for all $0<t<t_0$ 
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}I_2(t,x) &\le e^{-(\eta_t - \lambda/2)\delta} \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \int_{\mathbb X} G_t(x,y) e^{\eta_t d(x,y)} m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq_CM:sub_g_exponential_moment_bound}}{\le} e^{-(\eta_t-\lambda/2)\delta}c_2.
		\end{align*}
		Hence 
		\begin{align*}
			\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{t \downarrow 0} \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}B_t(x) \le \Norm*{g}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}}\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{t \downarrow 0} \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \Parentheses*{I_1(t,x) +I_2(t,x)} \le \Norm*{g}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}}\varepsilon,
		\end{align*}
		since $\varepsilon >0$ can be arbitrarily small, we see the limit is zero.
		
		For ${A}_t(x)$, for any $\varepsilon > 0$,  we can choose sufficiently small $\delta \mathrel{\in} (0,1]$ so that $\Absolute*{g(x) - g(y)} < \varepsilon$ if $d(x,y) < \delta$. Hence, 
		\begin{align*}
			A_t(x) &\le \Parentheses*{\int_{d(x,y) < \delta} +\int_{d(x,y) \ge \delta}}G_t(x,y) \Absolute*{g(y)-g(x)}e^{-\lambda r(x) +\lambda r(y)}{m(\mathrm dy)}\\
			&\le \Parentheses*{\int_{d(x,y) < \delta} +\int_{d(x,y) \ge \delta}}G_t(x,y) \Absolute*{g(y)-g(x)}e^{\lambda d(x,y)}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&:= I_3(t,x)+I_4(t,x).
		\end{align*}
		Note that $I_3(t,x) \le e^{\lambda}\varepsilon$ for all $t>0,\, x \in {\mathbb X}$. In addition, since $\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{g(x)} < \infty$, it follows from the same argument as in $I_2$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\lim_{t \downarrow 0} \sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}} I_4(t,x) = 0. 
		\end{align*}
		Hence, $\mathop{\limsup}_{t \downarrow 0} \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} A_t(x) \mathrel{\le} e^{\lambda}\varepsilon$, and since $\varepsilon>0$ can be arbitrarily small, we see the limit is zero. Finally, collecting all terms, we see \eqref{ineq_CM:ctemp_continuous_subg} holds.  
		
		For \eqref{it_prop_subg_no_noise:3}, we let $L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}$ denote the collection of $L^\infty_{\mathbb X}$ functions with compact essential support. Note by \cite[Theorem 3.1 \& Corollary  4.2 \& Corollary 4.5]{barlow2012equivalence} (also see \cite[p. 1122]{barlow2012equivalence}), there exist $\alpha, \delta \in (0,1)$ and $C>0$ so that for any $(t,x) \in (0,\infty)\times {\mathbb X}$, $0< R< \Psi^{-1}(t)$,  $f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}$, the function $u(s,y):= P_{s}f(y)$ for $s>0$ and $y \in {\mathbb X}$ satisfies
		\begin{align}\label{ineq_CM:osc_inequality}
			\Absolute*{u(s_1,y_1) - u(s_2,y_2)} \le C \Parentheses*{\frac{\Psi^{-1}\Parentheses*{\Absolute*{s_1-s_2}} +d(y_1,y_2)}{R}}^\alpha \sup_{(s,y) \in D((t,x), R)} \mathopen{\lvert}u(s,y)\mathclose{\rvert}
		\end{align}
		for all $(s_1,y_1),(s_2,y_2) \in D((t,x),\delta R)$, where $D((t,x),r):= (t-\Psi(r), t]\times B_r(x)$ for $t\in \mathbb{R}$, $x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $r>0$.
		
		Recall $\delta, \alpha \in (0,1)$ in \eqref{ineq_CM:osc_inequality}, we note by \cite[p.1126 Proof of Lemma 4.6]{barlow2012equivalence}, it holds uniformly in $t>0$, $x',x'' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x',x'') \le \delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2)$ and $f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Absolute*{\int_{\mathbb X} \Parentheses*{G_t(x',y) - G_t(x'',y)} f(y)m(\mathrm dy)} \lesssim \Parentheses*{\frac{d(x',x'')}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)}}^\alpha \Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}}.
		\end{align*}
		On the other hand, if $\Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}} \le 1$, then by conservativeness, the left hand side is bounded above by $2$. Hence, 
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{G_t(x',\cdot) - G_t(x'',\cdot)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}&= \sup_{f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}\atop \Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}} \le 1 }\Absolute*{\int_{\mathbb X} \Parentheses*{G_t(x',y) - G_t(x'',y)} f(y)m(\mathrm dy)}\\
			&\lesssim \min\left\{1,\Parentheses*{\frac{d(x',x'')}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)}}^\alpha  \right\}.
		\end{align*}
		Now let $x \in {\mathbb X}$ be arbitrary and let $0<t< t'< \infty$. Suppose first that 
		$$
		t'-t < \Psi(\delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2)),
		$$
		then $(t,x),(t',x) \in D\Parentheses*{(t^{\prime},x),\delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2)}$. By \eqref{ineq_CM:osc_inequality}, we see it holds uniformly in   $f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}$ with $\Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}} \le 1$, $0<t'-t<\Psi(\delta\Psi^{-1}(t/2))$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\Absolute*{P_tf(x) - P_{t'}f(x)}\lesssim\Parentheses*{\frac{\Psi^{-1}\Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}}}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)}}^\alpha.
		\end{align*}
		If instead $t'-t\ge  \Psi\Parentheses*{\delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2)}$, by monotonicity of $\Psi^{-1}$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\Psi^{-1}\Parentheses*{t'-t} \ge \delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2),
		\end{align*}
		which implies 
		\begin{align*}
			\delta^\alpha\le \Parentheses*{\frac{\Psi^{-1}(t'-t)}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)} }^\alpha.
		\end{align*}
		Hence, for any $f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}$ with $\Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}} \le 1$, $x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $\Psi\Parentheses*{\delta \Psi^{-1}(t/2)}\le t'-t$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Absolute*{P_tf(x) - P_{t'}f(x)} \le 2\mathrel{\le}2\delta^{-\alpha} \Parentheses*{\frac{\Psi^{-1}(t'-t)}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)} }^\alpha.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, it holds uniformly in $0<t< t'< \infty$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{G_t(x,\cdot) - G_{t'}(x,\cdot)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} &= \sup_{f \in L^{\infty,c}_{\mathbb X}\atop \Norm*{f}_{L^\infty_{\mathbb X}} \le 1}\Absolute*{P_tf(x) -P_{t'}f(x)}\\
			&\lesssim \min\left\{ 1, \Parentheses*{\frac{\Psi^{-1}(t'-t)}{\Psi^{-1}(t/2)} }^\alpha\right\}.
		\end{align*}
		Finally, we see \eqref{it_prop_subg_no_noise:3} holds by Lemma \ref{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound} and the fact that $\alpha \in (0,1]$.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem_CM:subg_space_time_scaling}
		There exists $a>0$ so that for each $\lambda>0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\exp \Parentheses*{\lambda \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)}}G_t(x,y) \lesssim G_{at}(x,y),
		\end{align*}
		uniformly in $t>0$, $x,y \in {\mathbb X}$. Furthermore, it holds uniformly in $t>0,x,y \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			d(x,y)^\lambda G_{t}(x,y) \lesssim \Psi^{-1}(t)^\lambda G_{at}\left( x,y\right).
		\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $b_U,b_L,c_U,c_L>0$ be as in $\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)$, and we may assume without loss of generality that $b_U\le b_L$, $c_U \le c_L$ and $b_U,c_U \le 1$. Observe by Lemma \ref{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound}, for each $\theta >0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\Psi(\theta r) \asymp \Psi(r),\qquad \text{ uniformly in }r >0. 
		\end{align*}
		
		Let $r_\lambda(t):= \frac{b_Uc_U}{2\lambda}\Psi^{-1}(t)$ and $K_\lambda(t) := \frac{c_U}{2 \Psi\Parentheses*{r_\lambda(t)}}$, then for $r>0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\frac{c_U}{2}t\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_U \frac{r}{t}} \ge \frac{c_Ub_U r}{2r_\lambda(t)} - \frac{c_U t}{2\Psi(r_\lambda(t))}= \frac{\lambda r}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} - K_\lambda(t) t,
		\end{align*}
		In other words, 
		\begin{align*}
			\frac{\lambda r}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} - c_U t\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_U \frac{r}{t}} \le K_\lambda(t) t - \frac{c_U}{2} t\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_U \frac{r}{t}}.
		\end{align*}
		However, $K_\lambda(t) \asymp \frac{1}{t}$, hence by \eqref{ineq_CM:volume_all_scales} it holds uniformly in $t>0,x,y \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\exp \left(\lambda \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} \right)G_t(x,y) \lesssim \frac{1}{V(\Psi^{-1}(t)\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X}))} \exp \Parentheses*{-\frac{c_U}{2} t\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_U \frac{d(x,y)}{t}} }.
		\end{align*}
		By Lemma \ref{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound}, for any $\theta\ge 1$, there exist $\beta \ge 1$ and $C>0$ so that $\Psi(\theta r) \le C \theta^{\beta + 1}\Psi(r)$ for all $r>0$.  Hence, we may choose an appropriate $a>0$ so that for all $t,r>0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			c_L (at)\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_L  \frac{r}{at}} &= \sup_{s>0} \frac{c_Lb_L r}{s}- \frac{c_Lat}{\Psi(s)}\\
			&= \sup_{s>0} \frac{c_Ub_U r}{s} - \frac{c_L at}{\Psi\Parentheses*{s \frac{c_Lb_L}{c_Ub_U}}}\\
			&\le \sup_{s>0} \frac{c_Ub_Ur}{s} - \frac{c_La}{C \Parentheses*{\frac{c_L b_L}{c_Ub_U}}^{\beta+1}}\frac{t}{\Psi(s)}\\
			&\le \sup_{s>0} \frac{c_Ub_U r}{s} - \frac{c_U t}{\Psi(s)}\\
			&=c_Ut\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_U \frac{r}{t}}
		\end{align*}
		Hence, by replacing $c_U$ by $c_U/2$ if needed and \eqref{ineq_CM:volume_all_scales},  it holds uniformly in $t>0, x,y \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\exp \left(\lambda \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} \right)G_t(x,y) \lesssim \frac{1}{V(\Psi^{-1}(t)\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X}))} \exp \Parentheses*{-c_L at\Psi^*\Parentheses*{b_L \frac{d(x,y)}{at}} } \lesssim G_{at}(x,y).
		\end{align*}
		The second inequality is obtained by noting  
		\begin{align*}
			d(x,y)^\lambda = \Psi^{-1}(t)^\lambda \Parentheses*{\frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)}}^{\lambda} \lesssim \Psi^{-1}(t)^\lambda \exp \Parentheses*{\lambda \frac{d(x,y)}{\Psi^{-1}(t)}}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
We note \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} of \eqref{it_prop_subg_noise:1} is a direct consequence of the sub-Gaussian heat kernel upper bound and Proposition \ref{prop:sub_g_cond_no_noise}. For \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound}, fix $\delta\in(0,1)$ and let $k \ge 1$ to be determined later and consider by Lemma \ref{lem_CM:subg_space_time_scaling} that 
		\begin{align*}
			\int_{\mathbb X} \mathbf 1_{d(x,y)\ge \delta} G_t(x,y)^2 m(\mathrm dy) &\lesssim  t^{-\alpha_{0}} \delta^{-k}\int_{\mathbb X} d(x,y)^k G_t(x,y) m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\lesssim t^{-\alpha_{0}} \delta^{-k} \Psi^{-1}(t)^k.
		\end{align*}
		By Lemma \ref{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound}, we see there exists $k \ge 1$ so that $\Psi^{-1}(t)^k \lesssim t$ uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$, which implies the desired inequality. 
		
		To see \eqref{it_prop_subg_noise:2}, let us denote $r(x):= d(x_0,x)$ and let $\lambda \ge 0$ and $T\ge 1$ be fixed. It holds uniformly in $t \in (0, T]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that  
		\begin{align*}
			&e^{-2\lambda r(x)}\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}G_t(x,y)e^{\lambda \Parentheses*{r(y)+r(z)}}G_s(y,z) G_t(x,z) m(\mathrm dz)m(\mathrm dy)\\
			\le & \quad\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}  e^{\lambda \Parentheses*{d(x,y)+d(x,z)}} G_t(x,y)G_{s}(z,y)G_t(x,z)m(\mathrm dy)m(\mathrm dz)\\
			\overset{\text{Lem }\ref{lem_CM:subg_space_time_scaling} }{\lesssim} &\quad \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2} G_{at}(x,y)G_{at}(x,z) G_s(z,y) m(\mathrm dy)m(\mathrm dz)\\
			=& \quad G_{2at+s}(x,x).
		\end{align*}
		Since $V$ is increasing, $\frac{1}{V(r\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X}))}\le \frac{1}{V(r)}+\frac{1}{V(1\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X}))}$ for all $r>0$. Therefore, by \eqref{ineq_CM:volume_all_scales}, uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$
		\begin{align*}
			e^{-2\lambda r(x)}\Norm*{G_t(x,\cdot)h(\cdot)^\lambda}_{\mathcal H_{K_{\gamma}}}^2
			&\lesssim \int_0^\infty e^{-s}s^{\gamma} G_{2at +s}(x,x) \frac{\mathrm ds}{s}\\
			&\mathrel{\lesssim} \int_0^\infty e^{-s}s^\gamma \frac{1}{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(2at+s)\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X})}}\frac{\mathrm ds}{s}\\
			&\mathrel{\le} \int_0^\infty s^\gamma \frac{1}{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(2at+s)}}\frac{\mathrm ds}{s} + \frac{\Gamma(\gamma)}{V(1\wedge \text{diam}({\mathbb X}))}\\
			&\lesssim \int_0^\infty s^{\gamma} \frac{1}{V\Parentheses*{\Psi^{-1}(t+s)}}\frac{\mathrm ds}{s} \mathbin{+} 1\\
			&\lesssim t^{-\alpha_{0}},
		\end{align*}
		which implies \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} holds. Finally, we may take $k \ge 1$ sufficiently large so that $\Psi^{-1}(t/a)^{2k} \lesssim t$ uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$ and consider for each $\delta>0$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\mathbf 1_{B_\delta(x)^{\mathrm c}} G_{t/a}(x,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_{K_{\gamma}}}^2&\le \delta^{-2k}\Norm*{d(x,\cdot)^kG_{t/a}(x,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_{K_{\gamma}}}^2\\
			&\lesssim \delta^{-2k} \Psi^{-1}(t/a)^{2k} \Norm*{G_{t}(x,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_{K_{\gamma}}}^2\\
			&\lesssim \delta^{-2k}\, t\, t^{-\alpha_0}\\
			&\lesssim \delta^{-2k}\, t^{1-\alpha_{0}}
		\end{align*}
		uniformly in $t \in (0,1]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$. Since $0< \alpha_{0}<1$, we see the desired result holds.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{prop:on_diagonal_to_instant_propagation}
		Let $\rho:{\mathbb X}\times {\mathbb X} \to \mathbb{R}_+$ be a geodesic metric on the measure space $({\mathbb X},m)$, denote $B_r^\rho(x)$ be the open ball of radius $r>0$ centered at $x \in {\mathbb X}$ under the metric $\rho$. Let $V:\mathbb{R}_+\to \mathbb{R}_+$ be a doubling function so that for some $s_0>0$, 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:local_volume_doubling}
			V(s) \asymp m(B_s^\rho(x)),\qquad \text{uniformly in } x \in {\mathbb X}, \, s \in (0,s_0]. 
		\end{align}
		Let $\Psi:\mathbb{R}_+\to \mathbb{R}_+$ be an increasing homeomorphism for which there exists $\delta_0>0$ so that 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:on_diagonal_lower_bound}
			G_t(y,x) \gtrsim \frac{1}{V(\Psi^{-1}(t))}, \qquad \text{ uniformly for } t \in (0,1]\text{ and } \rho(x,y) \le \delta_0 \Psi^{-1}(t),
		\end{align}
		and 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:on_diagonal_space_time_scaling}
			\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\frac{t}{\Psi^{-1}(t)} =0.
		\end{align}
		Then condition \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation} holds under the metric $\rho$.
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Let $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$ and $0<r_0<v$ be arbitrary but fixed. By \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_space_time_scaling}, there exists $\varepsilon_0 \in (0,1/2]$ so that for all $\varepsilon \in (0, \varepsilon_0)$, 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:on_diagonal_to_ip_step_1}
			v \varepsilon \le \frac{\delta_0}{8} \Psi^{-1}(t),\qquad \frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t) < r_0 \wedge s_0,\qquad \text{ for all }t \in (\varepsilon, 2\varepsilon).
		\end{align}
		Let $\varepsilon \in (0,\varepsilon_0),\, t \in (\varepsilon, 2\varepsilon),\, r \in (r_0, v)$ and $x \in B_{r+\varepsilon v}^\rho(x_0)$. Set 
		\begin{align*}
			r_x:= \rho(x, B^\rho_r(x_0)):= \inf_{y \in B^\rho_r(x_0)} \rho(x,y).
		\end{align*}
		Since $\rho$ is a geodesic metric, $r_x=\max\{\rho(x,x_0)-r,0\}\leq v\varepsilon$. Let $\zeta:[0, \rho(x,x_0)]\to {\mathbb X}$ be a geodesic from $x$ to $x_0$ and set
		\begin{align*}
