			\ell:= \min\{ r_x +\frac{\delta_0}{4} \Psi^{-1}(t), \rho(x,x_0)\},\qquad w := \zeta(\ell).
		\end{align*}
		We claim that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:on_diagonal_set_inclusion}
			B^\rho_{\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)}(w) \subset B_r^{\rho}(x_0).
		\end{align}
		Indeed, if $\ell = r_x +\frac{\delta_0}{4}\Psi^{-1}(t)$, then $\rho(x_0,w) = \rho(x_0,x) - \rho(x,w)= \rho(x,x_0) - r_x - \frac{\delta_0}{4}\Psi^{-1}(t)$.
		Hence for all $y \in B^\rho_{\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)}(w)$, we see 
		\begin{align*}
			\rho(y,x_0)& \le \rho(w,y)+\rho(w,x_0) \\
			&\le \frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t) +\rho(x,x_0) - r_x - \frac{\delta_0}{4}\Psi^{-1}(t)\\
			&= \rho(x,x_0)-r_x-\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)\\
			&\le r - \frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)\\
			&< r.
		\end{align*}
		If $\ell = \rho(x,x_0)$, then $w = x_0$ and \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_set_inclusion} holds by \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_to_ip_step_1}. This proves \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_set_inclusion}.

		In addition, we see it holds for all $y \in B^\rho_{\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)}(w)$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\rho(y,x) &\le \rho(y,w) + \rho(w,x) \\
			&\le \frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t) + r_x +\frac{\delta_0}{4}\Psi^{-1}(t)\\
			& \le  \frac{3\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t) + r_x \\
			&\le  \frac{3\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t) +v\varepsilon\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_to_ip_step_1}}{\le} \frac{\delta_0}{2}\Psi^{-1}(t)\\
			&< \delta_0 \Psi^{-1}(t).
		\end{align*}
		Therefore,
		\begin{align*}
			\int_{B^\rho_r(x_0)} G_t(y,x) m(\mathrm dy) &\overset{\eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_set_inclusion}}{\ge} \int_{B^\rho_{\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t)}(w)} G_t(y,x) m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_lower_bound}{,\,\eqref{ineq:local_volume_doubling}}}{\gtrsim} \frac{V(\frac{\delta_0}{8}\Psi^{-1}(t))}{V(\Psi^{-1}(t))}\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:doubling_function}}{\gtrsim} 1,
		\end{align*}
		note that the implicit constant depends only on the doubling property of $V$ and the constant in \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_lower_bound}, which gives the desired inequality.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
By \cite[Theorem 2.11]{murugan2020length} and \cite[Proposition A.1]{kajino2020singularity}, there exists a geodesic metric $\rho$ on ${\mathbb X}$ so that 
		\begin{align*}
			\rho(x,y) \asymp d(x,y),\qquad \text{uniformly in }x,y \in {\mathbb X}. 
		\end{align*}
		Note that $\mathrm{HKE}_{\rm full}(\Psi)$ holds with $d(x,y)$ replaced by $\rho(x,y)$ for $x,y \in {\mathbb X}$. 

		By Lemma \ref{lem_CM:escape_polynomial_bound}, we see \eqref{ineq:on_diagonal_space_time_scaling} holds and the desired inequality is a direct consequence of Lemma \ref{prop:on_diagonal_to_instant_propagation}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{lem:manifold_gaussian_consequences}
 Assume \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian}. The heat kernel is conservative:
 \begin{align}
  \int_M G_t(x,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)=1,
  \qquad t>0,\quad x\in M.
  \label{eq:manifold_conservation}
 \end{align}
  Moreover, the following estimates hold.
 \begin{enumerate}
  \item There are constants $c,C,\kappa>0$, independent of the centers and radii, such that
 \begin{align}
  c r^n\leq m(B_r(x))\leq C r^n,
  \qquad x\in M,\quad 0<r\leq1.
  \label{eq:manifold_local_volume}
 \end{align}
  At all radii,
 \begin{align}
  m(B_R(x))\leq C R^n e^{\kappa R},\qquad x\in M,\quad R>0.
  \label{eq:manifold_volume_growth}
 \end{align}
  \item For every $q\geq0$,
 \begin{align}
  \sup_{x\in M,\,0<t\leq1}
  \int_M G_t(x,y)e^{q d(x,y)}\,m(\mathrm dy)<\infty.
  \label{eq:manifold_exp_moment}
 \end{align}
  \item There are constants $\eta\in(0,1)$ and $C>0$ such that, uniformly for $x,x'\in M$ and $t,t'\in(0,1]$,
 \begin{align}
  \|G_t(x,\cdot)-G_t(x',\cdot)\|_{L^1_M}
  &\leq C\frac{d(x,x')^\eta}{t^{\eta/2}},\nonumber\\
  \|G_t(x,\cdot)-G_{t'}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^1_M}
  &\leq C\frac{|t-t'|^{\eta/2}}{(t\wedge t')^{\eta/2}}.
  \label{eq:manifold_holder}
 \end{align}
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
For $0<r\leq1$, the sub-Markov property  gives
 \[
  1\geq\int_{B_r(x)}G_{r^2}(x,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)
  \overset{\eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian}}{\geq} c_0e^{-C_0}r^{-n}m(B_r(x)).
 \]
 For the reverse volume bound, set $a=c_0 2^{-n/2}$ and choose $A\geq\sqrt2$ such that $C_0e^{-c_0A^2}\leq a/2$. Splitting the semigroup integral over $B_{A\sqrt t}(x)$ and its complement gives, for $0<t\leq1/2$,
 \begin{align*}
	at^{-n/2}&\overset{\eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian}}{\le} G_{2t}(x,x)
	=\Parentheses*{\int_{B_{A\sqrt t}(x)}+\int_{B_{A\sqrt t}(x)^c}} G_t(x,y)^2\,m(\mathrm dy)\\
	&\leq C_0^2t^{-n}m(B_{A\sqrt t}(x))
       +\frac a2t^{-n/2}.
 \end{align*}
 Taking $t=(r/A)^2$ yields
 \[
  \frac{a}{2C_0^2A^n}r^n\leq m(B_r(x))
  \leq\frac{e^{C_0}}{c_0}r^n,
  \qquad x\in M,\quad 0<r\leq1,
 \]
 proving \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume}.

 Conservation \eqref{eq:manifold_conservation} follows from \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian}, \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume} and \cite[p.~1001]{coulhon2012heat}. The volume bound \eqref{eq:manifold_volume_growth} follows from \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume} and \cite[Section~2.3, (2.6)]{hebisch2001relation}.

 By \cite[Lemma~3.6]{hebisch2001relation} and \eqref{eq:manifold_conservation}, there are $c,C>0$ such that
 \begin{align}
  \int_{B_r(x)^c}G_t(x,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)
  \leq Ce^{-cr^2/t},\qquad x\in M,\quad 0<t\leq1,\quad r>0.
  \label{eq:manifold_gaussian_tail}
 \end{align}
 For $q=0$, \eqref{eq:manifold_exp_moment} is conservation. For $q>0$, Tonelli's theorem gives
 \begin{align*}
  \int_M G_t(x,y)e^{q d(x,y)}\,m(\mathrm dy)&=\int_M G_t(x,y)\Parentheses*{1+q\int_0^{d(x,y)}e^{qr}\,\mathrm dr}\,m(\mathrm dy)\\
  &=1+q\int_0^\infty e^{qr}
       \int_{\{y:d(x,y)>r\}}G_t(x,y)\,m(\mathrm dy)\,\mathrm dr\\
  &{\overset{\eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian_tail}}{\leq}} 1+Cq\int_0^\infty e^{qr-cr^2/t}\,\mathrm dr\\
  &\le 1+C\int_0^\infty e^{qr - cr^2}\,\mathrm dr
 \end{align*}
 which is finite and independent of $x$ and $0<t\leq1$. This proves \eqref{eq:manifold_exp_moment}.

 By \cite[pp.~1000--1001]{coulhon2012heat}, \cite[Theorem~2.3(2)]{hebisch2001relation} and the $L^\infty$-contractivity of $(P_t)$, there exist $C>0$ and $\eta\in(0,1)$ such that, for every bounded measurable $f$, $x,x'\in M$ and $t,t'\in(0,1]$,
 \[
  |P_tf(x)-P_{t'}f(x')|
  \leq C\left(\frac{d(x,x')+|t-t'|^{1/2}}{\sqrt{t\wedge t'}}\right)^\eta
       \|f\|_\infty.
 \]
 Taking the supremum over $\|f\|_\infty\leq1$ gives the same bound for $\|G_t(x,\cdot)-G_{t'}(x',\cdot)\|_{L^1_M}$ by duality. Setting $t'=t$ or $x'=x$ proves the two estimates in \eqref{eq:manifold_holder}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- lemma-24: body_md -->
\label{lem:manifold_semigroup}
 Assume \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} and let $h(x)=e^{d(o,x)}$. Then $(M,d,m)$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:entropy bound}, and $h$ satisfies \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and \eqref{ineq:ref_regular}. The heat semigroup is Feller and satisfies \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}, the $L^1$ inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}, and \eqref{cond:time_holder} with $\beta=\xi_1=\eta/2$ and $\xi_2=\eta$, where $\eta$ is as in Lemma~\ref{lem:manifold_gaussian_consequences}. Moreover, \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation} holds with the geodesic distance $d$.
<!-- end lemma-24 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
The volume-to-covering argument in \cite[pp.~167--168]{kaenmaki2017measures}, applied at bounded scales using \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume}--\eqref{eq:manifold_volume_growth}, gives $D_\varepsilon(B_R(x))\leq C_R\varepsilon^{-n}$ uniformly in $x\in M$ and $0<\varepsilon\leq1$, for each $R\geq1$. This proves \eqref{ineq:entropy bound}.

 By the $5r$-covering lemma \cite[Theorem~1.2, p.~2]{heinonen2001lectures} and separability, there is an at most countable family $(x_j)_{j\in\mathcal I}$ such that $M=\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal I}B_1(x_j)$ and the balls $B_{1/10}(x_j)$ are pairwise disjoint. Recall $\kappa>0$ in \eqref{eq:manifold_volume_growth} and fix $\lambda_0>\kappa$. By \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume}, the triangle inequality and disjointness, followed by Tonelli's theorem, there exists $C_{\lambda_0}>0$ such that
 \begin{align*}
  \sum_{j\in\mathcal I}h(x_j)^{-\lambda_0}
  \leq C_{\lambda_0}\int_M e^{-\lambda_0d(o,y)}\,m(\mathrm dy)
  =C_{\lambda_0}\lambda_0\int_0^\infty e^{-\lambda_0r}m(B_r(o))\,\mathrm dr\overset{\eqref{eq:manifold_volume_growth}}{<}\infty,
 \end{align*}
this proves \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} for every $\lambda\geq\lambda_0$. Condition \eqref{ineq:ref_regular} follows from $h(y)\leq e^{d(x,y)}h(x)$.

 The Gaussian upper bound in \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} and the semigroup property yield $P_t\mathcal C_M^0\subset\mathcal C_M^0$ for all $t>0$. The Feller property then follows from \cite[Example~4.2, p.~10]{benamor2023essential}. Moreover, \eqref{eq:manifold_exp_moment} gives  the $L^1$ inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}.
 
 Fix $\lambda>0$ and $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{M,h}$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:C_temp_density_0} (whose proof does not depend on this  subsection), choose $f_j\in\mathcal C_M^0$ with $\|f-f_j\|_{(\lambda)}\to0$. Then for each $\lambda>0$, there exists $C_\lambda >0$ so that 
 \[
  \limsup_{t\downarrow0}\|P_t f-f\|_{(\lambda)}
  \leq C_\lambda\|f-f_j\|_{(\lambda)}
       +\limsup_{t\downarrow0}\|P_t f_j-f_j\|_\infty =C_\lambda\|f-f_j\|_{(\lambda)}
 \]
 which converges to $0$ as $j\to\infty$. The same approximation shows that $P_t f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{M,h}$, and the semigroup property gives continuity at every time. Since $P_t^*=P_t$, this proves \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}. Equation~\eqref{eq:manifold_holder} gives \eqref{cond:time_holder}.

 Finally, $(M,d)$ is geodesic by the Hopf--Rinow theorem \cite[Chapter~7, Theorem~2.8]{docarmo1992riemannian}. With $\rho=d$, $V(r)=r^n$ and $\Psi(r)=r^2$, \eqref{eq:manifold_local_volume} and the Gaussian lower bound verify the volume and near-diagonal hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{prop:on_diagonal_to_instant_propagation}. Since $t/\Psi^{-1}(t)=\sqrt t\to0$, that lemma gives \eqref{ineq:instant_propagation}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:manifold_covariance}
 Assume \eqref{eq:manifold_gaussian} and \eqref{eq:manifold_gamma}. The measure $K$ in \eqref{eq:manifold_covariance} is symmetric, non-negative definite and Radon. Moreover, $L^2_M\subset\mathcal H_K$ and
 \begin{align}
  0\leq\|v\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2\leq\|v\|_{L^2_M}^2,
  \qquad v\in L^2_M.
  \label{eq:manifold_noise_contraction}
 \end{align}
 For every $\alpha$ with $\max\{0,n/2-\gamma\}<\alpha<1$, the $\mathcal H_K$ inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} and condition~\eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound} hold.
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Clearly, $K$ is a symmetric Radon measure. Applying Tonelli's theorem and $L^2$-contractivity to $|v|$ gives $v\in\mathcal H_K$ for every $v\in L^2_M$. Symmetry and the semigroup property then yield
 \[
  0\leq\|v\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2
  =\frac1{\Gamma(\gamma)}\int_0^\infty e^{-s}s^{\gamma-1}
       \|P_{s/2}v\|_{L^2_M}^2\,\mathrm ds
  \leq\|v\|_{L^2_M}^2.
 \]
 This proves non-negative definiteness and \eqref{eq:manifold_noise_contraction}. For $v(y)=G_t(x,y)h(y)^\lambda$, the Gaussian bound and \eqref{eq:manifold_exp_moment} give, uniformly for $x\in M$, $0<t\leq1$ and $0\leq\lambda\leq1$,
 \[
  \|v\|_{L^1_M}\leq C h(x)^\lambda,
  \qquad \|v\|_{L^2_M}^2\leq C t^{-n/2}h(x)^{2\lambda}.
 \]
 For $s\leq1$,
 \[
  \langle v,P_s v\rangle_{L^2_M}
  \leq\min\{\|v\|_{L^2_M}^2,\,
             \sup_{y,z}G_s(y,z)\|v\|_{L^1_M}^2\}
  \leq C h(x)^{2\lambda}(t+s)^{-n/2};
 \]
 for $s\geq1$, it is at most $C h(x)^{2\lambda}$ by the semigroup property. Since \eqref{eq:manifold_gamma} gives $\gamma>0$ and $n/2-\gamma<1$, we may fix
 \[
  \max\{0,n/2-\gamma\}<\alpha<1.
 \]
 In particular, $\gamma+\alpha-n/2>0$. For $0<t\leq1$, splitting at $s=t$ gives
 \begin{align*}
  \int_0^1s^{\gamma-1}(t+s)^{-n/2}\,\mathrm ds
  &\leq t^{-n/2}\int_0^t s^{\gamma-1}\,\mathrm ds
       +\int_t^1s^{\gamma-n/2-1}\,\mathrm ds\\
  &\leq\frac{t^{\gamma-n/2}}{\gamma}
       +t^{-\alpha}\int_t^1s^{\gamma+\alpha-n/2-1}\,\mathrm ds\\
  &\leq\left(\frac1\gamma+\frac1{\gamma+\alpha-n/2}\right)t^{-\alpha}.
 \end{align*}
 Therefore, using $1\leq t^{-\alpha}$,
 \begin{align}
  \|G_t(x,\cdot)h^\lambda\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2
  \leq C h(x)^{2\lambda}
    \left(1+\int_0^1s^{\gamma-1}(t+s)^{-n/2}\,\mathrm ds\right)
  \leq C t^{-\alpha}h(x)^{2\lambda},
  \label{eq:manifold_noise_bound}
 \end{align}
 This proves the $\mathcal H_K$ inequality in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} with $\alpha\in(0,1)$, as required. Equation~\eqref{eq:manifold_noise_contraction} and the Gaussian bound also give
 \[
  \|G_t(x,\cdot)\mathbf1_{B_\delta(x)^c}\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2
  \leq\|G_t(x,\cdot)\mathbf1_{B_\delta(x)^c}\|_{L^2_M}^2
  \leq C t^{-n/2}e^{-c\delta^2/t}\longrightarrow0,\quad t\downarrow0,
 \]
 uniformly in $x$ for each $\delta>0$, proving \eqref{cond:hk_uni_bound}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Lemmas~\ref{lem:manifold_semigroup} and~\ref{lem:manifold_covariance} verify all the geometric, semigroup and noise assumptions.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- lemma-26: body_md -->
\label{lem:ref_grow}
		Let $({\mathbb X},d)$ be a metric space with infinite diameter and let $h$ be a  weight function on it satisfying \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms} and \eqref{ineq:ref_regular}. Then for each $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}\label{cond:ref_grow}
			\lim_{R \to \infty} \inf_{x \in B_R(x_0)^c} h(x) = \infty.
		\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-26 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Suppose, to the contrary, that \eqref{cond:ref_grow} fails. Then for some $x_0\in{\mathbb X}$ and $M\geq1$, there are points $y_k\in{\mathbb X}$ such that $d(x_0,y_k)\to\infty$ and $h(y_k)\leq M$. Let $\lambda_0>0$ and $(x_n)_{n\in\mathcal I}$ be as in \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}. For each $k$, choose $n_k$ so that $y_k\in B_1(x_{n_k})$. Since $d(x_0,x_{n_k})\geq d(x_0,y_k)-1\to\infty$ as $k \to \infty$, after passing to a subsequence we may assume that the indices $n_k$ are distinct. By \eqref{ineq:ref_regular}, there is some $C\geq1$ such that $h(x_{n_k})\leq Ch(y_k)\leq CM$. Consequently,
		\begin{align*}
			\sum_{n\in\mathcal I}h(x_n)^{-\lambda_0}
			\geq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}h(x_{n_k})^{-\lambda_0}
			\geq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}(CM)^{-\lambda_0}=\infty,
		\end{align*}
		contradicting \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lemma-27: body_md -->
\label{lem:PS}
		$\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ is a Polish space.
<!-- end lemma-27 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
It is well known that if a metric space $(\mathbb X,d)$ is a locally compact separable complete metric space, then $\mathcal C_{\mathbb X}^0$  is separable.
		Let us fix an arbitrary $x_0 \in {\mathbb X}$.
		Take $\{g_n\}_{n \in \mathbb N} \subset \mathcal C_{\mathbb X}^0$ to be a dense subset and let $\mathcal X_n$  be continuous functions with values in $[0,1]$ whose support is contained in $B_{n+1}(x_0)$ and $\mathcal X_n(x) = 1$ for all $x \in B_n(x_0)$.
		Denote $g_{n,k}:= g_n \cdot \mathcal X_k$ for each $n,k \in \mathbb N$.
		Then $\mathcal A_d:=\{g_{n,k}\}_{n,k \in \mathbb N}$ is countably dense in $\mathcal C_{\mathbb X}^0$.
		
		We first show $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ is Polish. Define the metric $d_\mathrm{tem}$ on $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$ by 
		\begin{align*}
			d_\mathrm{tem}(f,g):= \sum_{n = 1}^\infty 2^{-n}\Parentheses*{1 \wedge \Norm*{f - g}_{(n^{-1})}},
		\end{align*}
		which makes it a complete metric space.
		Suppose $f \in \mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$, define $f_n:= f \cdot \mathcal X_n$ for each $n \in \mathbb N$ and recall that the support of $\mathcal X_n$ is contained in $B_{n+1} := B_{n+1}(x_0)$ for some $x_0$.
		Then consider for any fixed $\ell>0$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:first compact approx temp}
			\lim_{n\to \infty}\| f - f_n\|_{(\ell)} = \lim_{n\to \infty}\sup_{x \in B_n^c} h(x)^{-\ell}\left| f(x)\right| \le \|f \|_{(\ell/2)}\lim_{n\to \infty} \sup_{x \in B_n^c}h(x)^{-\ell/2},
		\end{align}
		which is equal to zero by Lemma \ref{lem:ref_grow} if ${\mathbb X}$ has infinite diameter; if ${\mathbb X}$ has finite diameter, then $f_n=f$ for all large $n$.
		Now for each $n$, take $\tilde g_{n,k}\mathrel{\in} \mathcal A_d$, $k\in\mathbb N$, so that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \|f_n - \tilde g_{n,k}\|_\infty = 0$; this is possible since $f_n\in\mathcal C^0_{\mathbb X}$ and $\mathcal A_d$ is dense in $\mathcal C^0_{\mathbb X}$.
		Hence, given $\varepsilon>0$, pick $n \in \mathbb N$ large enough so that $\| f - f_n\|_{(\ell)} < \frac{\varepsilon}{2}$, and pick $k = k(n) \in \mathbb N$ large enough so that $\| f_n - \tilde g_{n,k}\|_\infty < \frac{\varepsilon}{2}$.
		Hence we obtain
		\begin{align}
			\|f - \tilde g_{n,k}\|_{(\ell)}  \le \| f- f_n\|_{(\ell)} + \|f_n - \tilde g_{n,k}\|_{(\ell)} < \frac{\varepsilon}{2} + \| f_n - \tilde g_{n,k}\|_\infty < \varepsilon.
		\end{align}
		This shows the set of functions $\mathcal A_d$ is a dense subset of $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-28: body_md -->
\label{lem:C_temp_density_0}
		$\mathcal C^0_{\mathbb X}$ is dense in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
<!-- end lemma-28 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
If ${\mathbb X}$ has finite diameter, then \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} and completeness imply that ${\mathbb X}$ is compact, so the assertion is immediate. Suppose that ${\mathbb X}$ has infinite diameter. Fix $x_0\in{\mathbb X}$ and let $(\mathcal X_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be the compactly supported cutoff functions used in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:PS}, chosen so that $0\leq\mathcal X_n\leq1$ with $\mathcal X_n=1$ on $B_n(x_0)$ and $\mathcal X_n =0$ on $B_{n+1}(x_0)^c$. For $f\in\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$, set $f_n:=f\mathcal X_n\in\mathcal C^0_{\mathbb X}$. Then for every $\lambda>0$, 
		\begin{align*}
			\lim_{n \to \infty}\Norm*{f-f_n}_{(\lambda)}
			&\leq \lim_{n \to \infty}\Norm*{f}_{(\lambda/2)}
			\sup_{x\in B_n(x_0)^\mathrm c}h(x)^{-\lambda/2}=0,
		\end{align*}
		by Lemma \ref{lem:ref_grow}. Hence $f_n\to f$ in $\mathcal C^{\mathrm{tem}}_{{\mathbb X},h}$.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Since ${\mathbb X}$ is locally compact and separable, fix an increasing sequence of compact sets $(C_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ with ${\mathbb X}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}C_n$.
		Let $\mathcal S$ be the space of compactly supported real-valued Borel simple functions on ${\mathbb X}$, and define
		\begin{align*}
			B(f,g):=\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}f(x)g(y)K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy),\qquad f,g\in\mathcal S.
		\end{align*}
		The assumptions on $K$ imply that $B$ is a symmetric non-negative definite bilinear form on $\mathcal S$, and hence satisfies the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality.

		For each $f\in\mathcal H_K$, choose $f_n\in\mathcal S$ supported in $C_n$ such that $f_n\to f$ pointwise and $|f_n|\leq|f|$.
		For $f,g\in\mathcal H_K$, Fatou's lemma and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to $|f_n|,|g_n|$ give
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:H0 mixed absolute}
			\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}|f(x)g(y)|K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)
			&\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}B(|f_n|,|g_n|)\nonumber\\
			&\leq\left(\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}|f(x)f(y)|K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)\right)^{1/2}\nonumber\\
			&\qquad\times\left(\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}|g(x)g(y)|K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)\right)^{1/2}<\infty.
		\end{align}
		Thus $\mathcal H_K$ is a vector space, and the domain and the integral formula of $B$ extend to $\mathcal H_K$.
		By dominated convergence, $B(f,f)=\lim_{n\to\infty}B(f_n,f_n)\geq0$ for every $f\in\mathcal H_K$.
		Consequently,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:H0 Cauchy Schwarz}
			|B(f,g)|\leq B(f,f)^{1/2}B(g,g)^{1/2},\qquad f,g\in\mathcal H_K,
		\end{align}
		and $\Norm*{f}_{\mathcal H_K}=B(f,f)^{1/2}$ is a Hilbertian seminorm in the sense of \cite[Section 1.1]{Zbl054760064}.
		Moreover, since $|f-f_n|\leq2|f|$, dominated convergence also yields
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:H0 compact truncation}
			\lim_{n \to \infty}\Norm*{f-f_n}_{\mathcal H_K}^2
			\leq\lim_{n\to \infty}\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}|f(x)-f_n(x)||f(y)-f_n(y)|K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)=0.
		\end{align}

		To prove separability, for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$ let $\mu_n$ be the finite Radon measure on $C_n$ defined by $\mu_n(A):=K(A\times C_n)$ for Borel sets $A\subset C_n$.
		By the separability of $\mathcal C(C_n;\mathbb{R})$ under the uniform norm and \cite[Proposition~7.9, p.~217]{folland1999real}, choose a countable set $\mathcal D_n\subset\mathcal C(C_n;\mathbb{R})$ dense in $L^2(C_n,\mu_n)$, and extend its elements by zero outside $C_n$.
		For every bounded Borel function $\psi$ supported in $C_n$, we see by the symmetry of $K$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\psi}_{\mathcal H_K}^2
			&=\iint_{C_n^2}{\psi}(x)\psi(y)K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)\\
			&\leq\frac12\iint_{C_n^2}\bigl(|\psi(x)|^2+|\psi(y)|^2\bigr)K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)
			=\Norm*{\psi}_{L^2(C_n,\mu_n)}^2.
		\end{align*}
		Given $f\in\mathcal H_K$ and $\varepsilon>0$, first use \eqref{ineq:H0 compact truncation} to choose $n$ with $\Norm*{f-f_n}_{\mathcal H_K}<\varepsilon/2$.
		Since $f_n$ is bounded and supported in $C_n$, choose $g\in\mathcal D_n$ with $\Norm*{f_n-g}_{L^2(C_n,\mu_n)}<\varepsilon/2$.
		Applying the preceding estimate to $\psi=f_n-g$ gives
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{f-g}_{\mathcal H_K}
			&\leq\Norm*{f-f_n}_{\mathcal H_K}+\Norm*{f_n-g}_{\mathcal H_K}\\
			&\leq\Norm*{f-f_n}_{\mathcal H_K}+\Norm*{f_n-g}_{L^2(C_n,\mu_n)}<\varepsilon.
		\end{align*}
		Thus $\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal D_n$ is a countable dense subset of $\mathcal H_K$.

		Finally, $[0]_K$ is a linear subspace by the seminorm properties, and \eqref{ineq:H0 Cauchy Schwarz} shows that $B$ vanishes whenever one of its arguments belongs to $[0]_K$.
		Thus
		\begin{align*}
			\langle[f]_K,[g]_K\rangle:=B(f,g)
		\end{align*}
		is a well-defined inner product on $\mathcal H_K/[0]_K$, whose induced norm is the unique norm satisfying $\Norm*{[f]_K}_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}=\Norm*{f}_{\mathcal H_K}$.
		Its completion is therefore a separable Hilbert space, as asserted.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
Fix $g\in\mathcal H_K$.
		By the self-adjointness of $Q^{1/2}$, for every $f\in\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K$,
		\[
			(\Gamma_g\circ Q^{1/2})(f)
			=\left\langle[g]_K,Q^{1/2}f\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}
			=\left\langle Q^{1/2}[g]_K,f\right\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}.
		\]
		Thus $\Gamma_g\circ Q^{1/2}$ is a Hilbert--Schmidt  operator on $\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K$, whose norm is $\|Q^{1/2}[g]_K\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}$ by Parseval's identity.
		For every $f\in\operatorname{Ker}(Q^{1/2})^\bot$, the definitions of $Q^{-1/2}$ and the induced norm give
		\[
			\|Q^{1/2}f\|_{Q^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}
			=\|Q^{-1/2}Q^{1/2}f\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}
			=\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K};
		\]
		see \cite[Appendix~B.2, p.~429]{da2014stochastic}.
		Thus $Q^{1/2}$ restricts to an isometric isomorphism from $\operatorname{Ker}(Q^{1/2})^\bot$ onto $Q^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K$.
		Since $\Gamma_g\circ Q^{1/2}$ vanishes on $\operatorname{Ker}(Q^{1/2})$, the Hilbert--Schmidt norm identity in \cite[p.~96 and Section~4.2.1]{da2014stochastic} applies.
		Consequently, $\Gamma_g$ is Hilbert--Schmidt and
		\begin{align*}
			\|\Gamma_g\|^2_{\mathcal L^{\mathrm{HS}}_{Q^{1/2}\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}}
			=\|\Gamma_g\circ Q^{1/2}\|^2_{\mathcal L^{\mathrm{HS}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}}
			=\left\|Q^{1/2}[g]_K\right\|^2_{\widetilde{\mathcal H}_K}
			\leq\|Q^{1/2}\|^2\|g\|^2_{\mathcal H_K}.
		\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lemma-29: body_md -->
\label{lem:Min_H}
		Suppose that $(\Psi(z,x): z\in E, x\in {\mathbb X})$ is a non-negative measurable function on the product space $(E\times {\mathbb X}, \mathscr E\otimes \mathscr B_{\mathbb X}, \nu\otimes m)$ where $(E, \mathscr E, \nu)$ is a $\sigma$-finite measure space.
		Set
		\begin{align*}
			F(x):=\int_{E}\Psi(z,x)\nu(\mathrm dz),\qquad x\in{\mathbb X},
		\end{align*}
		and suppose that $F\in\mathcal B_{\mathbb X}$, $\Psi(z,\cdot)\in\mathcal H_K$ for $\nu$-almost every $z\in E$, and
		\begin{align*}
			\int_{E}\Norm*{\Psi(z,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K}\nu(\mathrm dz)<\infty.
		\end{align*}
		Then $F\in\mathcal H_K$ and
		\begin{equation}
			\Norm*{\int_{E} \Psi(z, \cdot)\nu(\mathrm dz)}_{\mathcal H_K}
			\leq \int_{E} \Norm*{\Psi(z, \cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K} \nu(\mathrm dz).
		\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-29 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
By changing $\Psi$ on a $\nu$-null subset of $E$ if necessary, we may assume that $\Psi(z,\cdot)\in\mathcal H_K$ for every $z\in E$.
		By Tonelli's theorem and \eqref{ineq:H0 Cauchy Schwarz}, we see
		\begin{align*}
			\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}F(x)F(y)K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)
			&=\int_{E}\int_{E}\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}\Psi(z,x)\Psi(z',y)K(\mathrm dx,\mathrm dy)\nu(\mathrm dz)\nu(\mathrm dz')\\
			&\leq\int_{E}\int_{E}\Norm*{\Psi(z,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K}\Norm*{\Psi(z',\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K}\nu(\mathrm dz)\nu(\mathrm dz')\\
			&=\left(\int_{E}\Norm*{\Psi(z,\cdot)}_{\mathcal H_K}\nu(\mathrm dz)\right)^2<\infty.
		\end{align*}
		Therefore, $F\in\mathcal H_K$, and taking square roots proves the desired inequality.
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:HS} and the boundedness of $Q$ on $\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}$, we see by the  Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (cf. \cite[Theorem 4.37]{da2014stochastic}) that, uniformly in $T >0$, 
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:bdg_gen_mtg_bdg}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Norm*{\sup_{t \in [0,T]}\left|\iint_0^t \Phi(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy)\right|}_{L^p_\Omega} &\lesssim \Parentheses*{\int_0^T \mathbb E\left[\langle Q[\Phi(s,\cdot)]_K,[\Phi(s,\cdot)]_K\rangle_{\tilde{\mathcal H}_{K}}^\frac{p}{2}\right]^\frac{2}{p}\mathrm ds}^\frac{1}{2}\\
				&\lesssim \Parentheses*{\int_0^T \mathbb E\left[\langle \Phi(s,\cdot),\Phi(s,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal H_K}^\frac{p}{2}\right]^\frac{2}{p}\mathrm ds}^\frac{1}{2}.
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		For each $s \ge 0$, we see by Minkowski's and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities  that
		\begin{align}
			\mathbb E\left[\langle \Phi(s,\cdot),\Phi(s,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal H_K}^\frac{p}{2}\right]^\frac{2}{p}&=  \Norm*{\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}\Phi(s,x) \Phi(s,y)K\Parentheses*{\mathrm dy,\mathrm dx}}_{L^\frac{p}{2}_\Omega}\\
			&\le \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2} \Norm*{\Phi(s,x)\Phi(s,y)}_{L^\frac{p}{2}_\Omega} K\Parentheses*{\mathrm dy,\mathrm dx}\\
			&= \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}\mathbb E \left[\Absolute*{\Phi(s,x)}^\frac{p}{2}\Absolute*{\Phi(s,y)}^\frac{p}{2} \right]^\frac{2}{p}K(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dx)\\
			&\le \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2} \Norm*{\Phi(s,x)}_{L^p_\Omega} \Norm*{\Phi(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega} K(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dx)\\
			&=\Norm*{\Norm*{\Phi(s,\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2.
		\end{align}
		Inserting this into \eqref{ineq:bdg_gen_mtg_bdg}, we see the following inequality holds uniformly in $T>0$,
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{\sup_{t \in [0,T]}\left|\iint_0^t \Phi(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy)\right|}_{L^p_\Omega}\lesssim \Parentheses*{\int_0^T \Norm*{\Norm*{\Phi(s,\cdot)}_{L^p_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds}^\frac{1}{2} = \Norm*{\Phi}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K L^p_\Omega},
		\end{align}
		which is the desired result.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Suppose that \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} holds for the given $\lambda$ with $\alpha$ replaced by $\tilde\alpha$, and let $t>0$.
		Then $t = n+a$ for some $n \in \mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}$ and $a \in [0,1]$.
		Hence, there is $C>1$ such that for all $x \in {\mathbb X}$ and $a \in [0,1]$, we have
		\begin{align}
			\left(P_a^* h^\lambda\right)(x) \le  C h(x)^\lambda.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, by the semigroup property, we see 
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:weighted semigroup bound long time temporary}
			\begin{aligned}
				\left(P_t^* h^\lambda\right)(x) &= \left(\left(P_{1}^* \right)^n P_a^* h^\lambda\right) (x)\\
				&\le C^{n+1}h(x)^\lambda\\
				&\le b e^{\kappa t}h(x)^\lambda
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		where $b = \sup_{a \in [0,1]} C^{1-a}$, and $\kappa = \ln(\max\{1,C\})>0$, which proves \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}.
		
		By Lemma \ref{lem:H0} and the assumed bound with exponent $\tilde\alpha$, the following inequality holds uniformly in $\delta \in (0,1]$ and $y_1,y_2 \in {\mathbb X}$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:weighted cs temporary}
			\left \langle G_\delta(\cdot,y_1) h^\lambda,G_\delta(\cdot,y_2) h^\lambda \right \rangle_{ \mathcal H_K } &\le \|G_\delta(\cdot, y_1)h^\lambda\|_{\mathcal H_K}\|G_\delta(\cdot, y_2)h^\lambda\|_{\mathcal H_K}\\
			&\lesssim\delta^{-\tilde\alpha} h(y_1)^{\lambda} h(y_2)^{\lambda}.
		\end{align}
		For any $t >1$, fix $\delta \in [1/2,1]$. Then by the semigroup property and Fubini's theorem, we see that 
		\begin{align}
			\left\|G_t(\cdot,x) h(\cdot)^\lambda\right\|_{ \mathcal H_K}^2 & = \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}G_t(z_1,x) h(z_1)^\lambda G_t(z_2,x) h(z_2)^\lambda K(\mathrm dz_1,\mathrm dz_2)\\
			&= \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2} \Parentheses*{\int_{{\mathbb X}} G_\delta(z_1,y_1)G_{t-\delta}(y_1,x) m(\mathrm dy_1)} h(z_1)^\lambda  \\
			&\qquad  \qquad\qquad \times \Parentheses*{\int_{\mathbb X}  G_{\delta}(z_2,y_2) G_{t-\delta}(y_2,x) m(\mathrm dy_2)} h(z_2)^\lambda K(\mathrm dz_1,\mathrm dz_2) \\
			&= \int_{\mathbb X} \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t - \delta}(y_1,x)G_{t-\delta}(y_2,x)\\
			&\quad \quad \quad  \times \iint_{{\mathbb X}^2} G_\delta(z_1,y_1) h(z_1)^\lambda G_\delta(z_2,y_2) h(z_2)^\lambda K(\mathrm dz_1,\mathrm dz_2) m(\mathrm d y_1) m(\mathrm dy_2)\\
			&=\int_{\mathbb X} \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-\delta}(y_1,x) G_{t-\delta}(y_2,x)\left \langle G_\delta(\cdot,y_1)h^\lambda, G_{\delta}(\cdot,y_2) h^\lambda\right\rangle _{\mathcal H_K} m(\mathrm dy_1) m(\mathrm dy_2)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:weighted cs temporary}}{\lesssim} \delta^{-\tilde\alpha}\Parentheses*{P^*_{t-\delta}\Parentheses*{h^\lambda}(x)}^2\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:weighted semigroup bound long time temporary}}{\lesssim} \delta^{-\tilde\alpha}b^2e^{-2\kappa \delta}e^{2\kappa t}h(x)^{2\lambda}\\
			&\lesssim t^{-\tilde\alpha}e^{\tilde\kappa t}h(x)^{2\lambda},
		\end{align}
		where $\tilde\kappa:=2\kappa+1$ and we used $\delta\ge1/2$ and $t^{\tilde\alpha}\le e^t$ for $t>1$.
		Together with the assumed bound for $t\in(0,1]$, this proves \eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}.
		
		To show \eqref{ineq:time_holder_long_time}, we first note that for each $T>0$, it holds uniformly for $T\ge t' \ge t>0$ with $t'-t \le \frac{1}{2}$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:time_holder_proof_half}
			\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1}}{t^\beta}.
		\end{align}
		To see this, consider the case where $0 < t \le \frac{1}{2}$, then $t' \le 1$ and the estimate is exactly \eqref{cond:time_holder}. Now suppose $t > \frac{1}{2}$ then by the semi-group property, we see uniformly in $T \ge t' >t \ge \frac{1}{2}$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$, 
		\begin{align}
			&\Norm*{\int_{\mathbb X} G_{t - \frac{1}{2}}(\cdot, y)\Parentheses*{G_{\frac{1}{2}}(y,x) - G_{t'-t+ \frac{1}{2}}(y,x)  } m(\mathrm dy)    }_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\\
			\le & \int_{\mathbb X} \Norm*{G_{t - \frac{1}{2}}(\cdot,y)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \Absolute*{G_{\frac{1}{2}}(y,x) - G_{t'-t+ \frac{1}{2}}(y,x) } m(\mathrm dy) \\
			\overset{\eqref{ineq:hk_m long time} }\lesssim & \int_{\mathbb X} \Absolute*{G_{\frac{1}{2}}(y,x) - G_{t'-t+ \frac{1}{2}}(y,x) } m(\mathrm dy)\\
			\overset{\eqref{cond:time_holder} }\lesssim& \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1}}{(1/2)^\beta } \lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1}}{t^\beta },
		\end{align}
		where we used the fact that $t'-t+\frac{1}{2} \in (0,1]$.
		This proves \eqref{ineq:time_holder_proof_half}.
		Suppose $0< t< t' \le T$, and $\Absolute*{t-t'} \ge \frac{1}{2}$.
		Let $n_1  \le n_N\in \mathbb{N}$ be
		\begin{align}
			n_1 := \min\left\{n \in \mathbb{N}: t \le \frac{n}{2}\right\},\qquad n_N:= \max\left\{n \in \mathbb{N}: \frac{n}{2} \le t' \right\},
		\end{align} 		
		Then by \eqref{ineq:time_holder_proof_half}, it holds uniformly in $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that 
		\begin{equation}\label{ineq:time_holder_end_case}
			\begin{aligned}
				\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{\frac{n_1}{2}}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} &\lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t - \frac{n_1}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge 1}}{t^\beta}\\
				\Norm*{G_{t'}(\cdot,x) - G_{\frac{n_N}{2}}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} &\lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t' - \frac{n_N}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge 1}}{(n_N/2)^\beta}.
			\end{aligned}
		\end{equation}
		By the triangle inequality,
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			&\le \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{\frac{n_1}{2}}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			+\Norm*{G_{t'}(\cdot,x) - G_{\frac{n_N}{2}}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \\
			&\qquad + \sum_{j=n_1+1}^{n_N}\Norm*{G_{\frac{j}{2}}(\cdot,x)-G_{\frac{j-1}{2}}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:time_holder_end_case},\,\eqref{ineq:time_holder_proof_half}}{\lesssim}
			\frac{\Absolute*{t-\frac{n_1}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}{t^\beta}
			+\frac{\Absolute*{t'-\frac{n_N}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}{(n_N/2)^\beta}
			+\sum_{j=n_1+1}^{n_N}\frac{\Parentheses*{\frac12}^{\xi_1}}{\Parentheses*{\frac{j-1}{2}}^\beta} \\
			&\lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t-\frac{n_1}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}{t^\beta}
			+\frac{\Absolute*{t'-\frac{n_N}{2}}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}{t^\beta}
			+\frac{n_N-n_1}{t^\beta} \\
			&\le t^{-\beta}\Parentheses*{2\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1\wedge1}+2|t-t'|} \\
			&\lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}{t^\beta}.
		\end{align}
		Here we used that $(j-1)/2\ge n_1/2\ge t$ in the third inequality. 
		This proves the desired result.
		To prove \eqref{ineq:spatial_holder_long_time}, fix $T>0$. For $t\in(0,1]$, the estimate is exactly \eqref{cond:time_holder}. If $1<t\le T$, then the semigroup property, \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time} with $\lambda=0$ and \eqref{cond:time_holder} at  $t=1$ give
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x)-G_t(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			&\le \int_{\mathbb X} \Norm*{G_{t-1}(\cdot,y)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\Absolute*{G_1(y,x)-G_1(y,x')}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\lesssim d(x,x')^{\xi_2}\\
			&\lesssim \frac{d(x,x')^{\xi_2}}{t^\beta},
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$, and $t \in [1,T]$. This proves \eqref{ineq:spatial_holder_long_time}.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
We first show \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder m} holds for $\lambda =0$.  Let $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$  with $d(x,x') \le 1$, consider first for $t > 1$ that
		\begin{align}
			\int_0^{t} \int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_{t - s}(y,x) - G_{t - s}(y,x') \right|m(\mathrm dy)\mathrm ds & = I_1+I_2,
		\end{align}
		where 
		\begin{align}
			I_1 &{=}\int_{t-1}^t\int_{\mathbb X}\Absolute*{G_{t - s}(y,x) - G_{t-s}(y,x')}m(\mathrm dy)\mathrm ds  \\
			&{=}\int_0^1 \int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_{1-s}(y,x) - G_{1-s}(y,x') \right|m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds \\
			&{\overset{\eqref{cond:time_holder}}{\lesssim}} d(x,x')^{\xi_2},
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ and $t>1$, and 
		\begin{align}
			I_2 &= \int_0^{t - 1}\int_{\mathbb X}\left| G_{t - s}(y,x) - G_{t - s}(y,x')\right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds.
		\end{align}
		By the semigroup property and Tonelli's theorem, it holds uniformly in $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ and $t>1$ that
		\begin{align}
			I_2 
			&\,\,\,= \int_0^{t-1} \int_{\mathbb X} \left|\int_{\mathbb X} G_{t - s -1} (y,z) \left(G_{1}(z,x) - G_1(z,x') \right) m(\mathrm dz) \right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds\\
			&\,\,\,\le \int_0^{t-1}\int_{\mathbb X} \left(\int_{\mathbb X} G_{t-s-1}(y,z)m(\mathrm dy)\right)
			\left|G_1(z,x)-G_1(z,x')\right|m(\mathrm dz)\mathrm ds\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}}{\lesssim} \int_0^{t-1} e^{\kappa(t-s-1)}\mathrm ds
			\int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_1(z,x)-G_1(z,x')\right|m(\mathrm dz)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{cond:time_holder}}{\lesssim} d(x,x')^{\xi_2}\int_0^{t-1}e^{\kappa(t-s-1)}\mathrm ds,
		\end{align}
		where \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time} was used with $\lambda=0$.
		Note that the time integral is increasing in $t$ and bounded for each $t \ge 1$.
		For $t\in(0,1]$, the same spatial bound follows by integrating \eqref{cond:time_holder}, since $\beta<1$.
		Hence we see for each $T> 0$, it holds uniformly for $d(x,x') \le 1$ and $t \in [0,T]$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:hk spatial temporary}
			\int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_{t - s}(y,x) - G_{t - s}(y,x') \right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds \lesssim d(x,x')^{\xi_2}.
		\end{align}
		
		Fix $T>0$ and consider $\delta\in(0,1]$ and $t\in[0,T]$. Then
		\begin{align}
			\int_0^{t+\delta} \int_{\mathbb X} \left| G_{t+\delta - s}(y,x) - G_{t-s}(y,x) \right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds = I_1+ I_2,
		\end{align}
		where 
		\begin{align}
			I_1 = \int_{t}^{t+\delta} \int_{\mathbb X} G_{t+\delta-s}(y,x) m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds\overset{\eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}}{\lesssim} \delta,
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $\delta \in (0,1]$, and 
		\begin{align}
			I_2 = \int_0^t \int_{\mathbb X} \left| G_{t+\delta-s}(y,x) - G_{t-s} (y,x)\right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds.
		\end{align}
		
		By the change of variables  and \eqref{ineq:time_holder_long_time}, applied with time horizon $T+1$, we see it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $\delta \in (0,1]$ that
		\begin{align}
			I_2
			=\int_0^t\Norm*{G_{r+\delta}(\cdot,x)-G_r(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\mathrm dr
			\lesssim \delta^{\xi_1\wedge1}\int_0^t r^{-\beta}\mathrm dr
			\lesssim \delta^{\xi_1\wedge1},
		\end{align}
		where the endpoint $r=0$ is immaterial and the last inequality follows from $\beta\in[0,1)$. Since $\delta\in(0,1]$, we also have $\delta\le\delta^{\xi_1\wedge1}$.
		
		Therefore, for each $T>0$,  it holds uniformly for $(t,x) \in [0,T] \times {\mathbb X}$ and $\delta \in (0,1]$  that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:hk temporal temporary}
			\int_0^{t+\delta} \int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_{t +\delta- s}(y,x) - G_{t-s}(y,x) \right| m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds \lesssim \delta^{\xi_1\wedge 1}.
		\end{align}
		Therefore, we see by \eqref{ineq:hk temporal temporary}, \eqref{ineq:hk spatial temporary} and the triangular inequality that, for each $T>0$, it holds uniformly for $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$,  $t \in [0,T]$ and $\delta \in (0,1]$ that
		\begin{align}
			\int_0^{t+\delta} \int_{\mathbb X} \left|G_{t+\delta-s}(y,x') - G_{t-s}(y,x) \right|m(\mathrm dy) \mathrm ds \lesssim \delta^{\xi_1\wedge 1} + d(x,x')^{\xi_2}.
		\end{align}
		For $t,t'\in[0,T]$ with $|t-t'|>1$, \eqref{eq:GC}, the triangle inequality and \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time} with $\lambda=0$ give
		\begin{align*}
			\int_0^T\Norm*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)-G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\,\mathrm ds
			&\le \int_0^t\Norm*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\,\mathrm ds
			+\int_0^{t'}\Norm*{G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\,\mathrm ds\\
			&\lesssim \int_0^t e^{\kappa(t-s)}\,\mathrm ds
			+\int_0^{t'}e^{\kappa(t'-s)}\,\mathrm ds\\
			&\le 2T e^{\kappa T}
			\lesssim |t-t'|^{\xi_1\wedge1}.
		\end{align*}
		The last inequality uses $|t-t'|^{\xi_1\wedge1}\ge1$.
		Thus, by the triangle inequality, uniformly for $t,t'\in[0,T]$ and $x,x'\in{\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\le1$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:hk abs holder m lambda = 0}
			\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot; t',x')}_{L^1_{[0,T)} L^1_{\mathbb X}} \lesssim  \Absolute*{t - t'}^{\xi_1\wedge 1} + d(x,x')^{\xi_2},
		\end{align}
		which implies \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder m} when $\lambda=0$. To show \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder m} for $\lambda \in [0,1/2]$, we see by H\"{o}lder's inequality that
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{h(\cdot)^\lambda \Parentheses*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot; t',x')}}_{L^1_{[0,T)} L^1_{\mathbb X}} &\lesssim \Norm*{ \Absolute*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')}^{1/2} h(\cdot)^\lambda }_{L^2_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}}
			\\& \qquad \times \left\| \left|G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')\right|^{1/2} \right\|_{L^2_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}}. \label{eq:Phi1U}
		\end{align}
		
		Noticing $2\lambda \in [0,1]$, uniformly for every $t,t'\in [0,T]$ and $x,x'\in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x, x')\leq 1$,
		\begin{align}
			& \Norm*{\left|G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')\right|^{1/2}h(\cdot)^\lambda }^2_{L^2_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}}
			\\& = \int_0^T \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} \Absolute*{G(s,y;t,x) - G(s,y;t',x')} h(y)^{2\lambda} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\&\leq \int_0^T \left(\left\| G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) h(\cdot)^{2\lambda} \right\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} + \left\| G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x') h(\cdot)^{2\lambda} \right\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\right) \mathrm ds 
			\\&\label{eq:Phi1Ua}
			\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}} \lesssim 
			h(x)^{2\lambda} + h(x')^{2\lambda}
			\overset{\eqref{ineq:ref_regular}} \lesssim 
			h(x)^{2\lambda}. 
		\end{align}
		On the other hand, uniformly for every $t,t'$ in $[0,T]$ and $x,x'\in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\leq 1$,
		\begin{align}
			&\left\| \left|G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x')\right|^{1/2} \right\|_{L^2_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}} = \left\| G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot;t',x') \right\|^{1/2}_{L^1_{[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}}}
			\\
			& \overset{\eqref{ineq:hk abs holder m lambda = 0}} \lesssim 
			\left(|t-t'|^{\xi_1 \wedge 1} + d(x,x')^{\xi_2}\right)^{1/2} \leq |t-t'|^{(\xi_1\wedge 1)/2}+ d(x,x')^{\xi_2/2}
			\\
			\label{eq:Phi1Ub} 
			&\lesssim |t-t'|^{(\xi_1\wedge 1)\over 2}+ d(x,x')^{{\xi_2 \over 2}\wedge 1}.
		\end{align}
		Putting \eqref{eq:Phi1Ua} and \eqref{eq:Phi1Ub} back to \eqref{eq:Phi1U}, we obtain that uniformly for every $t,t'\in [0,T]$ and $x,x'\in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x, x')\leq 1$,
		\begin{align}
			\Norm*{h(\cdot)^\lambda \Parentheses*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) - G(\cdot,\cdot; t',x')}}_{L^1_{[0,T)} L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\lesssim \left(|t-t'|^{{{\xi_1 \wedge 1}\over 2}} + d(x,x')^{{\xi_2 \over 2}\wedge 1}\right) h(x)^{\lambda},
		\end{align}
		which is exactly \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder m}.
		
		To prove \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k}, recall that $\alpha\in(0,1)$ in \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda}, and that $\beta\in[0,1)$ and $\xi_1,\xi_2>0$ are given in \eqref{cond:time_holder}. Take $q\ge2$ large enough so that $0<\bar\alpha:=\alpha/2+\beta/q<1/2$ and $\xi_2/q<1$.
		Take $q' \geq 1$ so that $\frac{1}{q} + \frac{1}{q'} = 1$.
		Define $\tilde \xi_1:=\min(\frac{\xi_1\wedge 1}{q}, \frac{1-\alpha}{2}) \in (0,1)$ and $\tilde \xi_2 = \frac{\xi_2}{q} \in (0,1)$. Let $\lambda_1\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]\subset[0,1/q']$.
		Pay attention that \eqref{cond:exp_hk_lamda} holds with $\lambda$ being replaced by either $\lambda_1 q'$ or $\lambda_1$.
		
		Hence, it holds uniformly for  every $0< t \leq t'$ in $[0,T]$, and $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that,
		\begin{align}
			&\qquad
			\Norm*{\Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')} 
				h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K}
			\\ &\overset{\eqref{eq:G}}=\Norm*{\Absolute*{\int_{\mathbb X} G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,y)\Parentheses*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}(y, x)- G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(y,x')} m(\mathrm dy) }
				h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}
			}_{\mathcal H_K}
			\\  &\leq 
			\Norm*{\Parentheses*{\int_{\mathbb X} G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,y)\Absolute*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}(y, x)- G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(y,x')} m(\mathrm dy)}h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K} 
			\\ &\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:Min_H}}}\le \int_{\mathbb X} \Norm*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot, y) 
				h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}
			}_{\mathcal H_K} \Absolute*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}(y, x)- G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(y,x')} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\ & \overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}}{\lesssim } t^{-\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb X} h(y)^{\lambda_1} \Absolute*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}( y, x)- G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(y,x')} m(\mathrm dy). \label{ineq:continuity stochastic part middle step 1} 
		\end{align}
		
		Note uniformly in  $t, t'$ in $[0,T]$ and $x,x'\in {\mathbb X}$
		with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that
		\begin{align}
			&\int_{\mathbb X} h(y)^{\lambda_1}  \left|G_t(y,x) -G_{t'}(y,x') \right| m(\mathrm dy)
			\\&=\int_{\mathbb X} h(y)^{\lambda_1}  \left|G_t(y,x) -G_{t'}(y,x') \right|^\frac{1}{q'} \left|G_t(y,x) -G_{t'}(y,x') \right|^\frac{1}{q} m(\mathrm dy)
			\\&\overset{\text{H\"older}}{\leq} \Norm*{h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1} \Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x)- G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{\frac{1}{q'}}}_{L^{q'}_{{\mathbb X}}} \Norm*{\Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x)- G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{\frac{1}{q}}}_{L^{q}_{{\mathbb X}}}
			\\&=\Norm*{h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1 q'} \Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x)- G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}}^{1\over q'}_{L^1_{{\mathbb X}}} \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x)- G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{{\mathbb X}}}
			\\&\le \Norm*{h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1 q'} \Parentheses*{ G_t(\cdot,x)+G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}}^{1\over q'}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\\&\overset{\text{Minkowski}}\le \Parentheses*{\Norm*{h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1 q'}  G_t(\cdot,x)}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} + \Norm*{h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1 q'} G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}}^{1\over q'} \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\\&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}}\lesssim  \Parentheses*{h(x)^{\lambda_1 q'} +h(x')^{\lambda_1 q'}}^{\frac{1}{q'}} \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			\\&\overset{\eqref{ineq:ref_regular}}\lesssim  h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Norm*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}. \label{eq:Gtt}
		\end{align}
		Therefore, by the triangular inequality, we have uniformly for every 
		$0< t \leq t'$ in $[0,T]$, and $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that, 

		\begin{align}
			&\Norm*{\Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,x')} 
				h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}
			}_{\mathcal H_K}
			\\ & {\overset{\eqref{ineq:continuity stochastic part middle step 1}}{\lesssim}} t^{-\alpha/2}\int_{\mathbb X} h(y)^{\lambda_1} \left|G_{\frac{t}{2}}( y, x)- G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(y,x') \right|m(\mathrm dy)
			\\ & {\overset{\eqref{eq:Gtt}}{\lesssim} }t^{-\alpha/2} h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Norm*{ 
				G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x) - 
				G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x')
			}^{1\over q}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} 
			\\& {\overset{\text{
					Minkowski
			}}{\leq}} t^{-\alpha/2} h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{\Norm*{ G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x) - G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x')}_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} + \Norm*{G_{\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x') - G_{t'-\frac{t}{2}}(\cdot,x') }_{L^1_{\mathbb X}} }^{1\over q}
			\\& {\overset{
			\eqref{ineq:spatial_holder_long_time},\ \eqref{ineq:time_holder_long_time}}{\lesssim}}t^{-\alpha/2} h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{\frac{d(x,x')^{\xi_2}}{\Parentheses*{t/2}^\beta} + \frac{\Absolute*{t'-t}^{\xi_1 \wedge 1}}{\Parentheses*{t/2}^\beta}}^{\frac{1}{q}}
			\\& \lesssim t^{-\bar\alpha} h(x)^{\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{\xi_2} + \Absolute*{t'-t}^{\xi_1 \wedge 1}}^{\frac{1}{q}}. \label{eq:G-GS}
		\end{align}
		On one hand, uniformly for $0<t\leq t'$ in $[0,T]$, and $x,x'\in{\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\leq1$, since $2\bar\alpha\in(0,1)$,
		\begin{align}
			&\int_0^t \Norm*{\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} 
				h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}
			}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds 
			\\& \overset{\eqref{eq:G-GS}}\lesssim \int_0^t (t-s)^{-2\bar\alpha}h(x)^{2\lambda_1}\Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{\xi_2}+\Absolute*{t'-t}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}^{\frac{2}{q}}\,\mathrm ds
			\\& \lesssim t^{1-2\bar\alpha}h(x)^{2\lambda_1}\Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{\xi_2}+\Absolute*{t'-t}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}^{\frac{2}{q}}. \label{eq:Gtq}
		\end{align}
		On the other hand, uniformly in $0<t\leq t'$ in $[0,T],$ and $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x')\leq 1$, 
		\begin{align}
			\int_{t}^{t'} \Norm*{G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x') h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds 
			& \overset{\text{Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}}}\lesssim \int_{t}^{t'} (t'-s)^{-\alpha} h(x')^{2\lambda_1} \mathrm ds \\
			&= h(x')^{2\lambda_1} \int_0^{t'-t} r^{-\alpha} \mathrm dr \\
			&\lesssim h(x')^{2\lambda_1} |t'-t|^{1-\alpha}\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:ref_regular}}{\lesssim} h(x)^{2\lambda_1} |t'-t|^{1-\alpha}. \label{eq:Gt'q}
		\end{align}
		Now, we see uniformly in $0 <t\leq t'$ in $[0,T]$, and $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that   
		\begin{align}
			&\int_0^T \Norm*{\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:GC}}=\int_0^t \Norm*{\Absolute*{G_{t-s}(\cdot,x) - G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')} h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds +  \int_{t}^{t'} \Norm*{G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')h(\cdot)^{\lambda_1}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds
			\\&\overset{\eqref{eq:Gtq} \& \eqref{eq:Gt'q}}\lesssim h(x)^{2\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{\xi_2} + \Absolute*{t'-t}^{\xi_1\wedge1}}^{\frac{2}{q}} + h(x)^{2\lambda_1} |t'-t|^{1-\alpha}
			\\&\lesssim h(x)^{2\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{\frac{2\xi_2}{q}}+ |t'-t|^{\frac{2\Parentheses*{\xi_1\wedge1}}{q}} + |t'-t|^{1-\alpha}}
			\\& \lesssim h(x)^{2\lambda_1} \Parentheses*{d(x,x')^{2\tilde \xi_2}+ |t'-t|^{2\tilde \xi_1}}.
		\end{align}
		This proves \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k}.

		It remains to prove \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted}. Let us fix $T>0$. Since $q\ge2$, its conjugate exponent satisfies $q'\le2$, and hence $\lambda q'\le1$ for every $\lambda\in[0,1/2]$. Thus, by \eqref{eq:Gtq} and \eqref{eq:Gt'q}, with $\lambda_1$ replaced by $\lambda$, it holds uniformly in $0<t\leq t'$ in $[0,T]$, and $x,x' \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,x') \le 1$ that
		\begin{align*}
			&\int_0^{t'}\left\|\left|G_{t-s}(\cdot,x)-G_{t'-s}(\cdot,x')\right|h(\cdot)^\lambda\right\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2\,\mathrm ds\\
			\lesssim &h(x)^{2\lambda}\left[t^{1-2\bar\alpha}\left(d(x,x')^{2\xi_2/q}+|t-t'|^{2(\xi_1\wedge1)/q}\right)+|t-t'|^{1-\alpha}\right].
		\end{align*}
		Set
		\[
			\alpha_0:=\frac{1+2\bar\alpha}{2},\qquad
			\xi:=1\wedge\frac{2\xi_2}{q}\wedge\frac{2(\xi_1\wedge1)}{q}\wedge(\alpha_0-\alpha).
		\]
		Since $t\le t'$ and $|t-t'|\le t'$, this proves \eqref{ineq:hk abs holder k weighted}; the same estimate also covers $t=0$.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{cor:hk_abs}
		For each $T>0$, there
		exist $\xi\in (0,1]$ and $\alpha_0\in(0,1)$ so that the following inequality holds uniformly in $t,t' \in (0,T]$ and $x,y \in {\mathbb X}$ with $d(x,y) \le 1$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{
			\Absolute*{G_t(\cdot,x) - G_{t'}(\cdot,y)}
			}_{\mathcal H_K}^2 \lesssim \frac{\Absolute*{t-t'}^\xi + d(x,y)^\xi}{\Parentheses*{t\wedge t'}^{
			\alpha_0}},
		\end{align*}
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
Let $T\geq1$ and let $j\leq T$ be a non-negative integer. Then, for any $\lambda>0$ and sufficiently large $p\geq1$, the following inequality holds uniformly in $x\in{\mathbb X}$:
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb E}\left[ |X(j,x)|^p\right] &\lesssim {\mathbb E}\left[|X(0,x)|^p \right] + \sum_{k = 1}^{j} {\mathbb E}\left[\left|X(k - 1,x)- X(k,x) \right|^p \right]\\
			&\lesssim h(x)^{\lambda}.
		\end{align}
		Let $d_\mathcal S$ be the metric on $\mathbb{R}_+\times{\mathbb X}$ defined by
		\begin{align}
			d_\mathcal S((t,y),(t',y')):=|t-t'|^{\tilde \xi_1}+d(y,y')^{\tilde \xi_2},
		\end{align}
		and, for each non-negative integer $j$ and $x\in{\mathbb X}$, set $\mathcal S_{j,x}:=[j,j+1]\times B_1(x)$. Let $b>0$ be as in \eqref{ineq:entropy bound} for $R=1$ and set $\kappa:=1/\tilde \xi_1+b/\tilde \xi_2$. By Lemma \ref{lem:product metric entropy}, each $\mathcal S_{j,x}$ is totally bounded under $d_\mathcal S$ and
		\begin{align}
			D(\mathcal S_{j,x},d_\mathcal S;\varepsilon)\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\kappa}
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $j$, $x$ and $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$. Hence, using \eqref{cond:tight_moment} with time horizon $T+1$, we may apply \cite[Theorem~1.1]{kratschmer2022kolmogorov} to $\mathcal S_{j,x}$ for each $j$ and $x$.
		Patching the resulting modifications over the unit time intervals and the countable spatial cover in \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}, we obtain a single locally H\"older-continuous modification, which we still denote by $X$, such that, for any $\lambda>0$, sufficiently large $p\geq1$ and $\varrho\in(0,1-\kappa/p)$,
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:holder norm bound temp}
			{\mathbb E}\left[\sup_{(t,y),(t',y')\in\mathcal S_{j,x}\atop(t,y)\neq(t',y')}\frac{\Absolute*{X(t,y)-X(t',y')}^p}{\Parentheses*{\Absolute*{t-t'}^{\tilde \xi_1}+d(y,y')^{\tilde \xi_2}}^{\varrho p}}\right]\lesssim h(x)^\lambda
		\end{align}
		uniformly in $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and non-negative integers $j\leq T$.
		From now on, we work with this modification. Therefore, uniformly in $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and non-negative integers $j\leq T$,
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb E}\left[ \sup_{y \in B_1(x)\atop s \in [j,j+1]} \left| X(s,y)\right|^p \right] & \lesssim  {\mathbb E}\left[\left|X(j,x) \right|^p \right] + {\mathbb E} \left[\sup_{y \in B_1(x)\atop s \in [j,j+1]} \left| X(s,y) - X(j,x) \right|^p \right]\nonumber\\
			&\lesssim h(x)^\lambda\label{ineq:tight_x0_bound}.
		\end{align}
		Let $\{x_i\}_{i \in \mathcal I}\subset {\mathbb X}$ and $\lambda_0 >0$ be as in condition \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}. By Markov's inequality we see for each $\lambda >0$, it holds uniformly in $A>0$ that
		\begin{align}
			{\mathbb P} \left(\sup_{s \le T} \|X(s)\|_{(\lambda)} > A \right)&\le   \sum_{0 \le j \le T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I} {\mathbb P} \left( \sup_{t\in [j,j+1]\atop x \in B_1(x_i)}  \left|X(t,x) \right|^p h(x)^{-\lambda p} \ge  A^p \right)\\
			&\le  \sum_{0 \le j \le T}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I} {\mathbb P} \left( \sup_{t \in [j,j+1]\atop x \in B_1(x_i)}  \left|X(t,x) \right|^p  \ge  A^p \inf_{x \in B_1(x_i)}h(x)^{\lambda p}\right)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:tight_x0_bound}}{\lesssim} \sum_{i\in\mathcal I}\frac{1}{A^p}h(x_i)^{\lambda}  \sup_{x \in B_1(x_i)}h(x)^{-\lambda p} \\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:ref_regular}}{\lesssim} \frac{1}{A^p}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I} h(x_i)^{-\lambda p + \lambda}.
		\end{align}
		By \eqref{ineq:faster_than_ms}, we may choose $p$ to be large enough so that $-\lambda p+\lambda <- \lambda_0$, which makes the last sum finite. Therefore, we see by the continuity of the probability measure that
		\begin{align*}
			\mathbb P \Parentheses*{\sup_{t \in [0,T]}\Norm*{X(t)}_{(\lambda)} < \infty} = 1.
		\end{align*}
		Fix $T\geq1$, $\lambda>0$ and $x_0\in{\mathbb X}$, and work on a probability-one event on which
		\begin{align*}
			M:=\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\Norm*{X(s)}_{(\lambda/2)}<\infty.
		\end{align*}
		For $t,t'\in[0,T]$ and $R\geq1$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{X(t)-X(t')}_{(\lambda)}
			&\leq\sup_{x\in\overline{B_R(x_0)}}\Absolute*{X(t,x)-X(t',x)}
			+2M\sup_{x\in B_R(x_0)^c}h(x)^{-\lambda/2}.
		\end{align*}
		The first term converges to zero as $t' \to t$ by joint continuity and compactness of $\overline{B_R(x_0)}$. If ${\mathbb X}$ has finite diameter, the second term vanishes for all sufficiently large $R$. Otherwise, we see by Lemma \ref{lem:ref_grow} that the second term can be made arbitrarily small by choosing $R>0$ large enough. 
		Therefore, $t \mapsto X(t,\cdot)$ is continuous under $\Norm*{\cdot}_{(\lambda)}$ on $[0,T]$. Finally, we may take a countable intersection over $T\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lambda=n^{-1}$ for $n\in\mathbb{N}$ to conclude the desired result.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Suppose that both $(B,\Sigma)$ and $(B',\Sigma')$ are Duhamel-integrable pairs and that $B'$ is a predictable modification of $B$.
			Then, for each $y\in{\mathbb X}$, $B(s,y,\omega)=B'(s,y,\omega)$ for $\Lambda\otimes\mathbb P$-almost every $(s,\omega)\in[0,T]\times\Omega$.
			Therefore, for each $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$, we see by Tonelli's theorem that
			\begin{align*}
				&\mathbb E\left[\int_0^t\mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} G(s,y;t,x)\Absolute*{B(s,y)-B'(s,y)}m(\mathrm dy)\right]\\
				&\quad=\int_0^t\mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} G(s,y;t,x)\mathbb E\left[\Absolute*{B(s,y)-B'(s,y)}\right]m(\mathrm dy)=0,
			\end{align*}
			which implies  $\Psi_1(B')$ is a modification of $\Psi_1(B)$.
			
			Now suppose that $\Sigma'$ is a predictable modification of $\Sigma$, and let $\widetilde\Sigma(s,y):=\Sigma(s,y)-\Sigma'(s,y)$ for $(s,y)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$.
			For each $y\in{\mathbb X}$, Fubini's theorem gives
			\begin{align*}
				\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(r,y)}_{L^2_\Omega}=0,\qquad \text{for $\Lambda$-almost every $r\in[0,T]$}.
			\end{align*}
			Fix $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$, and for $y,z\in{\mathbb X}$, define
			\begin{align}
				I_1(y,z;t,x)&:=\Parentheses*{\int_0^tG(r,y;t,x)G(r,z;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(r,y)}_{L^2_\Omega}^2\mathrm dr}^{1/2},\\
				I_2(y,z;t,x)&:=\Parentheses*{\int_0^tG(r,y;t,x)G(r,z;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(r,z)}_{L^2_\Omega}^2\mathrm dr}^{1/2}.
			\end{align}
			It follows that $I_1(y,z;t,x)=I_2(y,z;t,x)=0$ for every $y,z\in{\mathbb X}$.
			By Tonelli's theorem and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, we see
			\begin{align}
				&\int_0^t\Norm*{G(s,\cdot;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,\cdot)}_{L^2_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds\\
				&=\int_0^t\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}G(s,y;t,x)G(s,z;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,y)}_{L^2_\Omega}\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,z)}_{L^2_\Omega}K(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dz)\mathrm ds\\
				&=\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}\int_0^tG(s,y;t,x)G(s,z;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,y)}_{L^2_\Omega}\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,z)}_{L^2_\Omega}\mathrm dsK(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dz)\\
				&\leq\iint_{{\mathbb X}^2}I_1(y,z;t,x)I_2(y,z;t,x)K(\mathrm dy,\mathrm dz)=0.
			\end{align}
			Therefore, by the linearity of the stochastic integral and Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs},
			\begin{align*}
				\Norm*{\Psi_2(\Sigma)(t,x)-\Psi_2(\Sigma')(t,x)}_{L^2_\Omega}^2
				&=\Norm*{\iint_0^tG(s,y;t,x)\widetilde\Sigma(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy)}_{L^2_\Omega}^2\\
				&\lesssim\int_0^t\Norm*{G(s,\cdot;t,x)\Norm*{\widetilde\Sigma(s,\cdot)}_{L^2_\Omega}}_{\mathcal H_K}^2\mathrm ds=0.
			\end{align*}
			Hence, $\Psi_2(\Sigma')$ is a modification of $\Psi_2(\Sigma)$.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
Fix arbitrary $\lambda \in \mathbb{R}$ and $p \ge 1$, and observe that we may assume without loss of generality that $A = 0$. Clearly, $\Norm*{\cdot}_{\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}}$ is a norm on $\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}$, hence it is enough to show the space $(\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}, \Norm*{\cdot}_{\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}})$ is complete.
			
			\setcounter{step}{0}\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. }
			Suppose now $\{X^{(n)}\}_{n \in \mathbb N}$ is Cauchy in $(\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}, \Norm*{\cdot}_{\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p}})$. Then there exists $\{X_n\}_{n \in \mathbb N}$ in $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$ so that 
			\begin{align}
				[X_n]_{\mathrm p} = X^{(n)} \qquad \text{ in }\mathbb M^\sharp_{\lambda,p},
			\end{align}
			and $\{X_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy in $(\mathbb M_{\lambda,p},\Norm*{\cdot}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}})$. We will first show the sequence $\{X_n\}_{n \in \mathbb N}$ has a predictable limit $X$ so that $\lim_{n \to \infty}\Norm*{X_n - X}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p}} = 0$.
			
			
			
			Let $\mathcal P_T:=\mathcal P|_{\Omega\times[0,T]}$, and equip $\widehat\Omega:=\Omega\times[0,T]$ with $\mathcal P_T$ and the probability measure $\widehat{\mathbb P}:=T^{-1}(\mathbb P\otimes\Lambda)|_{\mathcal P_T}$.
			For every $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and $n,m\in\mathbb{N}$, we have
			\begin{align*}
				\Norm*{X_n(\cdot,x)-X_m(\cdot,x)}_{L^p(\widehat{\mathbb P})}
				\leq h(x)^\lambda\Norm*{X_n-X_m}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}.
			\end{align*}
			Hence, for every fixed $x\in{\mathbb X}$, the sequence $\{X_n(\cdot,x)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is Cauchy in $L^p(\widehat{\mathbb P})$ and therefore Cauchy in $\widehat{\mathbb P}$-probability.
			Regarding $x\in{\mathbb X}$ as a parameter, it follows from \cite[Lemma~5.6 and Corollary~5.33]{MR4226142} that there exists a $\mathcal P_T\otimes\mathscr B_{\mathbb X}$-measurable random field $X$ such that $X_n(\cdot,x)$ converges to $X(\cdot,x)$ in $\widehat{\mathbb P}$-probability for every $x\in{\mathbb X}$.
			In particular, $X$ is jointly predictable.
			
			\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. } We now prove that $X_n$ converges to $X$ in the $\Norm*{\cdot}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}$ norm.
			For $N\in\mathbb{N}$, set
			\begin{align*}
				r_N:=\sup_{n,m\geq N}\Norm*{X_n-X_m}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}},
			\end{align*}
			so that $r_N\downarrow0$ as $N\to\infty$.
			Fix $x\in{\mathbb X}$ and $n\geq N$.
			By \cite[Lemma~5.2]{MR4226142}, there exists a subsequence $\{X_{m_k}(\cdot,x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}$, with $m_k\geq N$, which converges to $X(\cdot,x)$ $\widehat{\mathbb P}$-almost surely.
			Therefore, Fubini's theorem and Fatou's lemma imply that, for $\Lambda$-almost every $t\in[0,T]$,
			\begin{align*}
				h(x)^{-p\lambda}\mathbb E\left[\Absolute*{X_n(t,x)-X(t,x)}^p\right]
				&\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}h(x)^{-p\lambda}\mathbb E\left[\Absolute*{X_n(t,x)-X_{m_k}(t,x)}^p\right]\\
				&\leq r_N^p.
			\end{align*}
			Taking the $p$-th root, the essential supremum in $t$, and then the supremum in $x$, we obtain
			\begin{align*}
				\Norm*{X_n-X}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}\leq r_N,\qquad n\geq N.
			\end{align*}
			Since $r_N\to0$, it follows that $X\in\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$ and $\lim_{n\to\infty}\Norm*{X_n-X}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}=0$.
			
			\refstepcounter{step}\emph{Step \thestep. } We see $[X]_{\mathrm p} \in \mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^\sharp$, and 
			\begin{align}
				\lim_{n \to \infty} \Norm*{X^{(n)}-[X]_{\mathrm p}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^\sharp} &= \lim_{n \to \infty} \Norm*{[X_n]_{\mathrm p}-[X]_{\mathrm p}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}^\sharp} \\
				&=\lim_{n \to \infty}\Norm*{X_n - X}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}\\
				&=0,
			\end{align}
			which concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
We first show that $(B_U, \Sigma_U)$ is a Duhamel-integrable pair.
		Note that the predictability of $B_U$ and $\Sigma_U$ is given by the fact that the coefficients $\sigma(t,x,u),\, b(t,x,u)$ are predictable functions on $\Omega\times\mathbb R_+\times{\mathbb X}\times\mathbb R$ by \eqref{eq:DC}--\eqref{eq:NC}, and $U(t,x)$ itself is a  predictable random field.
		Hence, it is enough to show for some $p \ge 2$ that
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:bcc D pair}
			\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot; t,x) B_U}_{L^1_{[0,T]}L_{\mathbb X}^1}}_{L^p_\Omega} + \Norm*{ \Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x)\Sigma_U}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K} }_{L^p_{\Omega}}<\infty.
		\end{align}
		Observe by Minkowski's inequality that it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $A \ge 1$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot; t,x) B_U}_{L^1_{[0,T]}L_{\mathbb X}^1}}_{L^p_\Omega}&\,\,=\Norm*{\int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X}  G(s,y;t,x) |B_U(s,y)|m(\mathrm dy)}_{L^p_\Omega}\\
			&\,\,\le \int_0^t \mathrm ds  \int_{\mathbb X}  G(s,y;t,x) \Norm*{B_U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega}m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\overset{\eqref{eq:LiG}}{\lesssim}\int_0^t \mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} G(s,y;t,x) \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U(s,y)}_{L^p_\Omega}} m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\,\,\le \int_0^t\mathrm ds \int_{\mathbb X} e^{sA}G(s,y;t,x) h(y)^{\lambda} m(\mathrm dy) \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p,A}}}\\
			&\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:hk longtime}, \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}}}{\lesssim}h(x)^\lambda \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}\int_0^t \mathrm ds \,e^{sA} \\
			&\,\,\le A^{-1} e^{At} h(x)^\lambda \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}.
		\end{align*}
		
		
		Following the proof of Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs}, we see it holds uniformly in $(t,x)\in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $A \ge 0$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot; t,x) \Sigma_U}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K}}_{L^p_\Omega}^2 
			&\,\,\le \int_0^t \mathrm ds \Norm*{G(s,\cdot; t,x) \Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,\cdot)}_{L^{p}_\Omega} }_{\mathcal H_K}^2\\
			&\,\,=\int_0^t \mathrm ds \Norm*{G(s,\cdot; t,x) h(\cdot)^\lambda h(\cdot)^{-\lambda} \Norm*{\Sigma_U(s,\cdot)}_{L^{p}_\Omega} }_{\mathcal H_K}^2\\
			&\,\,\le \Norm*{\Sigma_U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p,A}}^2\int_0^t\mathrm ds\, e^{2As}\Norm*{G(s,\cdot; t,x) h(\cdot)^\lambda}_{\mathcal H_K}^2  \\
			&\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:hk longtime}, \eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}}}{\lesssim} \Norm*{\Sigma_U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}^2 h(x)^{2\lambda}\int_0^t\mathrm ds \, \Parentheses*{e^{2As} (t-s)^{-\widetilde\alpha}}.
		\end{align*}
		Note for every $\widetilde\alpha \in [0,1)$, it holds uniformly in $A,t >0$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\int_0^t e^{2As}(t-s)^{-\widetilde\alpha} \mathrm ds  \lesssim A^{\widetilde\alpha - 1} e^{2At}.
		\end{align*}
		Hence it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $A \ge 1$ that 
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x) \Sigma_U}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K}}_{L^p_\Omega} \lesssim  A^\frac{\widetilde\alpha -1}{2}e^{A t}\Norm*{\Sigma_U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}} h(x)^\lambda \lesssim A^\frac{\widetilde\alpha - 1}{2} e^{At} h(x)^\lambda\Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}.
		\end{align*}
		Hence \eqref{ineq:bcc D pair} holds. More precisely, for each $p \ge 2$, the following inequality holds uniformly in $A \ge 1$ and $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ 
		\begin{align}\label{ineq:bcc D pair explicit}
			&\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot; t,x) B_U}_{L^1_{[0,T]}L_{\mathbb X}^1}}_{L^p_\Omega} + \Norm*{ \Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x)\Sigma_U}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K} }_{L^p_{\Omega}}\\
			\lesssim & A^{\frac{\widetilde\alpha - 1}{2}}h(x)^\lambda e^{A t} \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda, p,A}}}.
		\end{align}
		
		
		We next show that $\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)$ admits a predictable modification in $\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$.
		
		We first verify predictability. By \eqref{cond:ctem continuous}, $\Psi_0(g)$ is deterministic and jointly continuous.
		For $n\in\mathbb{N}$, let $\delta_n:=2^{-n}T$ and define $\Psi^{(n)}(0,x):=g(x)$. For $t\in(j\delta_n,(j+1)\delta_n]$, where $j=0,\ldots,2^n-1$, set
		\begin{align}\label{eq:predictable_approximation}
			\Psi^{(n)}(t,x)
			&:=(P_t^*g)(x)+\int_0^{j\delta_n}\mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} G(s,y;t,x)B_U(s,y)m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\qquad+\iint_0^{j\delta_n}G(s,y;t,x)\Sigma_U(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy).
		\end{align}

		Set $S:=(j\delta_n,(j+1)\delta_n]\times{\mathbb X}$. The drift integral in the definition of $\Psi^{(n)}(t,x)$ has an $\mathscr F_{j\delta_n}\otimes\mathscr B(S)$-measurable version. The stochastic integral vanishes when $j=0$, so suppose that $j\ge1$.

		By Lemma \ref{lem:H0}, the standard elementary predictable approximations of $G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x)\Sigma_U$ on $[0,j\delta_n]$, using  finite-dimensional projections, can be chosen jointly measurable in $(t,x)\in S$. 
		Hence, by \cite[Corollary~5.33]{MR4226142}, the third term on the right hand side of \eqref{eq:predictable_approximation} 
		has an $\mathscr F_{j\delta_n}\otimes\mathscr B(S)$-measurable version.
		Since $\mathscr F_{j\delta_n}\otimes\mathscr B((j\delta_n,(j+1)\delta_n])\subset\mathcal P_T$ on $(j\delta_n,(j+1)\delta_n]$, patching the measurable versions of the drift and stochastic integrals over these time intervals, together with the deterministic term $(P_t^*g)(x)$ and the value $g(x)$ at $t=0$, gives a $\mathcal P_T\otimes\mathscr B_{\mathbb X}$-measurable version of $\Psi^{(n)}$, where $\mathcal P_T:=\mathcal P|_{\Omega\times[0,T]}$. We use this predictable version of $\Psi^{(n)}$ below.

		By Minkowski's inequality and Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs}, using \eqref{eq:LiG} and \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}--\eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}, we have, uniformly in $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\Psi^{(n)}}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}
			\lesssim \Norm*{g}_{(\lambda)}+1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}<\infty.
		\end{align*}
		Together with the predictability established above, this proves that $\Psi^{(n)}\in\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}$.

		For $t\in(j\delta_n,(j+1)\delta_n]$, \eqref{eq:Psi} and \eqref{eq:predictable_approximation} give
		\begin{align*}
			\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)(t,x)-\Psi^{(n)}(t,x)
			&=\int_{j\delta_n}^t\mathrm ds\int_{\mathbb X} G(s,y;t,x)B_U(s,y)m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\qquad\quad+\iint_{j\delta_n}^tG(s,y;t,x)\Sigma_U(s,y)W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy).
		\end{align*}
		By \eqref{eq:LiG}, Minkowski's inequality, Lemma \ref{cor:mtg+mink_cs}, and \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}--\eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}, it follows uniformly in $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $A\geq1$, and $(t,x)\in(0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align*}
			&e^{-At}h(x)^{-\lambda}\Norm*{\Psi^{(n)}(t,x)-\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}\\
			&\lesssim\Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}
			\left[\int_{j\delta_n}^t e^{-A(t-s)}\mathrm ds
			+\left(\int_{j\delta_n}^t e^{-2A(t-s)}(t-s)^{-\widetilde\alpha}\mathrm ds\right)^{1/2}\right]\\
			&\lesssim\Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}\left(\delta_n+\delta_n^{(1-\widetilde\alpha)/2}\right).
		\end{align*}
		The last inequality uses $0<t-j\delta_n\le\delta_n$ and $\widetilde\alpha<1$. The estimate also holds at $t=0$, since both random fields equal $g(x)$. 
		Since $\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\delta_n+\delta_n^{(1-\widetilde\alpha)/2})<\infty$, we see $\Psi^{(n)}(t,x)$ converges almost surely to $\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)(t,x)$. Taking the pointwise limit of $\Psi^{(n)}$ wherever it exists and is finite, and setting the field equal to zero elsewhere, yields a $\mathcal P_T\otimes\mathscr B_{\mathbb X}$-measurable modification of $\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)$ whose value at $t=0$ is $g(x)$. We use this modification below without changing notation.

		It remains to bound $\Norm*{\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}$. For $p\ge2$, the following estimate holds uniformly in $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times{\mathbb X}$ and $A\ge1$:
		\begin{align*}
			&\Norm*{\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega}\\
			&\quad\lesssim \Absolute*{P^*_t g(x)} +\Norm*{\Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot; t,x) B_U}_{L^1_{[0,T]}L_{\mathbb X}^1}}_{L^p_\Omega}+ \Norm*{ \Norm*{G(\cdot,\cdot;t,x)\Sigma_U}_{L^2_{[0,T]}\mathcal H_K} }_{L^p_{\Omega}}\\
			&\quad\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:hk longtime}, \eqref{ineq:hk_m long time}},\eqref{ineq:bcc D pair explicit}}{\lesssim} h(x)^\lambda + \Parentheses*{A^\frac{\widetilde\alpha-1}{2}e^{At}} h(x)^\lambda  \Parentheses*{1+\Norm*{U}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}}}.
		\end{align*}
		Taking $A=1$ in this estimate gives $\Norm*{\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p}}<\infty$.
		
		Finally, to prove the difference estimate, we write
		\begin{align*}
			\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U) - \Psi(g,B_V,\Sigma_V) &= \int_0^t\mathrm ds  \int_{\mathbb X}  G(s,y; t,x) \widetilde B_{U - V}(s,y) m(\mathrm dy)\\
			&\qquad + \iint_0^t G(s,y; t,x) \widetilde \Sigma_{U - V}(s,y) W(\mathrm ds,\mathrm dy),
		\end{align*}
		where 
		\begin{gather*}
			\widetilde B_{X}(s,y):= \widetilde b(s,y,X(s,y)):=b(s,y,X(s,y)+V(s,y)) - b(s,y,V(s,y)),\\ \widetilde \Sigma _{X}(s,y):= \widetilde\sigma(s,y,X(s,y)):=\sigma(s,y,X(s,y)+V(s,y)) - \sigma(s,y,V(s,y)).
		\end{gather*}
		We see $\widetilde b$ and $\widetilde \sigma$ satisfy \eqref{ineq:g_lip} and 
		\begin{align*}
			|\widetilde b(t,x,u)| + |\widetilde \sigma(t,x,u)| \lesssim |u|,\qquad \text{ uniformly in } (t,x,u) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X} \times  \mathbb{R}.
		\end{align*}
		Hence, by repeating the above argument, we see it holds uniformly in $(t,x) \in [0,T]\times {\mathbb X}$ and $A \ge 1$ that
		\begin{align*}
			\Norm*{\Psi(g,B_U,\Sigma_U)(t,x) - \Psi(g,B_V,\Sigma_V)(t,x)}_{L^p_\Omega} \lesssim \Parentheses*{A^\frac{\widetilde\alpha - 1}{2} e^{At} } h(x)^\lambda \Norm*{U - V}_{\mathbb M_{\lambda,p,A}},
		\end{align*}
		which implies the desired inequality.
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- lemma-30: body_md -->
\label{lem:poisson moment bounds}
		Let \(Z\sim \operatorname{Pois}(\lambda)\), \(\lambda>0\). For every positive even integer \(p=2m\), \(m\in\mathbb{N}\),
		there exists \(C_p<\infty\) such that
		\[
		\mathbb E|Z-\lambda|^p
		\le C_p\bigl(\lambda+\lambda^{p/2}\bigr).
		\]
<!-- end lemma-30 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
Let \(p=2m\) be a positive even integer. By \cite[Lemma 3.1]{privault2011generalized},
		\[
		\mathbb E[(Z-\lambda)^{2m}]
		=
		\sum_{a=0}^{2m}\lambda^a S_2(2m,a),
		\]
		where \(S_2(n,a)\) denotes the number of partitions of an \(n\)-element set into \(a\) blocks
		of size at least \(2\). Hence \(S_2(2m,a)=0\) for \(a>m\). Indeed, if there are $a$ number of sets of size at least $2$, then the total elements is at least $2a$. Hence, for each $m \in \mathbb{N}$, there exists $C_m>0$, independent of $\lambda>0$, so that 
		\[
		\mathbb E|Z-\lambda|^{2m}
		=
		\sum_{a=1}^{m}\lambda^a S_2(2m,a)
		\le C_m(\lambda+\lambda^m).
		\]
		This proves the lemma.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
<!-- proof-43: body_md -->
(1) Let $t>0$, $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$. We see by Lemma \ref{lem:H0}, Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime} and {Jensen's} inequality that uniformly in $x \in {\mathbb X}$, $t >0 $ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$,
		\begin{align}
			\|r_t^\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2 &\le \left(e^{-t/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!}\|G(n\varepsilon,\cdot,x)\|_{\mathcal H_K} \right)^2\\
			&\,\,\le e^{-t/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!} \|G(n\varepsilon,\cdot,x)\|_{\mathcal H_K}^2\\
			&\overset{\eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}}{\lesssim} e^{-t/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!} (n\varepsilon)^{-\alpha} e^{\kappa \varepsilon n}\\
			&\,\, =\exp \left(t\left(e^{\kappa \varepsilon} - 1 \right) /\varepsilon \right) e^{-te^{\kappa \varepsilon}/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{\left(e^{\kappa \varepsilon}t/ \varepsilon\right)^n}{n!} (n\varepsilon)^{-\alpha},
		\end{align}
		for some $\alpha \in (0,1)$. Hence, by {Jensen's} inequality,
		\begin{align}
			e^{-te^{\kappa \varepsilon}/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{\left(e^{\kappa \varepsilon}t/ \varepsilon\right)^n}{n!} (n\varepsilon)^{-\alpha} &\le \left (e^{-t e^{\kappa \varepsilon}/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{(e^{\kappa \varepsilon}t/\varepsilon)^{n}}{n!} (n\varepsilon)^{-1} \right)^{\alpha}\\
			&{\le}\left(e^{-te^{\kappa \varepsilon}/\varepsilon}\sum_{n=1}^\infty \frac{\left(e^{\kappa \varepsilon}t/\varepsilon \right)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!} \frac{n+1}{n}t^{-1}\right)^\alpha \\
			& \le 2^\alpha t^{-\alpha}.
		\end{align}
		Finally, observe that there is $\kappa' \in \mathbb{R}$ so that for $t \ge 0$,
		\begin{align}
			\sup_{ \varepsilon \in (0,1]} \exp \left(t \left(e^{\kappa \varepsilon} - 1 \right) /\varepsilon\right) \le  e^{\kappa ' t}.
		\end{align}
		Collecting all terms, we see it holds uniformly in $x \in {\mathbb X}$, $t >0$ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ that
		\begin{align}
			\|r_t^\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\|_{\mathcal H_K }^2 \lesssim e^{\kappa' t} t^{-\alpha}
		\end{align}
		for some $\kappa ' \in \mathbb{R}$. Moreover, by Lemma \ref{lem:hk longtime}, there exists $\kappa'' \in \mathbb{R}$ so that for $t>0$, $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ and $x \in {\mathbb X}$, it holds uniformly in $x \in {\mathbb X}$, $t >0 $ and $\varepsilon \in (0,1]$ that
		\begin{align}
			\|r_t^\varepsilon(\cdot,x)\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}
			&\le e^{-t/\varepsilon}\sum_{n=1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!}
			\|G_{n\varepsilon}(\cdot,x)\|_{L^1_{\mathbb X}}\\
			&\lesssim e^{-t/\varepsilon}\sum_{n=1}^\infty \frac{(t/\varepsilon)^n}{n!}e^{\kappa n\varepsilon}
			\le \exp\left(t\frac{e^{\kappa\varepsilon}-1}{\varepsilon}\right)
			\lesssim e^{\kappa''t},
		\end{align}
		Since $1\lesssim t^{-\alpha}e^t$ for $t>0$, enlarging the exponential constant if necessary proves both bounds in \eqref{ineq:R_bound} with a single $\kappa$ independent of $\varepsilon$.
		
		(2) The objective is to show
		\begin{align}\label{lim:sup_cov}
			\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0} \sup_{x\in {\mathbb X}} \int_0^t \left\|{\Absolute*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon(y,x)G_\varepsilon(\cdot,y)m(\mathrm dy) - G(t-s,\cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K}\,\mathrm ds {=} 0.
		\end{align}
		For $t>0$, by Lemma \ref{lem:H0} and {Jensen's} inequality, it holds uniformly in $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align}
			&\int_0^t \left\|{\Absolute*{\int_{\mathbb X} r_{t-s}^\varepsilon({y,x}) G_\varepsilon({\cdot,y}) m(\mathrm dy) - G(t-s,\cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K} \mathrm ds\\
			\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:hk longtime}, \eqref{ineq:hk_k long time}}}{\lesssim}& \int_0^t  \left\|e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}\sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{((t-s)/\varepsilon)^n}{n!} {\Absolute*{G((n+1)\varepsilon,\cdot,x) - G(t-s, \cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K } \mathrm ds\\
			&\qquad + \int_0^t e^{-2(t-s)/\varepsilon} (t-s)^{-\alpha} \mathrm ds\\
			\le \,\,\,&\int_0^t e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon} \sum_{n = 1}^\infty \frac{\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left\|{\Absolute*{G(\varepsilon(n+1),\cdot,x) - G(t-s,\cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K } \mathrm ds +  \int_0^t e^{-2s/\varepsilon} s^{-\alpha} \mathrm ds\\
			=: \,\,&\int_0^t {\left(I_{1,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon)+I_{2,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon)\right)} \mathrm ds +\int_0^t e^{-2s/\varepsilon} s^{-\alpha} \mathrm ds,
		\end{align}
		where we used {Jensen's} inequality and Lemma \ref{lem:H0} in the second inequality and
		\begin{gather*}
			I_{1,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon) :=e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}{\sum_{n\varepsilon > T+1}} \frac{\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left\|{\Absolute*{G(\varepsilon(n+1),\cdot,x) - G(t-s,\cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K }{,}\\
			I_{2,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon):= e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}\sum_{\substack{\\n \varepsilon \le T+1}}\frac{\left(\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left\|{\Absolute*{G(\varepsilon(n+1),\cdot,x) - G(t-s,\cdot,x)}}\right\|^2_{\mathcal H_K }{.}
		\end{gather*}
		
		
		First, for an arbitrary but fixed $T>0$, it holds uniformly in $0 < s< t$  and $x \in {\mathbb X}$ that
		\begin{align}
			&e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}\sum_{n \varepsilon > T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left\|{\Absolute*{G\left(\varepsilon(n+1), \cdot, x \right)  - G(t-s, \cdot,x)}}\right\|_{\mathcal H_K }^2\\
			{\lesssim} &e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}\sum_{n \varepsilon > T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left(\left\|G\left(\varepsilon(n+1), \cdot, x \right)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K }^2 + \left\| G(t-s, \cdot,x)\right\|_{\mathcal H_K }^2\right)\\
			\lesssim& e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon}\sum_{n\varepsilon>T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \left((\varepsilon (n+1))^{-\alpha}e^{\kappa n\varepsilon} + (t-s)^{-\alpha}\right)\\
			\le & e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon} \sum_{n \varepsilon >T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\kappa \varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} T^{-\alpha} + e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon} \sum_{n \varepsilon >T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\kappa \varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!}(t-s)^{-\alpha}\\
			= &{e^{(e^{\kappa \varepsilon}-1)(t-s)/\varepsilon}} \left(T^{-\alpha}+(t-s)^{-\alpha} \right)e^{- \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\varepsilon \kappa }}\sum_{n\varepsilon>T+1}  \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\kappa \varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!}.
		\end{align}
		
		Let $\psi(u):= (1+u) \ln(u+1)- u$ for $u \ge 0$ and note that $\psi$ is an increasing function on $\mathbb{R}_+$. Hence, by {Bennett's inequality} for Poisson random variables, we see for sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$ and $0<s<t\le T$,
		\begin{align}
			e^{- \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\varepsilon \kappa }}\sum_{n\varepsilon>T+1}  \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}e^{\kappa \varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} &\le \exp \left( - e^{\kappa \varepsilon }\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\psi\left( \frac{T+1}{t-s}e^{-\kappa \varepsilon}-1\right)\right)\\
			&\le \exp \left( - e^{\kappa \varepsilon }\frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\psi\left( \frac{T+1}{T}e^{-\kappa \varepsilon}-1\right)\right),
		\end{align}
		which is uniformly bounded by $1$ and converges to zero as $\varepsilon \downarrow 0$. Hence, by the dominated convergence theorem,
		\begin{align}
			\lim_{\varepsilon \downarrow 0}\sup_{x \in {\mathbb X}}\int_0^t I_{1,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon) \mathrm ds = 0 \qquad \text{ for each }T\ge t >0.
		\end{align}
		{On the other hand, since  $\varepsilon(n+1)\le T+2$, Corollary~\ref{cor:hk_abs}, applied with time horizon $T+2$ implies that, there exist $\xi \in (0,1]$ and $\beta \in (0,1)$, so that the following estimate uniformly in $\varepsilon\in(0,1)$, $0\le s<t\le T+1$, and $x\in{\mathbb X}$,}
		\begin{align}
			I_{2,T}(t-s,x,\varepsilon)&\lesssim e^{-(t-s)/\varepsilon} \sum_{n \varepsilon \le T+1} \frac{\left( \frac{t-s}{\varepsilon}\right)^n}{n!} \frac{{\Absolute*{\varepsilon(n+1) - (t-s)}^\xi}}{\varepsilon^\beta(n+1)^\beta \wedge (t-s)^\beta}\nonumber\\
